## Open questions

- **Scope of generic desktop runtime:** revive the retired `desktop-llama-adapter` (libllama + shim, OOM-prone cmake per the risk register) or standardize on the explicit-path `llama-cpp-capacitor`/AOSP binding for desktop too? Decision affects build/CI surface.
- **Optimization policy for generic GGUF:** stock f16 KV + no MTP only, or allow a generic model to opt into stock-KV speculative paths where available? (eliza-1 fork kernels are tokenizer/format-specific and won't apply.)
- **Default vs allowed:** confirm product intent — eliza-1 stays the *recommended/default* but generic local models become a first-class *selectable* option (consistent with "users can download any HF GGUF"), correct?
- **Embedding/voice/vision slots:** generic GGUF text-only is in scope; should `TEXT_EMBEDDING` generic selection also be supported on desktop, or do those slots stay eliza-1/fused-only for now?
- **Platform matrix:** is generic local model selection desktop-only initially, or must mobile (capacitor) expose the same selector (it already has the explicit-path binding)?
22:T34a6,## Summary

Installing a local model today works on the happy path (curated Eliza-1 tiers and ungated HuggingFace GGUFs), with resumable streaming downloads, live SSE progress, ETA/speed, cancel, and SHA-verified Eliza-1 bundles. But the experience breaks down on the edges that real users hit: **gated/auth'd HuggingFace downloads fail with a bare `fetch failed`/`HTTP 401` because no `Authorization: Bearer` header is ever sent** (despite `HF_TOKEN`/`HUGGINGFACE_TOKEN`/`HF_HUB_TOKEN` being documented), there is **no HF-token entry UI anywhere**, **no disk-space preflight on desktop**, **no pause and no retry-on-failure affordance**, **no quantization picker** even though the catalog already models quant variants, and the ad-hoc single-file path **skips GGUF-magic validation** so an HTML error body can be registered as an installed model. This issue makes local model install a polished, reliable flow end to end.

## Current state

### Two parallel downloaders, both missing auth
- The desktop/app-core path mounts `handleLocalInferenceCompatRoutes` (`packages/app-core/src/api/server.ts:762`), which delegates to `localInferenceService.startDownload` → the `Downloader` class (`plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/downloader.ts`). Its HTTP layer (`loadHttpClient`, downloader.ts:865-898) and request headers (downloader.ts:498-503 single-file, downloader.ts:790-795 bundle) send **only `user-agent`** + optional `range`. No `Authorization`.
- The upstream-agent route handler has a **second, independent** downloader, `downloadModel` (`plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/local-inference-routes.ts:498`), whose headers (line 517-520) are likewise `user-agent` + `range` only.
- The **only** path that does it correctly is embedding warmup: `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/runtime/embedding-manager-support.ts:367-381` reads `HF_TOKEN`/`HUGGINGFACE_TOKEN`/`HF_HUB_TOKEN` and sets `downloadHeaders.Authorization = \`Bearer ${hfToken}\``. The model-install downloaders never adopted this. This asymmetry is the root of the known "fetch failed" bug.
- `searchHuggingFaceGguf` (`hf-search.ts:194`, detail fetches at lines 211/228) also sends no token, so gated/private repos are invisible in search and report wrong/zero sizes.

### No place to put a token
- Grep across `packages/ui/src`, `packages/app/src`, `packages/app-core/src`, and the plugin routes for `HF_TOKEN`/`HUGGINGFACE_TOKEN`/`hfToken`/"gated" returns **nothing in any UI**. The env vars are the only way to set a token, and most desktop users never set env vars. A gated download therefore fails with an opaque message and no actionable next step.

### No disk-space preflight on desktop
- `HardwareProbe` (`packages/shared/src/local-inference/types.ts:476-505`) exposes `totalRamGb`/`freeRamGb`/VRAM/CPU but **no free-disk field**. `MobileHardwareProbe` has `freeStorageGb` (line 454) but it is not consulted before a download starts.
- The only disk handling is **post-hoc**: `isNoSpaceMessage()` regex-matches ENOSPC strings after the write fails (`local-inference-routes.ts:753`, status `no_space` at 822-825). A user can start a 16 GB download with 2 GB free and only learn it failed near the end.

### No pause, no retry, weak failure recovery
- `DownloadState` (`types.ts:507-512`) is `queued | downloading | completed | failed | cancelled` — there is **no `paused`** state. `Downloader` supports `start`/`cancel` only (downloader.ts:326, 385); resume happens implicitly on restart via the `.part` file + `Range` request, but there is no user-facing pause/resume.
- A failed job surfaces the raw `error` string in `ModelCard` (`ModelCard.tsx:112-119`) and `DownloadQueue` (`DownloadQueue.tsx:83-85`) with **no retry button**. The only recovery is the "Download" button reappearing (per `ModelCard.stories.tsx:135`, "retry path through Download"). The "Retry" in `LocalInferencePanel.tsx:334` is for a **panel-level catalog/hardware load error**, not a failed download.

### No quant guidance even though the data model supports it
- `CatalogModel` already carries `quantization?: CatalogQuantization` with `defaultVariantId` + per-variant `sizeGb`/`minRamGb`/`status` (`types.ts:306-320, 396-397`). The UI ignores it: `ModelCard.tsx:82` and `ModelHubView.tsx:142-146` show a single `model.quant` string with no picker and no "which quant should I pick" guidance tied to the device's RAM.

### Integrity gap on the ad-hoc path
- The bundle path strictly SHA-verifies every file (downloader.ts:858-861). The **single-file** `runJob` path (used for HF-search GGUFs) computes a SHA for the registry baseline (downloader.ts:569) but has no `expectedSha256` to compare and **never checks the GGUF magic header**. The upstream route does call `isGgufFile()` after rename (`local-inference-routes.ts:574`), but the `Downloader` class does not — so a gated repo returning an HTML login/error page with HTTP 200 could be renamed `<id>.gguf` and registered as an installed model.

### What already works (do not rebuild)
- Resumable streaming with atomic `.part` → final rename (downloader.ts:558-560), throttled progress + bytes/sec + ETA (downloader.ts:538-556), Range-resume with a fallback when the server ignores Range (downloader.ts:516-520; tested at downloader.test.ts:434). Terminal-status persistence (downloader.ts:439-467). SSE progress via `/api/local-inference/downloads/stream` consumed by `LocalInferencePanel` EventSource (LocalInferencePanel.tsx:76-78) and `useHomeModelStatus`. Per-device fit badge (`computeFit`/`assessCatalogModelFit`, hub-utils.ts:71-76) and bundle device-compatibility gating (`assertBundleInstallable`, downloader.ts:112-139). Engine-backed verify-on-device for Eliza-1 bundles (`verify-on-device.ts`).

## Proposed work

Ordered, high-confidence first.

1. **Send HF auth on every download + search.** Add a single shared helper (e.g. `resolveHubAuthHeaders()` reading `HF_TOKEN`/`HUGGINGFACE_TOKEN`/`HF_HUB_TOKEN`, mirroring `embedding-manager-support.ts:373-381`) and apply it in: `Downloader.loadHttpClient`/request headers (downloader.ts:498-503, 790-795, 865-898), the upstream `downloadModel` headers (`local-inference-routes.ts:517-520`), and the HF search/detail fetches (`hf-search.ts:211, 228`). Gate it to `huggingface.co` hosts only (do not leak tokens to ModelScope/mirrors).
2. **GGUF validation on the single-file path.** Add the GGUF-magic check (reuse `isGgufFile` logic) to `Downloader.runJob` after rename, and reject/clean an HTML or non-GGUF body before registering. Surface a clear "this looks like an auth/redirect page, not a model — add a HuggingFace token?" error when the body is HTML.
3. **HF-token entry UI.** Add a token field to the local-model surface (Model Hub / settings, coordinating with #8793). Persist it the way other secrets are persisted (not a raw env edit), thread it to the downloader/search, and add a "test token" affordance. When a gated 401/403 is detected, deep-link the user to this field instead of showing a bare error.
4. **Disk-space preflight (desktop).** Add a free-disk field to `HardwareProbe` (desktop probe in `hardware.ts`) using a real `statvfs`/`fs.statfs` of the models dir, and consult it (plus mobile `freeStorageGb`) before `startDownload`. Show a "needs X GB, Y GB free" warning on the card and block/confirm when it won't fit, parallel to the existing RAM `computeFit` badge.
5. **Retry on failure.** Add a "Retry" button to failed jobs in `DownloadQueue.tsx` and `ModelCard.tsx` that re-invokes the same download (the `.part` file makes this a resume, not a restart). Map auth-class failures (401/403) to a token-specific CTA.
6. **Quantization picker.** Surface `CatalogModel.quantization` variants in `ModelCard`/`ModelHubView`: a small variant selector defaulting to `defaultVariantId`, annotated per-variant with size + fit-for-this-device, so users get "which quant should I pick" guidance grounded in their RAM/disk (ties into #8794's eval data and #8800's RAM/perf guidance).
7. **Pause/resume affordance (optional, if scoped in).** Either expose the implicit resume as an explicit Pause/Resume control (adding a `paused` `DownloadState` and a `Downloader.pause` that aborts but keeps the `.part`), or explicitly document that cancel+re-download already resumes. Decide in Open Questions.
8. **Post-install "ready to use" clarity + picker reflection.** Ensure the installed → active → ready transition is unmistakable (the bundle `verifyOnDevice` already gates default-eligibility) and that install/download state shows in the chat model picker via `useHomeModelStatus`/`home-model-status`, not only in the Model Hub.
9. **Tests for the auth/integrity gaps.** Add downloader tests for: 401/403 with no token → actionable error; 200 + token → success; HTML body rejected (not registered); disk-preflight block. The current `downloader.test.ts` has zero auth/disk coverage.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A gated/auth'd HuggingFace GGUF downloads successfully when a token is configured, and fails with a clear, token-specific message (not bare `fetch failed`) when it is missing or invalid.
- [ ] Both the `Downloader` class and the upstream `downloadModel` route attach `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (from `HF_TOKEN`/`HUGGINGFACE_TOKEN`/`HF_HUB_TOKEN`) for `huggingface.co` requests, and never for non-HF hosts.
- [ ] HF model search returns gated/private repos (with correct sizes) when a token is set.
- [ ] A user can enter, persist, and test a HuggingFace token from the UI without editing env vars; a gated failure deep-links to this field.
- [ ] `HardwareProbe` reports free disk for the models directory on desktop; the Model Hub warns/blocks before starting a download that won't fit, using real free-disk + size.
- [ ] A failed download exposes a Retry action that resumes from the partial `.part` file rather than restarting from zero.
- [ ] The single-file path validates the GGUF magic header (and rejects HTML/non-GGUF bodies) before registering an installed model.
- [ ] `CatalogModel.quantization` variants are selectable in the UI with per-variant size + per-device fit guidance.
- [ ] Install/download state (downloading / installed / ready / failed) is reflected in the chat model picker, not only the Model Hub.
- [ ] New downloader tests cover: missing-token 401, valid-token success, HTML-body rejection, and disk-preflight block.
- [ ] `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-local-inference test` and `bun run --cwd packages/ui test` pass; any cloud-frontend-touching UI passes the visual-review loop.

## Files & areas likely involved

- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/downloader.ts` — auth headers (`loadHttpClient` 865-898, request headers 498-503 / 790-795), GGUF-magic validation on single-file `runJob`, optional `pause`/`paused` state.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/local-inference-routes.ts` — second downloader `downloadModel` (498) auth headers (517-520); `no_space` handling (753, 822-825) to be promoted to a preflight.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/routes/local-inference-compat-routes.ts` — download start/cancel handlers (357-385, 640-643); add preflight/token surfacing.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/runtime/embedding-manager-support.ts:367-381` — reference implementation of HF auth to factor into a shared helper.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/hf-search.ts` — auth on search + detail fetches (211, 228); `inferParams` heuristics.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/service.ts` — `startDownload`/`cancelDownload`/`subscribeDownloads` facade (289-378); inject preflight + token.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/hardware.ts` — add desktop free-disk probe; `assessFit`.
- `packages/shared/src/local-inference/types.ts` — `HardwareProbe` (476-505) disk field; `DownloadState`/`DownloadJob` (507-565) optional `paused`; `CatalogQuantization` (306-320) already present.
- `packages/shared/src/local-inference/catalog.ts:699-733` — `buildHuggingFaceResolveUrl*` host detection for auth gating.
- `packages/ui/src/components/local-inference/ModelCard.tsx`, `ModelHubView.tsx`, `DownloadQueue.tsx`, `DownloadProgress.tsx`, `CustomModelSearch.tsx`, `LocalInferencePanel.tsx`, `hub-utils.ts`, `useHomeModelStatus.ts` — retry buttons, quant picker, disk/fit warnings, token field, picker reflection.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/downloader.test.ts` — add auth/integrity/disk tests.

## Open questions

- **Token storage:** should the HF token live in the existing secrets/vault mechanism, plain config, or both — and does it sync via Eliza Cloud, or stay strictly local-only on the device that downloads?
- **Pause/resume:** add an explicit `paused` `DownloadState` + Pause control, or keep the implicit cancel-then-resume behavior and just document it? (Adding a state touches the SSE contract and every consumer.)
- **Disk preflight strictness:** hard-block a won't-fit download, or warn-and-confirm? What headroom margin above the file size (temp `.part` + final coexist briefly during rename on the same volume)?
- **Quant default policy:** should the per-device "recommended quant" be derived purely from `minRamGb`, or wait on #8794's eval data so the recommendation reflects measured quality/perf, not just memory fit?
- **Scope overlap:** how much of the token-entry UI belongs here vs. the settings rework in #8793, and how much fit-to-RAM/quant guidance should defer to #8800's mobile resource workbench?
23:T3871,## Summary

elizaOS already has strong, genuinely non-larp e2e machinery — the scenario-runner boots a real `AgentRuntime` over PGLite and exercises real plugin route handlers via its `api` turn kind, a deterministic LLM proxy with strict fixtures gives secret-free runs, a Mockoon-derived external-API mock fleet stands in for real provider calls, and a `external-api-live-drift.yml` nightly re-verifies those mocks against the real APIs. There are even static "ships-without-a-test → CI fails" gates for UI routes/pages and views (`route-coverage.test.ts`, `view-interaction-coverage.test.ts`, `plugin-tui-view-coverage.test.ts`). **What is missing is an umbrella coverage matrix and the equivalent ship-gate for the three surfaces that fall through the cracks: slash commands, pre-LLM shortcuts (#8791, new), and plugin-registered HTTP routes.** This issue is the audit/umbrella that inventories every command, shortcut, view, plugin and route, asserts each has a real recorded-mock e2e, and adds a CI gate that fails when a new one ships uncovered.

## Current state

### What is real and good (credit where due)

- **Scenario-runner is a real e2e harness, not larp.** `packages/scenario-runner/src/executor.ts` boots a real runtime and, for `kind: "api"` turns, stands up a loopback `http.createServer` that dispatches against `runtime.routes` (the actual plugin-registered handlers) and `fetch`es them (`startScenarioApiServer`, ~line 556; api-turn handler ~line 1261). `packages/scenario-runner/test/scenarios/deterministic-generated-app-routes.scenario.ts` is the gold standard: it registers a real `AppRegistryService`, calls real `handleAppsRoutes`/`handleAppPackageRoutes`/`handleViewsRoutes`, and asserts exact registry/catalog/route-ledger state — zero trivial assertions.
- **Secret-free determinism exists.** `packages/test/mocks/helpers/llm-proxy-plugin.ts` (`createDeterministicLlmProxyPlugin`, strict mode via `SCENARIO_LLM_PROXY_STRICT=1`) fails closed when no fixture matches, validates tool-call/schema output, and reports diagnostics. Wired through `runtime-factory.ts` (`shouldUseStrictDeterministicLlmProxy`).
- **Recorded provider mocks derived from real APIs, kept honest.** `packages/test/mocks/environments/*.json` (Mockoon) + the in-process stateful runner (`packages/test/mocks/scripts/start-mocks.ts`) cover ~24 providers; `packages/test/mocks/README.md` documents covered surfaces and remaining gaps; `provider-coverage-contract.test.ts` fails when the table drifts; `.github/workflows/external-api-live-drift.yml` re-runs `*.real.test.ts` against live APIs nightly to detect mock drift. This is exactly the "mock APIs derived from real calls, cached, validated in CI" the product owner asked for — for **external** APIs.
- **PR-lane deterministic e2e runs.** `.github/workflows/ci.yaml` runs `bun run --cwd packages/scenario-runner test:pr:e2e` (17 deterministic scenarios incl. `deterministic-generated-app-routes`, `deterministic-view-switching`, `deterministic-mcp-actions-routes`, `deterministic-workflow-actions-routes`, `deterministic-github-actions-routes`) plus a scenario-catalog completeness check (`packages/scripts/check-scenario-workflow-coverage.mjs`) that fails if any scenario in `packages/test/scenarios` is orphaned from the workflow globs. 701 scenario files exist.
- **Static ship-gates already exist for UI routes + views.** `packages/app/test/route-coverage.test.ts` diffs the canonical route catalog (`buildRouteCatalog`, mirroring `@elizaos/ui` `TAB_PATHS`) against the all-pages click-safe smoke matrix and fails when a new view/page/tile ships without smoke coverage. `packages/app/test/view-interaction-coverage.test.ts` and `packages/agent/src/__tests__/plugin-tui-view-coverage.test.ts` do the same for view interactions / TUI parity. Rich Playwright `ui-smoke` suite (~60 specs incl. `plugin-views-visual.spec.ts`, `orchestrator-gui-workbench.spec.ts`, `view-manager-actual-flow.spec.ts`).

### The gaps (the actual work)

1. **Slash commands: catalog is served, execution is never e2e-tested.** `plugins/plugin-commands` is a *registry only* — it ships `DEFAULT_COMMANDS` (~26 text commands) + a `COMMAND_REGISTRY` provider but **no action handlers** (confirmed in its CLAUDE.md and `src/index.ts`). The connector catalog (`src/connector-catalog.ts`) adds ~15 navigation/client commands. The wire surface is `GET /api/commands` (`packages/agent/src/api/commands-routes.ts`), consumed by `packages/ui/src/chat/useSlashCommandController.ts`. Tests today:
   - `packages/agent/src/api/commands-routes.test.ts` — calls the handler with **mocked** `json`/`error` fns (no real server, no execution).
   - `plugins/plugin-commands/__tests__/parser.test.ts` + `connector-catalog.test.ts` — unit-level; assert a handful of keys (`bash`, `think`), not per-command.
   - **No scenario, no Playwright spec types `/help` / `/settings` / `/model` into chat and asserts the command executes / navigates.** `grep` for any scenario referencing `api/commands` or slash execution returns empty. So most of the ~40 catalog commands have **zero** behavioral coverage of the click-to-effect path. (Pairs with #8790.)
2. **Shortcuts (pre-LLM action shortcuts): proposed, not built.** #8791 introduces a pre-LLM shortcut registry. It does not exist yet, so it has no coverage and no gate. This umbrella must include shortcuts in the matrix + gate the moment #8791 lands.
3. **Plugin HTTP routes: large route-level e2e gap.** ~38 plugins declare `Plugin.routes` (served in prod via `packages/agent/src/api/runtime-plugin-routes.ts` `tryHandleRuntimePluginRoute`; tested generically in `runtime-plugin-routes.test.ts`). Of the plugins that declare routes in their main index, these have **no dedicated route test and no `api`-turn scenario**: `plugin-anthropic-proxy`, `plugin-cli`, `plugin-computeruse`, `plugin-discord-local`, `plugin-facewear`, `plugin-farcaster`, `plugin-github`, `plugin-imessage`, `plugin-music`, `plugin-mysticism`, `plugin-telegram`, `plugin-video`, `plugin-workflow`, `plugin-xr`. (Covered: `plugin-bluebubbles`, `plugin-discord`, `plugin-signal`, `plugin-social-alpha`, `plugin-whatsapp` have `*-routes.test.ts`.) 10 plugins have **no test file at all** (`plugin-action-bench`, `plugin-discord-local`, `plugin-elizamaker`, `plugin-google-meet-cute`, `plugin-local-embedding`, `plugin-mlx`, `plugin-native-shared-types`, `plugin-omnivoice`, `plugin-tee`, `plugin-xmtp`).
4. **No coverage matrix and no ship-gate for commands/shortcuts/routes.** The existing gates cover UI routes/pages/views. There is **no** `check-command-coverage` / `check-shortcut-coverage` / `check-plugin-route-coverage` gate. The advisory `coverage-gate.yml` only measures line coverage on changed files (`COVERAGE_GATE_ENFORCE=0`) — it does not enforce that a new command/route/view has a behavioral e2e.

## Proposed work

Ordered; depends on the sibling "e2e mock infra" issue for any new recorded fixtures.

1. **Build the canonical coverage inventory (source of truth).** A boot-free script (mirror `packages/scripts/check-scenario-workflow-coverage.mjs` style) that enumerates, from real source:
   - slash commands — `DEFAULT_COMMANDS` (`plugins/plugin-commands/src/registry.ts`) ∪ `navigationCommands()`/client commands (`src/connector-catalog.ts`), i.e. the exact set `GET /api/commands` serves;
   - shortcuts — the #8791 registry (once it exists);
   - views — `Plugin.views` declarations resolved by `packages/agent/src/api/views-registry.ts` + `BUILTIN_VIEWS` (reuse the existing view catalog, do not duplicate #8796/#8797/#8798);
   - plugins + routes — every `Plugin.routes` entry across `plugins/*` (path + method).
   Emit `reports/coverage/e2e-matrix.json` + an HTML contact sheet (same shape as the scenario catalog inventory viewer).
2. **Close the command e2e gap (consumes #8790).** Add a `deterministic-slash-commands.scenario.ts` that hits `GET /api/commands` via an `api` turn and, for each `target.kind`, asserts the real effect: `agent` commands route through the message pipeline, `navigate` commands resolve to a real route, `client` commands fire the right effect. Add a Playwright `ui-smoke` spec that types representative slash commands into the composer and asserts navigation/clear/palette via `useSlashCommandController`. Every catalog command must be referenced by at least one of these.
3. **Close the plugin-route e2e gap.** For each route-declaring plugin currently uncovered (§3 list), add an `api`-turn scenario or a `*-routes.test.ts` that boots the handler through `tryHandleRuntimePluginRoute` (real dispatch, real auth gate, real body parsing) with recorded provider mocks where the route calls out. Prioritize plugins with public routes and user-facing surfaces.
4. **Wire shortcuts** into the matrix + a deterministic scenario as soon as #8791 lands.
5. **Add the CI ship-gate.** A `check-e2e-coverage` script + `e2e-coverage-gate.yml` (or a step in `ci.yaml`) that diffs the inventory (§1) against the discovered test/scenario matrix and **fails** when a command, shortcut, view, plugin, or route exists with no real e2e — following the exact precedent of `route-coverage.test.ts` ("a new view/page/tile that ships without smoke coverage fails CI here"). Start advisory for one cycle (like `coverage-gate.yml`), then flip to required once the baseline is green.
6. **Larp-detection pass.** Add a lint rule / review-checklist item so a "test" that asserts only shape (`length > 0`, `kind === 'navigate'`) without exercising the real handler does not count as coverage in the gate — the gate should require a real `api`/`action`/Playwright turn, not a mocked-`json`-fn unit test.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A committed coverage inventory (`reports/coverage/e2e-matrix.json` + viewer) enumerating every slash command, shortcut, view, plugin, and route from real source.
- [ ] Every command in the `GET /api/commands` catalog has a real e2e (scenario `api`/`action` turn or Playwright spec) that exercises its actual effect — not a mocked handler unit test.
- [ ] Every shortcut in the #8791 registry has a deterministic e2e (gated on #8791 landing; tracked as a checklist item until then).
- [ ] Every route-declaring plugin in the §3 uncovered list has a route-level e2e booting the real handler via `tryHandleRuntimePluginRoute`/`api`-turn, with recorded provider mocks for any external calls.
- [ ] All 10 zero-test plugins either gain a real test or are documented as intentionally test-exempt with written justification.
- [ ] Views remain covered by the existing gates (no regression); this issue references #8796/#8797/#8798 rather than re-implementing view e2e.
- [ ] A `check-e2e-coverage` CI gate fails when a new command/shortcut/view/plugin/route ships without a real e2e; advisory for one cycle, then required.
- [ ] New behavioral tests run deterministically in the PR lane (no secrets) via `SCENARIO_USE_LLM_PROXY=1` + the Mockoon mock fleet; any new external fixtures are covered by `external-api-live-drift.yml`.
- [ ] `bun run --cwd packages/scenario-runner test:pr:e2e` and the new gate are green on `develop`.

## Files & areas likely involved

- `plugins/plugin-commands/src/registry.ts`, `src/connector-catalog.ts`, `src/parser.ts` — command source of truth.
- `packages/agent/src/api/commands-routes.ts` + `commands-routes.test.ts` — `GET /api/commands` (currently mocked-only).
- `packages/ui/src/chat/useSlashCommandController.ts`, `slash-menu.ts`, `components/shell/SlashCommandMenu.tsx` — command execution consumers.

## Open questions

- **Gate scope per surface:** should the gate require coverage of *every* route on a plugin, or one representative happy-path + auth-gate per plugin? (Per-route is exhaustive but high-churn; per-plugin-surface is the cheaper floor.)
- **What counts as "covered" for a command?** One scenario per `target.kind` (agent/navigate/client) vs one per command. Recommend per-command for `agent`-target commands (behavioral) and per-kind for navigate/client (effect is structural).
- **Larp threshold:** do we hard-fail the gate on mocked-`json`-fn unit tests (like the current `commands-routes.test.ts`), or just not count them? Recommend "don't count, don't fail" so existing unit tests stay.
- **Connector command parity:** should Discord/Telegram native-command registration (`plugins/plugin-discord/catalog-commands.ts`, `plugins/plugin-telegram/src/command-registration.ts`) be in-scope for this gate, or owned by #8790? (Currently they have catalog unit tests but no live-render e2e.)
- **Provider-key-gated routes:** for plugins whose routes only work with real credentials and have no mock yet (e.g. some `plugin-farcaster`/`plugin-video` paths), do we require a new Mockoon fixture (blocks on the mock-infra issue) or accept a `external-api-live-drift`-style nightly-only real test?
- **#8791 sequencing:** shortcuts can't be gated until the registry exists — confirm this umbrella stays open across #8791, or splits the shortcut requirement into a follow-up.
24:T3dca,## Summary

View switching in elizaOS routes a user utterance to one of ~30 registered views through a deterministic-first cascade (rigid multilingual `matchViewCommand` → passive `INTENT_VIEW_RULES` → small-model `viewContextEvaluator`), and the resolved view is broadcast to the renderer. Test coverage today is real but **uneven**: it is exhaustive for the deterministic resolver and the renderer navigate pipeline, but the view × language × trigger-modality matrix is sampled rather than complete, the multilingual/GEPA work is ad-hoc one-off scripts rather than CI, and the contextual `view_context` prompt is never auto-optimized. This issue proposes a complete e2e/scenario matrix (every view × multiple languages × every trigger modality) plus a closed GEPA loop that optimizes the view-routing prompts and gates on measured accuracy.

## Current state

**The routing cascade (well-built, this is what we test against):**
- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/view-command-matcher.ts` — `matchViewCommand()`: rigid, zero-model multilingual matcher. Covers 20 views with synonym tables (`VIEW_NOUNS`) across **en, es, pt, fr, de, zh, ja, ko, vi, tl**, gated by nav-verb/possessive/view-word signals. `MATCHER_VIEW_IDS` and `__matcherData` are exported "for exhaustive test generation" — but no test actually fans out over the full noun×verb×possessive cross-product.
- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/views-show.ts` — `resolveIntentView()` wraps `matchViewCommand` then falls back to English `INTENT_VIEW_RULES` (passive intents like "am i free" → calendar) plus a block of multilingual passive regexes. `runViewsShow()` also implements the "user's words beat a hallucinated model `view` param, but only if the intent view is registered" correction.
- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/evaluators/view-command-shortcut.ts` — `viewCommandShortcutEvaluator` (priority 30): the EARLY hook that **forces** the VIEWS action when `resolveIntentView` matches, so a weak local model can't drop the navigation.
- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/evaluators/view-context.ts` — `viewContextEvaluator` (small model, priority 60): the CONTEXTUAL path for situations that imply a surface but name none ("fix the login bug" → task-coordinator). Its instruction half (`BASELINE_VIEW_CONTEXT_INSTRUCTION`) is GEPA-optimizable and loaded at runtime via `resolveOptimizedPromptForRuntime(runtime, "view_context", …)`. Its `CONTEXT_VIEWS` enum is a **separate, smaller** list (12 ids, no settings/help/etc.).
- `packages/agent/src/runtime/view-action-affinity.ts` — `VIEW_ACTION_MAP` + `renderActiveViewContextBlock`: a *third* per-view list (which actions get full param detail while a view is foreground). Drift-checked at boot by `validateViewActionMap`, but not cross-checked against the matcher/registry.
- `packages/agent/src/api/builtin-views.ts` — `BUILTIN_VIEWS`: the 13 first-party shell views (tutorial, help, camera, chat, character, automations, plugins-page, trajectories, memories, database, logs, settings; camera is android-only, several are `developerOnly`).

**Existing tests (what's actually covered):**
- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/views-switching.test.ts` — strong unit coverage of the VIEWS resolver: every built-in view via active command, passive planner-routed ids, hallucinated-param correction, ambiguity, connector gating, and a `resolveIntentView` multilingual block (en/es/fr/de/zh). **No ja/ko/vi/tl/pt** despite the matcher supporting them, and it asserts the pure resolver, not the rendered view.
- `packages/scenario-runner/test/scenarios/deterministic-view-switching.scenario.ts` — drives the real VIEWS `action=show` handler for the 9 navigable built-ins, asserts the loopback navigate POST. Zero-cost, **English label only**, never renders UI.
- `packages/scenario-runner/test/scenarios/deterministic-app-control-nl-routing.scenario.ts` — strict-fixture NL routing for show/search/edit/delete, exact HTTP ledger. English only.
- `plugins/plugin-app-control/test/scenarios/views-voice-navigate.scenario.ts` — one single-word voice utterance ("settings"). The only "voice" scenario; English, one view.
- `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/view-switching-chat-e2e.spec.ts` — the real composer → real renderer navigate pipeline, deterministic tier always-on + live tier behind `ELIZA_UI_SMOKE_LIVE_STACK`. Includes a handful of multilingual cases (es/fr/zh) but only for **calendar/wallet/inbox/todos** — a sampled subset.
- `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/view-switching-local-llm-e2e.spec.ts` — drives a real local eliza-1 via llama-server, screenshots chat + resulting view, emits an HTML report. 10 cases across direct/multilingual/contextual/negative. Requires a running llama-server (not CI-gated); report-only (the test passes as long as the matrix ran).
- `deterministic-xr-view-actions.scenario.ts` — XR open/switch/list view actions (a separate surface/modality), English.

**GEPA / optimization (ad-hoc, not in the loop):**
- `plugins/plugin-training/scripts/gepa-view-context.ts` — runs GEPA + bootstrap-fewshot on the `view_context` instruction against a local model, scored by `scoreViewSelection`, writes the winner to `/tmp` (manual promotion only). Records measured tier scores (0.8b ~0.04, 2b ~0.57, 4b ~0.65).
- `plugins/plugin-training/scripts/verify-view-switching.ts` — a 24-case verification matrix (direct/passive/contextual/multilingual/negative) that mirrors `runViewsShow`'s end-to-end landing and emits a JSON+HTML report. **Not wired into any test command.**
- `plugins/plugin-training/scripts/multishot-view-switching.ts` — the empirical study showing prompt-only optimization is flat for the 0.8b and the deterministic resolver is the production answer.
- `plugins/plugin-training/src/optimizers/scoring.ts` — `scorePlannerAction` (view-aware partial credit) and `scoreViewSelection` exist and are the right scoring primitives.
- Fixtures: `view-switching.action_planner.jsonl` (24 rows) and `view-context.jsonl` (23 rows), both multilingual.

**The gaps:**
1. **No complete matrix.** No test asserts *every* registered/navigable view is reachable from *every* supported language across *every* trigger modality (text / voice-transcript / slash / keyboard-shortcut). Each existing test samples one or two axes.
2. **Multilingual coverage is a subset.** The matcher claims 10 languages; tests assert ~5, for ~4 views. ja/ko/vi/tl/pt are essentially untested end-to-end.
3. **`view_context` is never auto-optimized.** It has a runtime artifact-load path but is **absent from `ALL_TRAINING_TASKS`** (`plugins/plugin-training/src/core/training-config.ts` lists only should_respond/context_routing/action_planner/response/media_description), so the nightly GEPA/training trigger never produces a `view_context` artifact. Optimization is a manual `/tmp` script.
4. **No GEPA regression gate.** The verify/gepa scripts emit reports but nothing fails CI when routing accuracy regresses or an artifact underperforms its baseline.
5. **Registry/matcher/intent/affinity drift.** Four independent per-view lists (`BUILTIN_VIEWS`, `VIEW_NOUNS`, `INTENT_VIEW_RULES` + multilingual regexes, `CONTEXT_VIEWS`, `VIEW_ACTION_MAP`) with no test asserting they agree on the set of view ids.
6. **Voice modality is one case.** Only a single single-word voice scenario exists; no per-view voice-transcript coverage.

## Proposed work

Ordered, deterministic-first so the bulk runs in CI with no provider key.

1. **Single source of truth for the view matrix.** Add a shared, exported test fixture (e.g. `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/view-matrix.fixtures.ts`) enumerating each navigable view id with: canonical English label, expected nav path, `developerOnly`/platform flags, and a per-language phrase set (en/es/pt/fr/de/zh/ja/ko/vi/tl) for direct, possessive, and view-word forms. Derive it from `__matcherData` + `BUILTIN_VIEWS` where possible so it cannot silently drift.
2. **Drift guard test.** Assert that the view-id sets across `BUILTIN_VIEWS`, `MATCHER_VIEW_IDS`/`VIEW_NOUNS`, `resolveIntentView`'s rule targets, `CONTEXT_VIEWS`, and `VIEW_ACTION_MAP` are consistent (every contextual/affinity view id resolves to a real registered view; every navigable built-in has at least one matcher noun). Fail on additions that update one list but not the others.
3. **Exhaustive deterministic resolver matrix (unit).** Extend `views-switching.test.ts` (or a new `view-matrix.test.ts`) to fan out `matchViewCommand` + `resolveIntentView` over the full fixture: every view × every language × {direct verb, possessive, view-word, bare-noun}. Include negative-control phrases per language that must return `null` (no false routing). This is pure and fast.
4. **Exhaustive scenario matrix (deterministic, zero-cost).** Generalize `deterministic-view-switching.scenario.ts` to iterate the fixture: for each (view, language) drive the real VIEWS `action=show` handler and assert the loopback navigate POST + result DTO. Add a parallel strict-fixture scenario proving the EARLY hook (`viewCommandShortcutEvaluator`) forces VIEWS for a localized command even when the planner fixture chose REPLY.
5. **Trigger-modality coverage.** Add deterministic cases for each non-text modality: (a) voice — feed single-word and short transcribed phrases per view (generalize `views-voice-navigate.scenario.ts`); (b) slash — if a `/view`-style command exists in `plugins/plugin-commands`, assert it routes to VIEWS (otherwise note N/A with justification); (c) keyboard-shortcut / deep-link — assert the renderer `eliza:navigate:view` path lands the view (extend `view-switching-chat-e2e.spec.ts`).
6. **End-to-end renderer matrix.** Extend `view-switching-chat-e2e.spec.ts` to cover the full set of route-backed views with on-view render markers across the same language set in the deterministic tier (real composer → real `createNavigateViewHandler` → `ViewRouter`), keeping the live tier opt-in.
7. **Promote the GEPA scripts to a real loop.** (a) Add `view_context` (and a dedicated `view_routing`/action_planner view-slice if warranted) to `ALL_TRAINING_TASKS` so the nightly trigger + `OptimizedPromptService` produce and load an artifact. (b) Wire `verify-view-switching.ts`'s landed-accuracy matrix into a runnable command and a post-merge/live lane test that asserts `landedAccuracy` ≥ a floor for the deterministic cascade (the cascade should be ~100% on direct + multilingual regardless of model). (c) Add a GEPA regression gate: optimized `view_context` artifact must score ≥ baseline on the held-out `view-context.jsonl` rows (reuse `scoreViewSelection`), else the promotion is rejected.
8. **Per-language report artifact.** Have the local-LLM e2e + verify harness emit a per-(view, language, modality) pass/fail grid so regressions are visible by cell, not just an aggregate number.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A single exported fixture enumerates every navigable view × supported language × phrasing form, derived from the matcher/registry data so it can't silently drift.
- [ ] A drift-guard test fails when `BUILTIN_VIEWS`, `VIEW_NOUNS`/`MATCHER_VIEW_IDS`, `resolveIntentView` targets, `CONTEXT_VIEWS`, and `VIEW_ACTION_MAP` disagree on view ids.
- [ ] Deterministic unit + scenario tests cover **every** navigable view in **all** matcher-supported languages (en/es/pt/fr/de/zh/ja/ko/vi/tl), including per-language negative controls that must not navigate.
- [ ] Each trigger modality (text, voice-transcript, slash if present, keyboard/deep-link) has at least per-view deterministic coverage; any N/A is justified in writing.
- [ ] The renderer e2e (`view-switching-chat-e2e.spec.ts`) asserts the rendered view (URL + on-view marker) for the full route-backed set across the language matrix in the always-on deterministic tier.
- [ ] `view_context` is registered in `ALL_TRAINING_TASKS` and produces a loadable artifact under `optimized-prompts/view_context/` through the normal trigger path.
- [ ] A CI/post-merge lane asserts deterministic-cascade landed-accuracy ≥ floor (≈100% on direct+multilingual) via the `verify-view-switching` matrix, independent of model strength.
- [ ] A GEPA regression gate rejects a `view_context` artifact that scores below baseline on held-out rows (`scoreViewSelection`).
- [ ] A per-(view, language, modality) pass/fail grid is emitted as a build artifact.
- [ ] `bun run test` and `bun run verify` pass; the always-on lanes require no provider key (live/local-model lanes stay opt-in behind `ELIZA_UI_SMOKE_LIVE_STACK` / `LLAMACPP_URL`).

## Files & areas likely involved

- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/views-show.ts` (`resolveIntentView`, `INTENT_VIEW_RULES`, `runViewsShow`)

- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/evaluators/view-command-shortcut.ts` (EARLY hook)

## Open questions

- **Language scope:** the matcher supports 10 languages but the shipped UI supports fewer. Should the exhaustive matrix cover all 10 matcher languages, or only the supported UI locales (with the rest as best-effort, non-gating)?
- **Which views count as "all"?** `developerOnly` views (trajectories/memories/database/logs) and platform-gated ones (camera = android-only) — include them in the matrix as gated cases, or restrict the matrix to user-facing surfaces? `CONTEXT_VIEWS` is a deliberately smaller set than the registry.
- **GEPA task identity:** optimize the existing `action_planner` task with a view-routing slice of the dataset, add a dedicated `view_routing` task, or only promote the contextual `view_context` task? (The empirical scripts suggest the deterministic cascade — not the model — is the production answer for direct/multilingual, so the GEPA win is concentrated on the *contextual* path.)
- **CI cost / model availability:** the live + local-LLM lanes need a provider key or a running llama-server. Should the model-graded GEPA accuracy gate run post-merge only, on a fixed cheap eval model (Cerebras gpt-oss-120b is already the judge), or nightly?
- **Accuracy floors:** what landed-accuracy floor blocks merge for (a) the deterministic cascade and (b) each local-model tier (0.8b/2b/4b), given the measured tier scores already documented in the scripts?

25:T3d8b,## Summary

Today the chat UI shows a single binary "the agent is busy" signal — three breathing dots — for the entire turn, whether the agent is thinking, calling a tool, streaming text, running a connector action, or waiting on a cloud sandbox to wake. We want a richer, Claude-like status indicator that names the current phase ("Thinking…", "Searching the web…", "Running EXECUTE_COMMAND…", "Waking up…", "Writing…") and is driven by real runtime phase events rather than a derived `responding` boolean. The runtime already produces most of this telemetry on its event bus, and there is a typed-but-unused `AgentEventService` stream vocabulary for it; the gaps are (a) wiring that telemetry into the per-turn chat channel and (b) consuming it in the chat surface with clean, non-flickery, accessible UX.

## Current state

**The UI status signal is binary and phase-blind.**
- `useShellController` derives a single predicate: `const responding = chatSending || voiceOutput.speaking;` and a coarse `ShellPhase` (`booting | idle | summoned | listening | responding`) in [`packages/ui/src/components/shell/useShellController.ts`](packages/ui/src/components/shell/useShellController.ts) (≈L517–526). `responding` is "a reply is in flight (text streaming OR being spoken)" — it carries no information about *what* the agent is doing.
- The overlay renders only breathing dots for any in-flight turn: `TypingDotsInner` / `TypingDots` in [`packages/ui/src/components/shell/ContinuousChatOverlay.tsx`](packages/ui/src/components/shell/ContinuousChatOverlay.tsx) (≈L476–515), shown inside the empty assistant bubble while `phase === "responding"`. `ShellMessage` has no phase/status field ([`shell-state.ts`](packages/ui/src/components/shell/shell-state.ts)).

**The chat transport (SSE) only carries `token` / `done` / `error`.**
- The client stream parser handles exactly three event types — `parseStreamChatDataLine` → `applyStreamChatDataLine` in [`packages/ui/src/api/client-base.ts`](packages/ui/src/api/client-base.ts) (L190–207): `token`, `done`, `error`. There is no `thinking`/`action`/`tool`/`status` event.
- `sendConversationMessageStream` ([`client-chat.ts`](packages/ui/src/api/client-chat.ts) L408–427) and the send path `useChatSend.ts` ([`runQueuedChatSend`](packages/ui/src/state/useChatSend.ts) L815–836) only thread `(token, accumulatedText)` plus a final `{ text, reasoning, usage, failureKind }`. The only intra-turn signal exposed is `setChatFirstTokenReceived(true)` — a single boolean.

**The server already distinguishes phases internally but flattens them onto the wire.**
- [`packages/agent/src/api/chat-routes.ts`](packages/agent/src/api/chat-routes.ts) tracks `activeStreamSource` (`unset` vs LLM-token vs action-callback), `actionCallbackHistory`, and emits literal progress strings like `"Executing action: ${action.name}"` (see `execute-planned-tool-call.ts` below). But `writeSse` only ever emits `{ type: "token" }` / `{ type: "done" }` / `{ type: "error" }` (`writeChatTokenSse`, L1414–1420). Action-callback status text is merged into `responseText` and shipped as ordinary tokens — so "🔍 searching…" briefly appears *as the message body*, not as a status indicator.

**The runtime emits real phase events — but they don't reach the chat channel.**
- The core event bus has the right vocabulary: `EventType.RUN_STARTED/RUN_ENDED`, `ACTION_STARTED/ACTION_COMPLETED`, `EVALUATOR_STARTED/COMPLETED`, `MODEL_USED` ([`packages/core/src/types/events.ts`](packages/core/src/types/events.ts) L55–69). `execute-planned-tool-call.ts` ([`packages/core/src/runtime/execute-planned-tool-call.ts`](packages/core/src/runtime/execute-planned-tool-call.ts) L205–259) emits `ACTION_STARTED`/`ACTION_COMPLETED` with the action name and a `"Executing action: ${action.name}"` content.
- `AgentEventService` ([`packages/core/src/services/agentEvent.ts`](packages/core/src/services/agentEvent.ts)) defines a fully-typed stream taxonomy — `lifecycle | tool | action | evaluator | provider | memory | assistant | message | error | heartbeat` ([`packages/core/src/types/agentEvent.ts`](packages/core/src/types/agentEvent.ts) L17–28) — with rich per-event data (`ActionEventData.actionName`, `ToolEventData.toolName`, `LifecycleEventData.type: "action_start" | "step_start" | …`). The agent server **already broadcasts every `AgentEventService` event to WS clients** as `type: "agent_event"` ([`packages/agent/src/api/server.ts`](packages/agent/src/api/server.ts) L3906–3924).
- **The blocker:** `emitActionStart` / `emitActionComplete` / `emitTool` / `emitProviderStart` / `emitEvaluatorStart` / `emitLifecycle` are **defined but never called** anywhere in `packages/core`, `packages/agent`, or `plugins/` (verified by grep — the only hits are the method definitions in `agentEvent.ts` and an unrelated lifeops `emitLifecycleState`). Chat turns notify via `runtime.emitEvent(MESSAGE_SENT)` on the EventType bus ([`message.ts` `emitMessageSent`](packages/core/src/services/message.ts) L11398), not via the AgentEventService streams. So the `action`/`tool`/`evaluator`/`provider`/`lifecycle` streams are effectively dead — the data structure exists, nothing fills it.

**A client consumer for these events already exists — but only for adjacent surfaces.**
- [`packages/ui/src/hooks/useActivityEvents.ts`](packages/ui/src/hooks/useActivityEvents.ts) already subscribes to WS `agent_event` (and `pty-session-event`, `proactive-message`) and even summarizes `tool_running`/`blocked`/`task_complete` — but it is scoped to the coding-agent PTY rail and proactive/lifeops messages, NOT the foreground chat turn, and the foreground chat turn doesn't populate those streams anyway.

**A strong UX precedent exists for voice only.**
- [`packages/ui/src/components/composites/chat/ChatVoiceStatusBar.tsx`](packages/ui/src/components/composites/chat/ChatVoiceStatusBar.tsx) already implements a 5-state status strip — `idle / listening / thinking / speaking / interrupting` — with a colored status dot, label, `role="status" aria-live="polite"`, and a latency badge. This is exactly the shape we want for text chat, but it is wired only to `VoiceContinuousStatus`. (Note: its `speaking` dot uses `bg-accent` (orange); reuse should respect the "orange = accent only" brand rule.)

**"Waking" states are mostly invisible.**
- The cloud dedicated-agent `202 Accepted` + `Retry-After` auto-resume is handled silently inside the client ([`client-base.ts`](packages/ui/src/api/client-base.ts) `RESUME_*` retry loop, L338+) with no UI surface — the user just waits on dots.
- The only "waking" affordance is the local-agent boot placeholder ("…waking up…") tied to `phase === "booting"` in [`ContinuousChatOverlay.tsx`](packages/ui/src/components/shell/ContinuousChatOverlay.tsx) (≈L2681, L2694) — it does not cover cloud sandbox wake / shared→cloud handoff.

## Proposed work

Ordered so each step is independently shippable.

1. **Define a canonical chat turn-phase status type.** Add a small discriminated status union (e.g. `ChatTurnStatus = { kind: "thinking" | "streaming" | "running_action" | "running_tool" | "evaluating" | "waking" | "speaking"; label?: string; actionName?: string; toolName?: string }`) co-located with the chat transport types ([`packages/ui/src/api/client-types-chat.ts`](packages/ui/src/api/client-types-chat.ts)) and mirror it server-side. Keep it schema-derived/shared rather than duplicated.

2. **Emit phase telemetry on the per-turn chat SSE.** Extend the chat stream in [`chat-routes.ts`](packages/agent/src/api/chat-routes.ts) to emit a new `{ type: "status", … }` SSE event (additive — `token`/`done`/`error` unchanged) at phase boundaries the route already knows: before first token (`thinking`), on `activeStreamSource` claim transitions (`streaming` vs `running_action`), and from the `"Executing action: X"` callback (carry `actionName` instead of injecting it into `responseText`). Coordinate with the chat-routes owner before changing the contract.

3. **Wire the dead `AgentEventService` action/tool/provider/lifecycle streams (or bridge the EventType bus).** Either (a) call `emitActionStart`/`emitActionComplete`/`emitTool`/`emitProviderStart` at the existing `EventType.ACTION_STARTED/…` emit sites in [`execute-planned-tool-call.ts`](packages/core/src/runtime/execute-planned-tool-call.ts) and the planner/provider loop, or (b) add a single bridge that maps the EventType bus → AgentEventService streams. This gives the WS `agent_event` channel real per-turn phase data and is reusable beyond chat. Decide (a) vs (b) — see open questions.

4. **Parse the new events in the client transport.** Extend `applyStreamChatDataLine` / `streamChatEndpoint` in [`client-base.ts`](packages/ui/src/api/client-base.ts) to surface a status callback (`onStatus(status)`), threaded through `sendConversationMessageStream` ([`client-chat.ts`](packages/ui/src/api/client-chat.ts)) and `runQueuedChatSend` ([`useChatSend.ts`](packages/ui/src/state/useChatSend.ts)) into chat state — replacing the lone `chatFirstTokenReceived` boolean with the richer status.

5. **Expose status from the controller.** Add `turnStatus: ChatTurnStatus | null` to `ShellController` ([`useShellController.ts`](packages/ui/src/components/shell/useShellController.ts)) — derived from the live stream status, falling back to `thinking` when `chatSending && !firstTokenReceived`, `streaming` after first token, `speaking` from `voiceOutput.speaking`, and `waking` for the cloud-202 resume path (step 7). Keep `responding` for back-compat gating.

6. **Render a Claude-like indicator in the overlay.** Replace the bare `TypingDotsInner` with a status-aware component that renders the phase label + a quiet animated affordance, anchored in the in-flight assistant bubble ([`ContinuousChatOverlay.tsx`](packages/ui/src/components/shell/ContinuousChatOverlay.tsx)). Mirror `ChatVoiceStatusBar`'s accessibility (`role="status"`, `aria-live="polite"`) and brand rules. Debounce transitions (min-dwell per phase, ~250–400ms) so fast action chains don't flicker; collapse to plain dots if no status arrives (graceful degradation for cloud/shared bases that lack the new event).

7. **Surface the "waking" state.** Plumb the cloud-agent `202`/`Retry-After` resume loop in [`client-base.ts`](packages/ui/src/api/client-base.ts) into the new status (`kind: "waking"`) so the indicator says "Waking up…" instead of silent dots; align with the local-boot "waking up" placeholder so both wake paths read consistently. (Cross-ref the referenced shared→cloud handoff issue once its number is confirmed.)

8. **Tests.** Unit-test the SSE status parser and the controller status derivation; add an overlay story/e2e asserting the indicator shows "Thinking…" pre-token, an action label mid-turn, and "Writing…"/streaming after first token, with no flicker on rapid transitions.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A canonical `ChatTurnStatus` type exists once and is shared between the chat client and the agent route (no divergent copies).
- [ ] The chat SSE stream emits additive `status` events at real phase boundaries; `token`/`done`/`error` semantics are unchanged and existing clients keep working.
- [ ] The `AgentEventService` `action`/`tool`/`provider`/`lifecycle` streams are populated during a real chat turn (or the EventType→stream bridge is in place), verified by a test asserting `emitActionStart`/equivalent fires for an executed action.
- [ ] Action-callback status text (e.g. "Executing action: X") is delivered as a *status* signal, not concatenated into the assistant message body.
- [ ] The chat overlay shows a labeled, phase-specific indicator ("Thinking…", "Running &lt;ACTION&gt;…", "Searching…", "Writing…", "Speaking…", "Waking up…") in place of undifferentiated dots, anchored where the reply will appear.
- [ ] The indicator is accessible (`role="status"`, `aria-live="polite"`), respects `prefers-reduced-motion`, and obeys brand color rules (orange = accent only; no blue).
- [ ] Phase transitions do not visibly flicker on fast action chains (min-dwell/debounce verified by test).
- [ ] When no status events arrive (older/cloud/shared bases), the UI degrades cleanly to the current breathing dots.
- [ ] The cloud-agent 202 auto-resume surfaces as a "Waking up…" status instead of silent dots.
- [ ] Unit + overlay e2e coverage for parser, controller derivation, and rendered phases.

## Files & areas likely involved

- `packages/ui/src/components/shell/useShellController.ts` — add `turnStatus`; keep `responding` for gating
- `packages/ui/src/components/shell/ContinuousChatOverlay.tsx` — status-aware indicator (replaces `TypingDotsInner`/`TypingDots`)
- `packages/ui/src/components/shell/shell-state.ts` — `ShellMessage`/phase additions if status is per-message
- `packages/ui/src/api/client-base.ts` — SSE parser (`applyStreamChatDataLine`, `streamChatEndpoint`), `onStatus`; 202 resume → waking
- `packages/ui/src/api/client-chat.ts` — `sendConversationMessageStream` signature (status callback)
- `packages/ui/src/api/client-types-chat.ts` — canonical `ChatTurnStatus` type
- `packages/ui/src/state/useChatSend.ts` — thread status into chat state; replace lone `chatFirstTokenReceived`
- `packages/ui/src/hooks/useActivityEvents.ts` — reference consumer of WS `agent_event`; possibly extend for foreground turn
- `packages/ui/src/components/composites/chat/ChatVoiceStatusBar.tsx` — UX precedent (5-state dot+label+aria-live) to mirror/share
- `packages/agent/src/api/chat-routes.ts` — emit `status` SSE at phase boundaries; stop merging action-callback text into body
- `packages/agent/src/api/server.ts` — WS `agent_event`/`heartbeat_event` broadcast wiring (L3886–3938)
- `packages/agent/src/runtime/agent-event-service.ts` — `AgentEventServiceLike` accessor
- `packages/core/src/services/agentEvent.ts` — currently-dead `emitActionStart`/`emitTool`/`emitProviderStart`/`emitEvaluatorStart`
- `packages/core/src/types/agentEvent.ts` — stream/data type vocabulary
- `packages/core/src/types/events.ts` — `EventType.ACTION_STARTED/…`, `RUN_STARTED/…`, `MODEL_USED`
- `packages/core/src/runtime/execute-planned-tool-call.ts` — existing `ACTION_STARTED/COMPLETED` emit sites + "Executing action: X" callback

## Open questions

- **Transport for phase events: SSE vs WS.** The per-turn SSE is the natural channel (ordered with tokens, scoped to the turn), but the WS `agent_event` stream already exists and is broadcast. Use SSE-only, WS-only, or SSE for foreground + WS for the activity rail? Recommend SSE for the in-bubble indicator to avoid cross-channel ordering races.
- **Wire `AgentEventService` emit methods directly vs bridge the EventType bus.** Option (a) sprinkles `emitActionStart`/etc. at emit sites; option (b) adds one EventType→stream bridge. (b) is DRY-er and lights up every existing event for free; (a) is more explicit. Which?
- **Granularity / verbosity.** How much to show by default — phase + action name only, or also provider fetches and evaluator runs? `AgentRunContext.verboseLevel` exists; should the indicator honor a user-facing verbosity setting (ties into #8793 settings rework)?
- **Brand color for `speaking`.** `ChatVoiceStatusBar` uses `bg-accent` (orange) for `speaking`. Reuse as-is, or restrict orange and use neutral tones for non-speaking phases per the "accent only" rule?
- **Cross-reference numbers.** Confirm the shared→cloud handoff/"waking" issue number (the brief cites #8804, which I could not locate in-repo) so the waking state work is scoped against the right sibling; coordinate the voice-status overlap with #8786.
26:T321c,## Summary

elizaOS already has a per-slot model-routing layer (router-handler + policyEngine + a RoutingMatrix settings UI) covering TEXT_SMALL, TEXT_LARGE, TEXT_EMBEDDING, TEXT_TO_SPEECH, and TRANSCRIPTION. But the product goal (mix-and-match local vs cloud per modality, or measure device performance and use on-device TTS/STT/ASR when the device is strong enough) is NOT met. The routing decision is capability-blind (prefer-local just means "is a local handler registered"), there is a second disconnected voice local/cloud control whose value is read by nothing, the only device-tier-aware local/cloud function is dead code with zero call sites, and classifyDeviceTier().recommendedMode is never wired to routing. This issue proposes unifying voice onto the per-slot router, adding an explicit local/cloud/auto per-modality control, and an AUTO mode driven by measured device capability.

## Current state

### Text routing - solid foundation, capability-blind
- plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/router-handler.ts installs a top-priority (Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER) handler for every AgentModelSlot. At dispatch it reads per-slot prefs, asks policyEngine.pickProvider(), invokes the chosen provider directly, records latency, and falls through on failure (router-handler.ts:213-311).
- plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/routing-policy.ts (PolicyEngine) implements 5 policies: manual, cheapest, fastest, prefer-local, round-robin. fastest uses a 32-sample p50 ring buffer; cheapest uses a static COST_PER_MILLION_TOKENS table (routing-policy.ts:60-81). Local providers are free.
- Prefs persist to STATE_DIR/local-inference/routing.json via packages/shared/src/local-inference/routing-preferences.ts (RoutingPolicy, readRoutingPreferences, setPolicy, setPreferredProvider). Default policy is prefer-local (routing-preferences.ts:23).
- There is a real per-slot UI: packages/ui/src/components/local-inference/RoutingMatrix.tsx renders a policy select plus preferred-provider select per slot, including TEXT_TO_SPEECH and TRANSCRIPTION (slot list in slot-metadata.ts). It is mounted inside packages/ui/src/components/settings/ProviderSwitcher.tsx:291.

Weakness: prefer-local is purely "if an eliza-local-inference / capacitor-llama / eliza-device-bridge handler is registered, pick it" (routing-policy.ts:188-200). It does NOT consult device tier, free RAM, thermal state, or measured tokens/sec. There is no auto policy value and no local/cloud semantic, only the 5 strategies. The only capability-ish global is ELIZA_LOCAL_ONLY=1, which force-pins every slot to local (router-handler.ts:220-231).

### Voice routing - two disconnected systems, one of them dead
1. The path that actually runs: TTS/ASR dispatch goes through runtime.useModel(TEXT_TO_SPEECH | TRANSCRIPTION) (e.g. plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/routes/local-inference-tts-route.ts:107-122), so it is governed by the SAME router-handler plus prefer-local policy as text. router-handler.ts documents a TTS precedence (local, then elizacloud, then ElevenLabs, OpenAI, Groq, Edge-TTS) but it is still capability-blind.
2. A second control wired to nothing: packages/ui/src/components/settings/VoiceSection.tsx exposes a VoiceLocalCloudStrategy = "auto" | "force-local" | "force-cloud" select. VoiceSectionMount.tsx persists it under messages.voice.strategy. Grep confirms messages.voice.strategy is read by NO runtime code - the select changes nothing about where ASR/TTS run.
3. The only tier-aware local/cloud function is dead code: plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/voice-settings.ts defines VoiceSettings.backendMode and effectiveBackendMode(settings, tier) (auto: MAX/GOOD = local, OKAY/POOR = cloud; voice-settings.ts:139-148), resolved from env vars (ELIZA_VOICE_BACKEND_MODE etc). Grep for effectiveBackendMode( and resolveVoiceSettings( finds ZERO call sites in src - only the definitions, a re-export in voice/index.ts:398-406, and compiled dist copies. This module never influences runtime behavior.
4. selectVoiceBackend in packages/shared/src/local-inference/kokoro/runtime-selection.ts only chooses OmniVoice vs Kokoro (both on-device) - not a local-vs-cloud decision.

### Device capability - measured, classified, then ignored
- plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/device-tier.ts classifyDeviceTier(probe) produces a full DeviceTierAssessment with tier (MAX/GOOD/OKAY/POOR), canRunLocalLm, canRunLocalVoice, and recommendedMode: "local" | "cloud-with-local-voice" | "cloud-only" (device-tier.ts:167-243). Well-specified (mobile clamps to OKAY, Apple-Silicon-8GB clamp, free-RAM demotion).
- recommendedMode is consumed by NOTHING that drives routing - grep finds it only inside device-tier.ts. It never calls setPolicy or alters provider selection.
- plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/inference-capabilities.ts exposes streamingLlm, mtpSupported, thermalState etc, but feeds the model picker / FFI path, not routing.
- The GET /api/local-inference/hardware route returns the RAW HardwareProbe, not a tier assessment (local-inference-compat-routes.ts:314-326; service.getHardware() calls probeHardware()). The UI service uses the probe only to recommend which eliza-1 tier to DOWNLOAD, never to classify a routing tier.
- Consequently VoiceSection's VoiceTierBanner is always rendered with tier=null (falls back to GOOD) in VoiceSectionMount.tsx:151 - the banner never reflects the real device.
- A live-measurement substrate already exists for the AUTO signal: plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/device-resource-metrics.ts (tokens/sec, RSS, battery, thermal timeline; explicitly built for the Mobile Resource Workbench, #8800) and inference-telemetry.ts.

### Net assessment
The hard parts (a top-priority unified router, a per-slot persistence format, a per-slot UI, a device-tier classifier, and a live resource profiler) already exist. What is missing is the glue: a single per-modality local/cloud/auto policy, a UI that exposes it coherently instead of two contradictory controls, and an AUTO mode that reads the existing tier/metrics signal instead of leaving recommendedMode and effectiveBackendMode as dead code.

## Proposed work

1. Unify the voice local/cloud control onto the router (delete the dead path). Make VoiceSection's strategy write to the same per-slot routing prefs RoutingMatrix uses (apply to both TEXT_TO_SPEECH and TRANSCRIPTION), or remove the VoiceSection strategy select in favor of RoutingMatrix rows. Either way, eliminate the messages.voice.strategy orphan. Delete or fully wire voice-settings.ts effectiveBackendMode/resolveVoiceSettings - if kept it must be the function the router calls for the TTS/STT auto decision; if not, remove it and its re-exports.

2. Add an explicit per-modality local/cloud/auto semantic. Extend RoutingPolicy in routing-preferences.ts with an auto value (capability-driven), and add local-only (cloud-disabled) / cloud-only as first-class, replacing the implicit ELIZA_LOCAL_ONLY env hack as the canonical control. Keep prefer-local/manual/cheapest/fastest for power users. Map UI labels per slot so voice slots read On-device / Cloud / Auto rather than provider jargon.

3. Implement an AUTO policy driven by measured capability. In policyEngine.pickProvider / router-handler, when policy is auto, consult a capability snapshot: classifyDeviceTier().recommendedMode and canRunLocalLm/canRunLocalVoice (device-tier.ts) as the static gate, plus live signals from device-resource-metrics.ts / inference-telemetry.ts (tokens/sec, thermal, free RAM) for dynamic demotion (e.g. drop to cloud under serious/critical thermal or when measured TPS falls below a per-slot budget). This is the natural consumer of the #8800 Mobile Resource Workbench metrics. AUTO must be per-modality: a device can be strong enough for local TTS (Kokoro, cheap) but route text to cloud.

4. Surface device tier through the API + UI. Add GET /api/local-inference/device-tier (or extend the hardware route) returning the DeviceTierAssessment. Have the UI service classify and pass the real tier into VoiceTierBanner (kill the hardcoded tier=null), and show per slot in RoutingMatrix the AUTO resolution (e.g. "Auto: on-device, GOOD tier, 38 tok/s") so the user sees what AUTO chose and why.

5. Consolidate to one mental model + docs. One settings surface (Where models run), per-modality rows (Text, Voice output/TTS, Transcription/ASR), each On-device / Cloud / Auto. Update packages/ui/src/services/local-inference/README.md and the router-handler doc comment.

6. Tests. Unit-test the AUTO decision across tiers/thermal/TPS per modality; e2e that flipping a row actually changes the dispatched provider (coordinate with the e2e matrix work in #8797/#8798/#8802 and zero-cost mock-LLM fixtures in #8801).

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Exactly ONE user-facing control for local-vs-cloud per modality; the orphaned messages.voice.strategy path is removed or wired so its value provably changes ASR/TTS routing.
- [ ] RoutingPolicy supports an auto value (and explicit local-only / cloud-only), persisted in routing.json, settable from the settings UI per slot including TEXT_TO_SPEECH and TRANSCRIPTION.
- [ ] AUTO routing consults the existing capability signal: a low-tier/POOR or thermally-throttled device routes the affected modality to cloud; a MAX/GOOD device with adequate measured TPS routes it on-device - verified by unit tests over classifyDeviceTier outputs and resource-metric inputs.
- [ ] AUTO is independent per modality (e.g. local TTS + cloud text on the same device).
- [ ] effectiveBackendMode / resolveVoiceSettings are either the real AUTO implementation invoked by the router, or deleted along with their re-exports - no dead tier-aware routing code remains.
- [ ] Device tier is exposed via an API and rendered live in VoiceTierBanner (no hardcoded tier=null), and RoutingMatrix shows the resolved AUTO choice + reason per slot.
- [ ] e2e proves a UI change to a modality's routing changes the dispatched provider; cloud-frontend visual review passes for the reworked settings surface.

## Files & areas likely involved

- packages/shared/src/local-inference/routing-preferences.ts - RoutingPolicy union, persistence
- plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/routing-policy.ts - policyEngine.pickProvider (add AUTO)
- plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/router-handler.ts - dispatch + AUTO capability read; retire ELIZA_LOCAL_ONLY hack as canonical control
- plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/device-tier.ts - classifyDeviceTier / recommendedMode (AUTO static gate)
- plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/device-resource-metrics.ts, inference-telemetry.ts - live AUTO signal (ties to #8800)
- plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/inference-capabilities.ts - thermal/streaming gates
- plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/voice-settings.ts - dead effectiveBackendMode/resolveVoiceSettings (wire or delete)
