## Open questions

- Should AUTO be allowed to switch MID-SESSION (e.g. demote to cloud when the device thermally throttles), or only at session/turn boundaries? voice-settings.ts had an autoSwap flag - do we resurface that concept?
- Granularity: per modality (Text / TTS / ASR / Embeddings) only, or also per size slot (TEXT_SMALL vs TEXT_LARGE)? The current router is per-slot; the product ask is per-modality - confirm we keep slot granularity under the hood but present modality-level controls.
- For privacy-sensitive users, should local-only be a hard guarantee (fail rather than fall through to cloud) or a preference? Today prefer-local silently falls through to cloud on any local failure.
- Does AUTO's cost dimension matter (route to cheapest cloud when cloud is chosen), or is the AUTO decision purely capability/latency? Reconcile with the existing cheapest/fastest policies.
- Where does Eliza Cloud sign-in state gate this? If the user is not signed into Cloud and has no direct API keys, cloud / auto-to-cloud options should be disabled or prompt to connect - needs a product decision on the empty-provider UX.
27:T446e,## Summary

The local-inference stack already has a real memory-management spine — a `MemoryArbiter` with refcounted handles, an LRU `evictToFit` budget path, a `MemoryMonitor` + `MemoryPressureSource` eviction loop, a `SharedResourceRegistry` priority table, a `ram-budget` admission gate, and a `device-tier` classifier — but four product-owner asks are only partially landed or missing entirely: (1) there is **no desktop memory-benchmarking harness** (the only grind runner is iOS-only and covers text/TTS/ASR, not embedding/VAD/vision); (2) the **LRU loaded-model eviction is effectively dead in production** because resident-footprint estimates (`estimatedMb`) are zero for the text and embedding roles, so the proactive fit-path never fires; (3) **"fit the model to available RAM" is static minRam-floor gating, not dynamic quant/context selection** — `selectBestQuantizationVariant` picks the catalog default, not a quant chosen for free RAM, and the memory-aware *context* selector described in `CONTEXT_SCALING.md` is unimplemented; (4) **predictive/preload is only KV-prefix and voice-stack prewarm** — there is no usage- or context-driven warm-load of a *model* before it is needed. This issue scopes hardening all four into one verifiable workstream.

## Current state

### What exists and works
- **MemoryArbiter** (`src/services/memory-arbiter.ts`) is the single in-process owner of every model handle (text, embedding, vision-describe, image-gen, transcribe, speak). It refcounts handles, serializes per-`(capability, modelKey)` loads (`inFlightLoads`), does same-role swap-on-conflict with a `waitForRefcountZero` drain (10 s timeout), and subscribes to pressure. Telemetry events (`model_load`, `model_unload`, `memory_pressure`, `eviction`, `capability_run`) are emitted via `onEvent`. There **is** an LRU cache of loaded models: `resident: Map`, with `lastUsedAt` tracking and an LRU `evictToFit(incomingMb)` path (`memory-arbiter.ts:682-727`) that drops the least-recently-used evictable resident (refcount 0, never `text-target`) before a load that would exceed the budget.
- **Pressure-driven eviction** is real: `handlePressure` (`memory-arbiter.ts:729-770`) purges the vision-embedding cache, evicts the lowest-priority role at `low`, and evicts every non-text role at `critical`; `acquire` throws for non-text capabilities under `critical`. `MemoryMonitor` (`memory-monitor.ts`) independently polls `os.freemem()` on a 30 s cadence and walks `SharedResourceRegistry.evictLowestPriorityRole()` under a low-water line, with eviction cooldown + exhausted-backoff.
- **Eviction priority table** is centralized: `RESIDENT_ROLE_PRIORITY` in `voice/shared-resources.ts:58-70` (`drafter < emotion < speaker-id < vision < embedding < vad < asr < tts < text-target`).
- **Static fit gate**: `ram-budget.ts` is the single source of truth for "does model X boot on a host with N MB usable RAM" (`assessRamFit` → `fits|tight|wontfit`), preferring the on-disk `eliza-1.manifest.json` `ramBudgetMb.{min,recommended}` and otherwise synthesizing `recommendedMb = minRamGb + compressedKvBytes × contextLength`. The load admission gate `assertModelFitsHost` (`active-model.ts:672-698`) throws `ModelDoesNotFitError` (with a fitting-context-variant hint) on no-fit. Budget resolution is memoized by `modelId + manifestSha256`.
- **Tier classifier**: `device-tier.ts` maps a `HardwareProbe` to `MAX|GOOD|OKAY|POOR`, clamps mobile/Apple-Silicon-8GB to OKAY, and applies a free-RAM demotion when `freeRamGb < totalRamGb × 0.25`.
- **Prewarm**: `voice-prewarm.ts` warms the voice stack (ASR+TTS) on model select; `prewarmResponseHandler`/`prewarmSystemPrefix` (`ensure-local-inference-handler.ts:1107+`) KV-prefill the stable system prefix slot. `maybeAutoResizeParallel` (`engine.ts:439`) grows llama.cpp `--parallel` when conversations outgrow slots *and* there's RAM headroom.
- **iOS grind**: `plugin-capacitor-bridge/src/ios/model-grind.ts` (`runModelGrind`) loads + exercises text/TTS/ASR on-device, samples `available_ram_gb` before/after to derive `peakUsedDeltaGb`, and gates on output/WER. Env-triggered via `ELIZA_IOS_RUN_MODEL_GRIND=1`.

### What's weak, broken, or missing
1. **The LRU fit-path is wired but inert for the two most important roles.** `evictToFit` is a no-op when `incomingMb <= 0` (`memory-arbiter.ts:685`), and `estimatedMb` defaults to 0 for any capability registered without it. The **text** capability and **embedding** capability are registered with no `estimatedMb`, and the engine's `text-target` `EvictableModelRole` is created with no `estimatedMb` (`engine.ts:370-383`) and no `estimatedResidentMb`. Only vision (`600`) and image-gen (`1100`) pass estimates (`service.ts:515,569`). Net effect: the proactive multi-model fit eviction described in `MEMORY_ARBITER.md` essentially never triggers for the dominant LLM/embedding footprints, and the `residentMb()` sum it compares against the budget systematically under-counts. This is a real bug, not just a tuning gap.
2. **No desktop memory-benchmarking harness.** `packages/benchmarks/loadperf` measures bundle/boot/frontend KPIs and carries a single `boot.peakRssMb: 1600` budget (`budgets.json`), but there is no harness that loads each Eliza-1 tier + voice/vision/embedding model and records resident RSS delta, peak RSS, tokens/sec, load ms, or eviction counts on desktop/server. The only model-grind that records memory is iOS-only and covers just text/TTS/ASR (not embedding, VAD, or vision). `memory-and-e2e-latency-review.md` §5 specs the per-model metric set (load ms, first-token/first-audio, tok/s or RTF, peak RSS delta, WER, VAD MAE, eviction count) but it is not built as a runnable harness with CI budgets. This overlaps the mobile-side **#8800 Mobile Resource Workbench** — the two should share a metric schema, with this issue owning the desktop/server harness + the arbiter telemetry feed.
3. **"Fit the model to available RAM" is floor-gating, not dynamic selection.** `selectBestQuantizationVariant` (`recommendation.ts:134-147`) picks `defaultVariantId` → first published → first variant — it never consults free RAM to choose a *smaller quant when memory is tight* or a *larger/more-accurate quant when there's headroom*. The recommender ladders (`SLOT_LADDERS`) pick the largest *tier* that passes `assessRamFit` against effective memory, which is good, but quant-within-tier and the **memory-aware context selector** are missing. `CONTEXT_SCALING.md` §3/§5 specifies the unimplemented piece: after a tier is chosen, raise runtime `mtp.contextSize` toward `min(tier.contextLength, baseNativeContext, floor((mem − weights − workingSet) / kvBytesPerToken) − margin)` using `estimateQuantizedKvBytesPerToken`, and an opt-in `preferAccurateKvWhenHeadroom` branch (f16/q8_0 KV on roomy hosts). Today every device runs the static catalog `contextLength`, leaving large slack on 16–128 GB hosts and the last ~8k of native range unused on phones.
4. **Predictive model loading does not exist.** Prewarm today is KV-prefix prefill and voice-stack warm only — both warm the *currently active* model/stack. There is no component that anticipates the *next model* and warm-loads it before a request: e.g. warm ASR/TTS for the next voice stage while the LM generates (called out as a TODO in `memory-and-e2e-latency-review.md` §3/§4: "pre-warm the next stage's model during the current stage"), warm a vision model when an image attachment is detected pre-LLM, or warm a larger tier when sustained usage/headroom suggests an upgrade. The arbiter is the right home (it already owns load/evict/refcount + LRU recency), but exposes no `preload(capability, modelKey)` / predictive admission API.
5. **Two parallel pressure/eviction loops that don't share state.** `MemoryMonitor` (30 s `os.freemem` poll, `evictLowestPriorityRole`) and the arbiter's `MemoryPressureSource` (5 s `nodeOsPressureSource` + capacitor bridge, `handlePressure`) both observe memory and both evict, but independently. They can double-evict or disagree on level. The relationship should be consolidated or explicitly layered.
6. **Mobile pressure + arbiter are JS-only and not wired to the OS on iOS.** `capacitorPressureSource.dispatch` and `MEMORY_ARBITER.md` "Validation status" both confirm the native `onTrimMemory`/`didReceiveMemoryWarning` → `dispatchMobilePressure` path is unimplemented (WS2/WS8), and `memory-and-e2e-latency-review.md` §2 item 11 notes "no runtime memory arbiter on iOS" — the TS arbiter does not run in the Swift bridge, which uses a single ~3 GB free-RAM probe to gate MTP. So on the most memory-constrained tier, none of the dynamic management actually runs.

## Proposed work

Ordered; each step is independently shippable.

1. **Fix resident-footprint estimates so the LRU fit-path is live.** Populate `estimatedMb` for the text and embedding arbiter capabilities and the engine's `text-target` `EvictableModelRole` from the resolved RAM budget (`ram-budget.resolveRamBudget` weights + KV at the active context, or the loaded GGUF byte size). Make `evictToFit` and `MemoryMonitor` eviction logs assert a non-zero reclaim estimate for these roles. Add a regression test that a second tier load on a constrained budget evicts the LRU resident.
2. **Unify the two eviction loops.** Decide and document one owner: either fold `MemoryMonitor`'s low-water poll into the arbiter's `MemoryPressureSource` (preferred — the arbiter already has the resident map + LRU + refcount semantics), or make `MemoryMonitor` delegate to the arbiter so there is a single eviction decision point. Remove the duplicate `os.freemem` poller.
3. **Build a desktop/server memory-benchmarking harness** under `packages/benchmarks/loadperf` (or a sibling `memperf`), sharing a metric schema with #8800. Per (tier × modality: text/embedding/ASR/TTS/VAD/vision) record: load ms, resident RSS delta, peak RSS, tokens/sec or RTF, first-token/first-audio ms, and arbiter eviction count under a scripted co-residency sequence (load text → load vision → load voice → force pressure). Feed the arbiter `onEvent` telemetry into the report. Add CI budgets to `budgets.json` (per-tier peak RSS, max evictions for a known-fitting set) and ratchet down over time, matching the existing loadperf gate pattern.
4. **Dynamic fit-to-RAM selection.** (a) Make quant selection memory-aware: extend `selectBestQuantizationVariant` (or add `selectQuantForBudget`) to pick a smaller published quant when `assessRamFit` is `tight`/`wontfit` and a more-accurate one when there's headroom. (b) Implement the memory-aware **context selector** from `CONTEXT_SCALING.md` §5 in `recommendation.ts`/load-args resolution: clamp/raise runtime `contextSize` to `min(tier.contextLength, baseNativeContext, maxFittingContext − margin)` using `estimateQuantizedKvBytesPerToken`, keeping `qjl+polarq4` as the assumed layout. (c) Add the opt-in `preferAccurateKvWhenHeadroom` (f16/q8_0 KV) branch keyed off probed free VRAM/RAM. No catalog `contextLength` edits (per the doc's RESOLVED/LOW notes).
5. **Predictive preloading.** Add an arbiter `preload(capability, modelKey)` that warm-loads under a "nominal pressure + budget fits" guard, refusing under `low`/`critical`. Wire concrete predictors: (a) voice pipeline warms the next stage's model during the current stage (LM generating → keep ASR warm for barge-in; ASR running → warm TTS) instead of fire-and-forget eviction; (b) pre-LLM image-attachment detection warms vision-describe; (c) an optional usage/recency heuristic (driven by the arbiter's existing `lastUsedAt`) that keeps the hot set resident on roomy devices. Gate all of it behind the device tier (no speculative preload on POOR/OKAY-mobile).
6. **iOS parity (coordinate with #8787/#8800).** Wire the native `onTrimMemory`/`didReceiveMemoryWarning` → `dispatchMobilePressure` bridge so `capacitorPressureSource` is live, and extend the iOS grind runner to cover embedding + VAD + vision so the on-device report matches the desktop harness schema.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Text and embedding resident roles report non-zero `estimatedMb`/`estimatedResidentMb`, and a test proves `evictToFit` drops the LRU resident when a new load would exceed the budget (no more silent `incomingMb <= 0` no-op for the dominant roles).
- [ ] Exactly one memory-pressure eviction loop owns the decision; the duplicate `os.freemem` poller is removed or explicitly delegated, with a test asserting no double-eviction on a single pressure event.
- [ ] A runnable desktop/server memory-benchmark harness exists, emits a per-(tier × modality) report (load ms, RSS delta, peak RSS, tok/s or RTF, eviction count), shares its metric schema with #8800, and is wired into CI with budgets in `budgets.json` that fail the build on regression.
- [ ] Quant and runtime context are selected as a function of available memory (smaller quant / shorter context when tight, larger context / accurate KV when there's headroom), verified by unit tests across the 4/8/16/24/128 GB device classes; no catalog `contextLength` drift.
- [ ] The arbiter exposes a guarded `preload` API and at least the voice next-stage predictor is wired, demonstrated by a test that the next-stage model is resident before its first request under nominal pressure and is refused under `critical`.
- [ ] Arbiter `onEvent` telemetry (loads/evictions/pressure) is surfaced where the benchmark harness (and #8800 workbench) can consume it.
- [ ] `memory-and-e2e-latency-review.md` and `MEMORY_ARBITER.md` "Validation status" are updated to reflect what is now live vs deferred.

## Files & areas likely involved

- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/memory-arbiter.ts` — `evictToFit`, `lruEvictionCandidate`, `estimatedMb` plumbing, new `preload` API, telemetry.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/memory-monitor.ts` + `memory-pressure.ts` — consolidate the two eviction loops.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/shared-resources.ts` — `RESIDENT_ROLE_PRIORITY`, `EvictableModelRole.estimatedResidentMb`.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/engine.ts` — `text-target` role `estimatedMb`, idle-unload, `maybeAutoResizeParallel`, prewarm hooks.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/service.ts` — arbiter wiring, `budgetMb`, capability registrations + estimates.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/ram-budget.ts` — budget resolution feeding `estimatedMb`.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/recommendation.ts` — `selectBestQuantizationVariant`, ladders, memory-aware context selector.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/active-model.ts` — load-args resolution, admission gate, `contextSize` clamping.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/device-tier.ts` — tier inputs for predictive/preload gating.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice-prewarm.ts` + `src/runtime/ensure-local-inference-handler.ts` — next-stage predictive preload.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/pipeline.ts` / `scheduler.ts` — voice next-stage warm wiring.
- `plugins/plugin-capacitor-bridge/src/ios/model-grind.ts` + `ios/bridge.ts` — iOS pressure bridge + grind coverage (embedding/VAD/vision).
- `packages/benchmarks/loadperf/` (`budgets.json`, `boot-kpi.mjs`, `run-all.mjs`) — new memory-benchmark harness + CI budgets.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/docs/memory-and-e2e-latency-review.md`, `docs/MEMORY_ARBITER.md`, `packages/shared/src/local-inference/CONTEXT_SCALING.md` — spec/status updates.
- `packages/shared/src/local-inference/` (`catalog.ts`, `kv-spill.ts`, `types.ts`) — `estimateQuantizedKvBytesPerToken`, quant variants.

## Open questions

- **Eviction loop ownership:** fold `MemoryMonitor` into the arbiter's pressure source, or keep `MemoryMonitor` as a thin delegator? (Recommend the former; needs a maintainer call since `MemoryMonitor` is exposed for diagnostics/tests via `engine.getMemoryMonitor()`.)
- **`estimatedMb` source of truth:** derive from `ram-budget` (weights + KV at active context) or from the actual loaded GGUF byte size + a fixed working-set adder? The former is consistent with the admission gate; the latter is closer to true RSS.
- **Predictive preload aggressiveness:** is speculative warm-load acceptable on GOOD-tier desktop only, or also on MAX-tier mobile? Where is the line given the jetsam ceiling, and should it be a user-facing toggle?
- **Benchmark harness home:** extend `packages/benchmarks/loadperf` (which is bundle/boot/frontend-oriented) or a new `memperf` sibling, and how tightly should it couple to #8800's mobile schema?
- **`preferAccurateKvWhenHeadroom`:** default off (current behavior) and opt-in, or auto-enable above a probed-VRAM threshold?
- **iOS arbiter:** port the TS arbiter decision logic into the Swift bridge, or keep eviction TS-side and have Swift only forward pressure + execute unload commands?

## Related (do not duplicate)

- **#8800 Mobile Resource Workbench** — owns the mobile battery/perf/memory/tok-per-sec surface; this issue owns the desktop/server harness + arbiter telemetry and should share its metric schema.
- **#8794 eliza-1 Gemma vs Qwen perf/memory eval** — model-choice memory comparison; consumes this harness rather than reimplementing it.
- **#8787 Kokoro mobile-default / OmniVoice native** and **#8786 voice through full pipeline** — voice-stack residency/prewarm intersects the predictive-preload work here.
28:T3f91,## Summary

elizaOS already has a genuinely good zero-cost test substrate — a first-class deterministic mock-LLM provider (`packages/test/mocks/helpers/llm-proxy-plugin.ts`), 22 Mockoon external-service environments, a contract test that keeps mock coverage honest (`provider-coverage.ts`), and a nightly drift lane that re-checks 5 mocks against live APIs — but it is **wired into only a thin slice of the repo**. Only ~13 of 159 plugins have any AgentRuntime-level test; the deterministic LLM proxy is consumed almost exclusively by `scenario-runner` and LifeOps; the connector and LLM Mockoon environments are mostly orphaned relative to the plugins they emulate; and the PR e2e lane runs **17 of 684 scenario files**. This issue proposes promoting that substrate to a repo-wide standard: one shared mock-LLM provider with an explicit correct-and-incorrect response contract, a recorded-fixture + drift system for every external service, and a PR CI lane that exercises the full scenario corpus at zero external cost.

## Current state

### What exists and is genuinely solid (not larp)

- **A real deterministic mock-LLM provider.** `packages/test/mocks/helpers/llm-proxy-plugin.ts` (1,529 LOC) exports `createDeterministicLlmProxyPlugin`. It registers handlers for every text `ModelType` (`TEXT_NANO`…`TEXT_MEGA`, `RESPONSE_HANDLER`, `ACTION_PLANNER`, reasoning tiers) plus `TEXT_EMBEDDING`, at `priority: 1000` so it wins model dispatch. It supports named fixtures matched on `modelType`, normalized user `input`, raw `prompt`, available `toolName`/`toolNames`, `responseSchema`, and tool-schema fingerprints, with consumption bounds (`times`), `strict` mode (fail-closed on unmatched / multi-matched calls), structured-output validation (JSON parse, `responseSchema` check, tool-arg check), and rich diagnostics (`getFixtureDiagnostics()`). Its own unit suite is thorough — 28 cases in `packages/test/mocks/__tests__/llm-proxy-plugin.test.ts` including fail-closed-on-no-match, tie-breaking, exhaustion, and "rejects raw prose from strict ACTION_PLANNER fixtures." This is the asset to build on, not replace.
- **22 Mockoon external-service environments** under `packages/test/mocks/environments/` (twilio, whatsapp, calendly, x-twitter, google/gmail, github, discord, slack, telegram, linear, shopify, payments, signal, bluebubbles, browser-workspace, plus `openai.json`/`anthropic.json`/`vision.json`), served by an in-process runner (`packages/test/mocks/scripts/start-mocks.ts`) with stateful dynamic routes. Coverage and remaining gaps per provider are documented and **executably enforced**: `packages/test/mocks/helpers/provider-coverage.ts` + `provider-coverage-contract.test.ts` fail when a provider, mock env, validation file, or documented gap drifts out of sync.
- **A drift-validation lane already exists for a few providers.** `.github/workflows/external-api-live-drift.yml` runs `*.real.test.ts` for Polymarket, Hyperliquid, CoinGecko, Calendly, and Tavily nightly against the real APIs specifically to catch mock-vs-reality drift. This is exactly the "validate fixtures against live API periodically" pattern — but it covers only 5 surfaces.
- **A real zero-key e2e lane runs on PRs.** `ci.yaml` (`zero-key-e2e` job) and `test.yml` (`zero-key-*` jobs, gated into the required `test-status` check) blank all provider keys (`OPENAI_API_KEY: ""`, etc.), set `SCENARIO_USE_LLM_PROXY: "1"`, and run scenario + UI-smoke + browser coverage with no secrets. `scenario-pr.yml` does the same for scenarios.
- **A silent-skip guard.** `packages/test/vitest/fail-on-silent-skip.setup.ts` fails any run that skips a test without an explicit `SKIP_REASON`, so credential-gated suites can no longer pass CI by vanishing.

### Where it is weak / missing / not wired (the actual problem)

- **The mock LLM is not integrated per-plugin.** A grep for `createDeterministicLlmProxyPlugin` / `deterministic-llm-proxy` across the repo returns essentially only `packages/scenario-runner/*`, `packages/test/mocks/*`, and a few `packages/app-core` tests. Of 159 plugins, only ~13 import an `AgentRuntime`/mock-runtime in any test, and 25 of those imports are a single plugin (`plugin-personal-assistant`). The remaining plugins either have no tests, or test in isolation with `vi.mock` at the SDK boundary. **The product ask — "every single plugin has extensive e2e testing with the calling of this mock LLM provider" — is not met.**
- **Two disjoint mock-LLM systems that never meet.** There is a *runtime-level* mock (the `deterministic-llm-proxy` plugin, intercepts `useModel`) and a *wire-level* mock (`environments/openai.json` + `anthropic.json`, served at `ELIZA_MOCK_OPENAI_BASE`/`ELIZA_MOCK_ANTHROPIC_BASE`). The wire-level LLM mocks are **orphaned**: no model-provider plugin (`plugin-openai`, `plugin-anthropic`) ever reads `ELIZA_MOCK_*_BASE` (grep confirms zero consumers outside the mock infra), and unlike the 5 connectors above, `openai.json`/`anthropic.json` have **no drift test** validating them against the real API — so the provider plugins' own request/response shaping is never exercised end-to-end against a captured fixture.
- **Model-provider plugin tests are SDK-shape, not runtime-e2e.** `plugin-anthropic`/`plugin-openai` tests are `*.shape.test.ts` (mock `@ai-sdk/anthropic`, assert request munging like temperature/topP rules — e.g. `plugins/plugin-anthropic/__tests__/provider-fetch.shape.test.ts`) and `*.live.test.ts` (real API, excluded by default). There is no keyless test that drives a real turn through these plugins against a captured Anthropic/OpenAI response.
- **Connector mocks aren't used by the connectors.** `environments/discord.json`, `telegram.json`, `slack.json`, `linear.json`, `shopify.json` are declared and contract-tested, but `plugin-discord`/`plugin-telegram`/`plugin-slack` do not consume them — grep for `ELIZA_MOCK_DISCORD_BASE`/`startMocks` in those plugins is empty. Those plugins test renderers/parsers in isolation (e.g. `plugins/plugin-discord/__tests__/interactions.test.ts`). The mock environments primarily exist to back LifeOps. So a connector's full inbound→runtime→mock-LLM→outbound loop is not e2e-tested keyless.
- **The PR e2e lane runs a tiny fraction of the corpus.** `packages/scenario-runner/package.json` `test:pr:e2e` → `test:deterministic:e2e` runs exactly **17 hand-named `deterministic-*` scenarios**. There are **684 `.scenario.ts` files** under `packages/test/scenarios/` (50+ domains: all 30 `connector-certification` scenarios, all `messaging.*` discord/telegram/signal/whatsapp/imessage, dozens of LifeOps domains, payments, relationships, todos, goals). The other ~667 run only in the live/scheduled lane (`live-scenarios.yml`) requiring real keys — even though many are pure deterministic flows that could run zero-cost through the proxy + Mockoon connectors today.
- **Most real coverage is nightly/non-blocking, not PR-gating.** The dimensions that need real fidelity live in scheduled, `continue-on-error: true` lanes that never run on PRs and never block: `app-live-e2e.yml` (07:00 UTC), `app-real-e2e.yml` (09:00), `voice-live-e2e.yml`, `external-api-live-drift.yml`. Their PR-time equivalent is the deterministic stub stack (`FORCE_STUB_STACK` / `ELIZA_UI_SMOKE_LIVE_STACK`), so any regression only the live stack catches is invisible until the next nightly.
- **"Incorrect response" testing is a capability, not a practice.** The proxy *can* emit deliberately-wrong outputs (the "rejects raw prose" case proves the mechanism), but there is no systematic per-plugin matrix that asserts graceful handling of a malformed/hallucinated/wrong-tool LLM response. The product explicitly wants "both correct and incorrect responses."

## Proposed work

Ordered so each step unlocks the next. Coordinate with siblings: voice owns its harness in #8785, LifeOps scenario authoring in #8795, view-switch/view-dependent matrices in #8797/#8798 — this issue provides the shared mock substrate those build on.

1. **Promote the mock-LLM provider to a published, documented test entrypoint.** Export `createDeterministicLlmProxyPlugin` and the fixture registry from a stable path consumable by any plugin's test (today it is reached via a relative `packages/test/mocks/helpers/...` import and a `loadTestMocks()` shim in `scenario-runner/src/runtime-factory.ts`). Add a tiny `withMockLlmRuntime()` helper (wrapping the existing `createMockedTestRuntime`/`mock-runtime.ts`) so a plugin author writes one import to get a PGLite runtime + mock LLM + mock connectors.

2. **Define an explicit correct/incorrect response contract.** Standardize fixture authoring so each plugin e2e declares (a) the canonical correct LLM output (exact tool-call or text) and (b) at least one adversarial output (malformed JSON, wrong tool name, hallucinated action, empty/`finishReason:"length"`), asserting the plugin/runtime degrades safely (no silent success, surfaced error, retry, or fallback). Reuse `strict` mode + `validateResponse` already in `llm-proxy-plugin.ts`; codify the negative cases as a shared fixture pack rather than per-test reinvention.

3. **Wire the wire-level LLM mocks to the real provider plugins, or retire them.** Either make `plugin-openai`/`plugin-anthropic` honor `ELIZA_MOCK_OPENAI_BASE`/`ELIZA_MOCK_ANTHROPIC_BASE` in test mode and add an `openai`/`anthropic` entry to `external-api-live-drift.yml` so `openai.json`/`anthropic.json` are captured-and-drift-checked like the other 5 — or delete the orphaned environments. Decide one owner per concept (runtime-level proxy vs wire-level mock) per the repo's single-source-of-truth rule.

4. **Build a recorded-fixture + drift system for external services, generalizing `external-api-live-drift.yml`.** Capture real responses once (redacted), store as fixtures the Mockoon runner replays, and extend the nightly drift lane from 5 providers to every provider in `provider-coverage.ts`, failing (or warning, as today via `continue-on-error`) when a mock diverges from live. Keep captures behind the existing redaction discipline already documented in the mocks README.

5. **Make connectors consume their own mocks end-to-end.** Add the inbound→runtime→mock-LLM→outbound loop for `plugin-discord`/`plugin-telegram`/`plugin-slack` (and others with a Mockoon env) using the existing `connector-certification` scenarios as the template — those already model `certify-core` / `certify-disconnected` / `certify-rate-limited` per connector. Run them keyless through the proxy.

6. **Widen the PR e2e lane from 17 to the full deterministic-eligible corpus.** Tag every one of the 684 scenarios as `pr-deterministic` (runnable keyless) vs `live-only`, then drive `test:pr:e2e` off the tag instead of a hand-maintained scenario list. Shard across CI (the `TEST_SHARD=N/M` knob in `run-all-tests.mjs` already exists) to keep wall-clock acceptable. The `check-scenario-workflow-coverage.mjs` gate should fail when a new scenario is neither tagged nor justified.

7. **Add a per-plugin e2e coverage gate.** Extend the existing coverage gate so a plugin shipping actions/providers/services without at least one mock-LLM-backed e2e (or a written `N/A`) fails CI — mirroring the architecture commandment that every endpoint needs a real caller.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] `createDeterministicLlmProxyPlugin` + a `withMockLlmRuntime()`-style helper are importable from a stable, documented path; at least the model-provider, top connector, and top app plugins have a keyless e2e that drives a real runtime turn through the mock LLM.
- [ ] A shared negative-response fixture pack exists; representative plugins assert safe handling of malformed JSON, wrong/hallucinated tool calls, and truncated outputs (correct **and** incorrect paths both covered).
- [ ] `openai.json`/`anthropic.json` are either consumed by `plugin-openai`/`plugin-anthropic` in test mode **and** drift-checked in `external-api-live-drift.yml`, or removed; no orphaned LLM wire-mocks remain.
- [ ] `external-api-live-drift.yml` covers every provider listed in `provider-coverage.ts` (not just 5); a divergence between mock and live surfaces as a failed/flagged nightly run.
- [ ] `plugin-discord`/`plugin-telegram`/`plugin-slack` have an inbound→mock-LLM→outbound e2e running keyless in PR CI via their Mockoon environments.
- [ ] Every `.scenario.ts` is tagged `pr-deterministic` or `live-only`; `test:pr:e2e` runs the full `pr-deterministic` set (sharded) instead of 17 hand-named files; `check-scenario-workflow-coverage.mjs` fails on an untagged scenario.
- [ ] A per-plugin e2e coverage gate fails on a plugin with action/provider/service surface and zero mock-LLM e2e (or unjustified `N/A`).
- [ ] The widened lanes are in the required `test-status` gate (PR-blocking), run with all provider/cloud keys blanked, and incur zero external API cost; `bun run test:e2e` passes locally with no secrets.

## Files & areas likely involved

- `packages/test/mocks/helpers/llm-proxy-plugin.ts` — the mock-LLM provider (extend/export, negative-response pack)
- `packages/test/mocks/helpers/mock-runtime.ts`, `packages/test/mocks/scripts/start-mocks.ts` — runtime + Mockoon runner glue
- `packages/test/mocks/helpers/provider-coverage.ts` + `provider-coverage-contract.test.ts` — coverage source of truth (extend to all providers)
- `packages/test/mocks/environments/*.json` (esp. `openai.json`, `anthropic.json`, `discord.json`, `telegram.json`, `slack.json`) and `packages/test/mocks/README.md` / `mockoon/INVENTORY.md`
- `packages/scenario-runner/src/runtime-factory.ts` (`loadTestMocks`, `SCENARIO_USE_LLM_PROXY`, strict mode), `packages/scenario-runner/package.json` (`test:pr:e2e` / `test:deterministic:e2e` scripts)
- `packages/scenario-runner/test/scenarios/_helpers/strict-llm-action-fixtures.ts` — strict fixture authoring template
- `packages/test/scenarios/**` — 684 scenario files to tag (esp. `connector-certification/`, `messaging.*`, `lifeops.*`)
- `packages/scripts/run-all-tests.mjs` (lanes/shards) and `packages/scripts/check-scenario-workflow-coverage.mjs` (gate)
- `.github/workflows/ci.yaml`, `test.yml`, `scenario-pr.yml`, `external-api-live-drift.yml`, `app-live-e2e.yml`, `app-real-e2e.yml`, `coverage-gate.yml`
- `plugins/plugin-openai/__tests__/**`, `plugins/plugin-anthropic/__tests__/**`, `plugins/plugin-discord/__tests__/**`, `plugins/plugin-telegram/**`, `plugins/plugin-slack/**`
- `packages/test/vitest/fail-on-silent-skip.setup.ts` — keep enforcing no silent skips on the widened lanes

## Open questions

- **One mock-LLM concept or two?** Should the runtime-level `deterministic-llm-proxy` be the single canonical mock (and the `openai.json`/`anthropic.json` wire-mocks deleted), or do we keep the wire-mocks specifically to exercise each provider plugin's request/response shaping end-to-end? They serve different layers; the repo's single-source-of-truth rule pushes toward one, but provider-plugin shaping coverage argues for both.
- **CI wall-clock budget for the full corpus.** Running ~667 additional scenarios keyless will lengthen PR CI. What is the acceptable PR-time ceiling, and how many shards / which runner class do we allocate? (The `TEST_SHARD` mechanism exists; the budget decision does not.)
- **Drift-lane secret availability.** Extending `external-api-live-drift.yml` to all providers needs read-only API credentials for each. Which providers can we get sandbox/read tokens for, and which stay mock-only with a hand-curated (un-drift-checked) fixture and an explicit caveat?
- **Fixture-capture provenance & redaction.** What is the approved pipeline to capture-and-redact real API responses into committed fixtures (the README already forbids raw mailbox captures)? Does this need a maintainer-run capture tool rather than ad-hoc capture?
- **Negative-case scope.** Is one adversarial fixture per plugin sufficient, or do we want a standard matrix (malformed-JSON / wrong-tool / empty / truncated) applied uniformly — and is a uniform matrix worth the maintenance vs targeted cases?
- **Per-plugin gate strictness.** Should the per-plugin e2e coverage gate hard-fail immediately, or land as warn-only first to avoid a large one-shot backfill blocking unrelated PRs?
29:T384b,## Summary

The view-dependent action system has three loosely-coupled layers (explicit `ViewCapability` arrays, the universal agent-surface `useAgentElement` bridge, and the hand-maintained server-side `VIEW_ACTION_MAP` affinity table), but there is no enforced 1:1 guarantee that everything a user can *do* in a view is reachable by the agent, and there is no per-view e2e coverage proving it. Today only ~7 of ~33 plugin views declare any `ViewCapability`, **zero plugins implement the `serverInteract` backend path**, the slash-command catalog (`CommandDefinition`) has no concept of view-scoping at all, and the only agent-surface e2e test drives a synthetic fixture rather than any real plugin view. We need an audit that asserts every in-view control maps to a registered view-dependent action (or universal agent-surface element) plus a view-dependent slash command, backed by deterministic e2e tests per view.

## Current state

**The three layers that exist (grounded in source):**

1. **Explicit `ViewCapability` per view** — `ViewDeclaration.capabilities?: ViewCapability[]` (`packages/core/src/types/plugin.ts:498-509`, `:580-590`). A capability is `{ id, description, params? }`. Dispatched via `POST /api/views/:id/interact` (`packages/agent/src/api/views-routes.ts:896-1000`), validated against the view's declared list (`:922-937`). **Adoption is sparse**: only `plugin-app-control` (views-manager TUI), `plugin-facewear` (2), `plugin-task-coordinator` (4), `plugin-feed`, `plugin-scape`, `plugin-clawville`, `plugin-trajectory-logger`, and `plugin-defense-of-the-agents` declare any. The other ~25 declared views (calendar, documents, messages, phone, contacts, finances, goals, health, inbox, relationships, todos, wallet-ui, steward, hyperliquid, polymarket, shopify-ui, screenshare, social-alpha, vincent, vector-browser, hyperscape, waifu apps, 2004scape) declare **none**.

2. **`serverInteract` backend handler** — `ViewDeclaration.serverInteract?` (`packages/core/src/types/plugin.ts:587-590`) is wired into the route (`packages/agent/src/api/views-routes.ts:959-991`) to run a capability *without* a mounted UI. **No plugin in the repo implements it** (grep for `serverInteract:` in plugin source returns nothing). It is a dead extension point; the only tests that touch it (`views-routes.interact-result.test.ts`) explicitly exercise the *no-serverInteract* frontend round-trip path.

3. **Universal agent-surface** — `useAgentElement` (`packages/ui/src/agent-surface/useAgentElement.ts`, README in `packages/ui/src/agent-surface/README.md`) makes any view element addressable/clickable/fillable via generic capabilities (`list-elements`, `agent-click`, `agent-fill`, `agent-focus`, `get-agent-state`, …) handled generically in `packages/ui/src/agent-surface/capabilities.ts:29-107`. This is the **well-adopted** layer: 292 call sites across 33 plugins. The README explicitly tells authors *not* to add a `capabilities` array because these verbs are universal. Dispatch routes through `view-interact-registry.ts` (`packages/ui/src/components/views/view-interact-registry.ts`).

4. **Server-side action affinity** — `packages/agent/src/runtime/view-action-affinity.ts` keeps the active view's *runtime actions* at full param detail in the planner prompt. `VIEW_ACTION_MAP` (`:124-169`) hand-maps ~24 view ids → action names (e.g. `wallet → [WALLET, EVM_SWAP, …]`, `calendar → [CALENDAR, CONFLICT_DETECT]`). Active view set by `POST /api/views/:id/navigate`; element snapshot by `POST /api/views/:id/elements`.

**The concrete gaps:**

- **No 1:1 enforcement.** Nothing asserts that the buttons/forms in a view correspond to a registered view-dependent action or an agent-surface element. Example: `plugin-calendar` has ~25 `onClick`/`<button>` controls but only 13 `useAgentElement` registrations and no `ViewCapability`/`VIEW_ACTION_MAP` entry verification beyond the curated list. There is no tool that walks a view's interactive controls and confirms agent reachability.
- **`VIEW_ACTION_MAP` is incomplete and drift-prone.** `validateViewActionMap` (`view-action-affinity.ts:188-202`) only warns when a *mapped* action name no longer exists — it never flags a *declared view that is missing from the map entirely*. Views like documents, messages, phone, contacts, shopify-ui, screenshare, social-alpha, vincent, vector-browser, hyperscape, feed, the waifu apps, and defense-of-the-agents have no affinity entry, so their domain actions are not weighted when that view is foreground.
- **Slash commands have no view scoping.** `CommandDefinition` (`plugins/plugin-commands/src/types.ts:32-45`) has `scope`/`category` but **no `viewId`/view-availability field**. The `viewId` references in `plugins/plugin-commands/src/connector-catalog.ts:46,150` are *navigation routing hints* (open a view), not "this command is available because you are in this view." There is no notion of a view-dependent slash command.
- **e2e coverage is thin and synthetic.** The agent-surface e2e harness (`packages/ui/src/agent-surface/__e2e__/run-e2e.mjs`) bundles only `fixture.tsx` — a hand-written synthetic view — and never loads a real plugin view. `packages/agent/src/__tests__/view-user-journeys.ts` is a 3-scenario narrative library for LLM-judge eval (`PLUGIN_VIEW_LLM_MOCK_CASES` lists ~21 mock view paths but only checks navigation intent). `plugin-app-control/src/actions/views-management.test.ts` (38 cases) tests VIEWS-action mode/capability resolution in isolation. None of these drive a real view's controls end-to-end via the interact protocol and assert state change.
- **`STANDARD_CAPABILITIES` duplication.** Two overlapping "standard capability" sets exist: `packages/ui/src/views/view-interact-protocol.ts:31-40` (`get-state`, `refresh`, `focus-element`, `get-text`) and the agent-surface `AGENT_SURFACE_CAPABILITY_IDS` (`list-elements`, `agent-click`, …). The README notes the legacy caps still route through the registry; this should be reconciled and documented as one canonical set during the audit.

## Proposed work

1. **Define the contract.** Decide and document (in `packages/ui/src/agent-surface/README.md` + `plugins/plugin-app-control/CLAUDE.md`) the canonical rule: *every interactive control in a view must be reachable by the agent through exactly one of* (a) a universal agent-surface element (`useAgentElement`), (b) a declared `ViewCapability` + handler (frontend `interact` or `serverInteract`), or (c) a runtime action listed in `VIEW_ACTION_MAP` for that view id. Reconcile the two `STANDARD_CAPABILITIES` sets into one canonical list.

2. **Build a static audit (`view-capability-audit`).** A test/script that, for every `Plugin.views` declaration: enumerates the view's interactive controls (parse `onClick`/`onSubmit`/`<button>`/`<input>`/form handlers in the view component), enumerates its `useAgentElement` ids, its declared `ViewCapability` ids, and its `VIEW_ACTION_MAP` entry; then flags any control with no agent-reachable counterpart. Output a per-view coverage report (model the format on the cloud-frontend `manual-review/<slug>.md` convention). Fail CI on regressions for views that have reached `good`.

3. **Close the `VIEW_ACTION_MAP` completeness gap.** Extend `validateViewActionMap` (or a sibling `validateViewCoverage`) to warn/fail when a *registered view id* has neither a `VIEW_ACTION_MAP` entry, any declared `ViewCapability`, nor any reported agent-surface elements. Add the missing high-confidence view→action mappings (documents, messages, phone, contacts, shopify, etc.) verified against each plugin's `actions/` source.

4. **Add view-dependent slash commands.** Extend `CommandDefinition` (`plugins/plugin-commands/src/types.ts`) with an optional `views?: string[]` (or `surfaceScope`) field so a command can declare it is available while a given view is foreground. Surface those view-scoped commands through `getConnectorCommands`/the `/api/commands` catalog when the active view matches (active view already tracked in `view-action-affinity.ts`). For each view with domain capabilities, register a corresponding view-dependent slash command (e.g. `/calendar add`, `/todos done`) routed to the same action the in-view control invokes.

5. **Per-view e2e tests.** Generalize the agent-surface e2e harness (`packages/ui/src/agent-surface/__e2e__/run-e2e.mjs`) to mount each **real** plugin view bundle, then drive it purely through the interact bridge: `list-elements` → assert the declared/expected control set is present → exercise `agent-click`/`agent-fill`/declared `ViewCapability` → assert the resulting state change (and, where applicable, the corresponding `view:event` broadcast). Run one spec per view id; gate completion on every view reaching `good`. Add a deterministic (non-LLM-judge) layer to `view-user-journeys` that asserts the VIEWS action resolves the right capability for each view.

6. **Wire `serverInteract` or remove it.** Either implement `serverInteract` for views whose capabilities are headless-capable (so the agent can act without a mounted shell), or, if it stays unused, delete the dead type/route branch (`view-action-affinity` constraints + `views-routes.ts:959-991`) per the repo's "remove on sight" rule. Decide explicitly rather than leaving a permanent unused extension point.

7. **Relate to slash-commands + proactive-commentary.** Ensure the universal slash catalog (`/api/commands`) reflects view-dependent commands, and that the active-view awareness block (`renderActiveViewContextBlock`, `view-action-affinity.ts:210-253`) — which already feeds proactive context — lists the view-dependent slash commands alongside the interact capabilities so proactive commentary can suggest in-view actions.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A documented, single canonical contract states how an in-view control becomes agent-reachable, and the two `STANDARD_CAPABILITIES` sets are reconciled to one.
- [ ] A static audit enumerates, for every registered view id, its interactive controls vs. its agent-reachable surface (agent-surface elements + declared `ViewCapability` + `VIEW_ACTION_MAP`), and emits a per-view coverage report.
- [ ] `validateViewActionMap` (or a sibling) fails/warns when a registered view id has **no** agent-reachable actions/capabilities/elements — not only on renamed action names.
- [ ] Every plugin view either declares the relevant `ViewCapability`/`VIEW_ACTION_MAP` entry or has its controls registered via `useAgentElement`; the audit reports zero unreachable controls for views marked `good`.
- [ ] `CommandDefinition` supports view scoping, and every view with domain capabilities has at least one view-dependent slash command wired to the same action the in-view control invokes; these appear in `/api/commands` when the view is active.
- [ ] A per-view e2e spec mounts each real view bundle and drives at least one declared/expected capability end-to-end through `POST /api/views/:id/interact` (or the universal agent-surface bridge), asserting the resulting state change.
- [ ] `serverInteract` is either implemented for at least the headless-capable views or removed as dead code, with the decision documented.
- [ ] `bun run test:server` and the agent-surface e2e lane pass; the audit runs in CI and blocks regressions.

## Files & areas likely involved

- `packages/agent/src/runtime/view-action-affinity.ts` — `VIEW_ACTION_MAP`, `validateViewActionMap`, `renderActiveViewContextBlock`
- `packages/agent/src/api/views-routes.ts` — `/interact`, `/elements`, `/navigate`, `serverInteract` dispatch (`:896-1000`)
- `packages/ui/src/agent-surface/` — `useAgentElement.ts`, `capabilities.ts`, `registry.ts`, `types.ts`, `__e2e__/run-e2e.mjs`, `__e2e__/fixture.tsx`, `README.md`

## Open questions

- **Canonical reachability layer:** Should the audit *prefer* the universal agent-surface (`useAgentElement`) for all element-level control and reserve declared `ViewCapability` only for view-specific compound operations that can't be expressed as click/fill? The README currently discourages `capabilities` arrays — does that policy hold for headless/`serverInteract` cases?
- **`serverInteract` fate:** Keep and implement (enables agent action with no mounted shell — useful for connectors/headless), or remove as dead code? This is a product call about whether headless view actions are in scope.
- **Slash-command scoping shape:** Is a `views?: string[]` allowlist on `CommandDefinition` the right model, or should view-dependent commands be contributed dynamically by the plugin that owns the view (like the inline-widget registry)?
- **e2e cost/runtime:** Mounting every real view bundle in headless chromium per CI run may be slow. Acceptable to run the full per-view e2e on a nightly/post-merge lane and keep a fast static audit on every PR?
- **Definition of "control":** What counts as an in-view control the agent *must* reach — only primary actions, or every clickable/fillable? Cosmetic toggles (theme, collapse) probably should be excluded; the contract needs an explicit boundary.
2a:T3c2e,## Summary

The app dashboard renders ~30 built-in views (`packages/ui/src/components/pages/`) plus ~40 plugin view bundles, and a large minimal/light redesign has already landed (see `packages/ui/docs/minimal-redesign/`). But there is **no single production-quality gate that covers every view with an explicit per-view verdict** the way cloud-frontend's `audit:cloud` does. The existing app visual specs (`builtin-views-visual.spec.ts`, `plugin-views-visual.spec.ts`) only assert "mounts + renders >10 chars + no uncaught crash" for a *subset* of views — not iconography, layout, space use, floating-chat integration, or brand-color compliance. This issue proposes a per-view production-quality pass backed by an app-side audit/screenshot loop that walks **all** views and produces actionable `good` / `needs-work` / `broken` verdicts.

## Current state

**View inventory (grounded):**
- Built-in tabs are enumerated in `packages/ui/src/navigation/index.ts` — `BuiltinTab` union (lines 46-79) and `TAB_PATHS` (lines 277-311): `chat, phone, messages, contacts, camera, tasks, automations, triggers, browser, companion, stream, apps, views, character, character-select, inventory, documents, plugins, skills, fine-tuning, trajectories, relationships, memories, rolodex, voice, runtime, database, desktop, settings, tutorial, help, logs`.
- Built-in view components live in `packages/ui/src/components/pages/` (~30 `*View.tsx`), ranging from 15-line wrappers (`RelationshipsView.tsx`) to 2,990 lines (`BrowserWorkspaceView.tsx`). They are routed by the `ViewRouter` in `packages/ui/src/App.tsx` (1,876 lines).
- Plugin views are merged into the launcher via `packages/ui/src/hooks/useAvailableViews.ts` + `packages/ui/src/hooks/view-catalog.ts` (`mergeViewCatalog`), dynamically mounted by `packages/ui/src/components/views/DynamicViewLoader.tsx` (1,316 lines). ~40 plugin views exist (each `plugins/plugin-*/.../views`), enumerated in `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/plugin-view-cases.ts` (GUI + TUI variants).

**Floating-chat integration is the single chat surface — and is under-audited:**
- The floating chat is `ContinuousChatOverlay` (`packages/ui/src/components/shell/ContinuousChatOverlay.tsx`), mounted once at the shell root and floating over every view (`App.tsx:1238` `ContinuousChatOverlayMount`, comment at `App.tsx:802` / `App.tsx:1155`).
- `AppWorkspaceChrome` (`packages/ui/src/components/workspace/AppWorkspaceChrome.tsx`) still contains a page-scoped right-rail chat, **but its own comment (lines 444-447) states "every current caller passes chatDisabled so this content is not rendered"** — the in-chrome rail and `PageScopedChatPane` path are effectively dead for shipped views. This is exactly the "two paths to avoid a decision" the architecture rules forbid.
- Views opt into the floating composer via `useViewChatBinding` (`packages/ui/src/state/view-chat-binding.tsx`) — placeholder override + live-draft `onQuery`. Only a handful of views use it (Help is the documented example). There is **no audit** that (a) every view leaves bottom clearance for the overlay (the `--eliza-continuous-chat-clearance` padding is applied ad-hoc in `App.tsx:440/457`), or (b) views that should bind to the composer actually do.

**Existing design tooling (partial, fragmented):**
- `scripts/generate-view-heroes.mjs` generates brand-consistent 1024² hero SVGs, but only for **15** hand-listed views (lines 30-46); 48 `hero*.svg/png` exist across plugins, so coverage is incomplete and the per-view config is manual.
- `packages/app/test/view-screenshots/run.mjs` is a real screenshot+quality harness, but scoped to **only the 9 LifeOps decomposed views** (header comment, lines 1-19). It checks blank/one-color and writes a contact sheet — good bones, narrow scope.
- `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/builtin-views-visual.spec.ts` screenshots **only 10 of ~30 built-in tabs** (`BUILTIN_VIEW_CASES`, lines 23-34: views, settings, plugins, character, automations, memories, database, logs, camera, help) and asserts only "mounts, readable content >10 chars, no uncaught page error" (lines 13-21). Uncovered built-in surfaces include: **chat, phone, messages, contacts, tasks, browser, companion, stream, apps, character-select, inventory/wallet, documents, skills, fine-tuning, trajectories, relationships, runtime, voice, tutorial, desktop**.
- `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/plugin-views-visual.spec.ts` + `plugin-view-cases.ts` screenshot ~40 plugin GUI/TUI views and emit `.audit.json` interaction signals into `packages/app/aesthetic-audit-output/plugin-views-app/`, but again the gate is functional (no "Failed to load view"), not design-quality.
- Iconography is partially guarded by `packages/ui/src/components/pages/view-glyph-cleanup.test.ts` (asserts a few views use Lucide icon components, not raw `✓`/`✗` glyphs) — narrow, per-view, hand-maintained.

**The gold-standard pattern exists but only for cloud-frontend:** `packages/cloud-frontend/tests/e2e/aesthetic-audit.spec.ts` (run via `audit:cloud` in `packages/cloud-frontend/package.json:18`) walks every router path at desktop+mobile, captures rest + `--hover` screenshots, measures logo/nav/button colors, **flags border-radius and orange↔black / any-blue hover violations**, collects console errors, and **auto-stubs `manual-review/<slug>.md` with a `good`/`needs-work`/`broken` verdict** plus a `contact-sheet.html`. `packages/app` has **no equivalent** — its `aesthetic-audit-output/` is a stale partial dump with no verdict markdowns and no all-views walker.

**Brand-rule drift (real, citable):**
- Project rule (root `CLAUDE.md` / cloud-frontend `AGENTS.md`): **"No blue anywhere"**, orange accent only, orange-resting → darker-orange hover.
- But `packages/ui/docs/minimal-redesign/00-MASTER-PLAN.md:7` and `:66` still sanction **"blue `#1d91e8` (info/secondary only)"** — directly contradicting the no-blue rule and guiding future view work wrong.
- The default theme swatch ships blue: `packages/ui/src/themes/presets.ts:12` `preview: "linear-gradient(135deg, #1d91e8 0%, #47b8ff 58%, #ff8a24 100%)"` — visible blue in the theme picker.
- The redesign log (`99-REDESIGN-LOG.md`) already flags un-fixed brand violations (e.g. "blue `primary` in `ElizaOsAppsView`", green `#10b981`) and TODO controls that were "restyled not deleted pending test updates."

**Net:** the redesign is substantially done and verified for ~10 builtin + ~27 plugin views, but (1) ~20 built-in surfaces have no design-quality coverage, (2) there is no unified all-views verdict gate for the app, (3) floating-chat integration is unaudited and carries a dead second path, and (4) the brand spec itself still permits blue, so drift will recur.

## Proposed work

1. **Reconcile the brand spec to "no blue anywhere."** Update `packages/ui/docs/minimal-redesign/00-MASTER-PLAN.md` (lines 7, 66) to drop blue as an allowed info/secondary color and match the project rule. Replace the blue in the default theme picker swatch (`packages/ui/src/themes/presets.ts:12`) with an orange/neutral gradient. Audit `--brand-blue` / `#1d91e8` / `#47b8ff` usages across `packages/ui/src` and repoint info states to neutral or orange. Resolve the open violations the redesign log already names (`ElizaOsAppsView` blue primary, stray greens).

2. **Build an app-side all-views aesthetic audit harness** modeled on `packages/cloud-frontend/tests/e2e/aesthetic-audit.spec.ts`. Drive it off a single source of truth — the union of `TAB_PATHS` (`packages/ui/src/navigation/index.ts`) for built-ins and `plugin-view-cases.ts` for plugin views — so no view can be silently omitted. Reuse the existing seams: `openAppPath` / `seedAppStorage` / `installDefaultAppRoutes` (`packages/app/test/ui-smoke/helpers.ts`) and `captureScreenshotWithQualityRetry` (`helpers/screenshot-quality.ts`). For each view at desktop + mobile, capture rest + primary-button `--hover`, run the blank/one-color analyzer, detect hover-color violations (orange↔black / any blue) and non-token border-radius, collect console errors, and **auto-stub `manual-review/<slug>.md` with a `good`/`needs-work`/`broken` verdict** + a `contact-sheet.html`. Wire a `audit:app` script in `packages/app/package.json`.

3. **Add a floating-chat-integration assertion to the harness.** For every view: assert the `ContinuousChatOverlay` is present and not occluded, that the view applies the `--eliza-continuous-chat-clearance` bottom padding (no content hidden behind the composer), and capture a "chat-open over view" screenshot so the integration is regression-guarded per view.

4. **Remove the dead page-scoped chat path.** Either delete the `chat` / `chatScope` / `PageScopedChatPane` branch of `AppWorkspaceChrome` (lines 448-546) and the `chatDisabled` prop now that every caller disables it, or document a real remaining caller. Collapse to the single floating-overlay path (architecture rule: one obvious codepath).

5. **Run the per-view production-quality grind** (the 5-loop pass from the project AGENTS.md, applied to the app): for each `needs-work`/`broken` verdict, fix iconography (Lucide components via `IconTag`, no raw glyphs — extend the `view-glyph-cleanup` guard), layout (the shared `page-panel` / `page-layout` composites, no card-in-card, hairline dividers over boxes), space use (glanceable content, kill restating subtitles/eyebrow labels per the existing design laws), and brand colors. Prioritize the ~20 uncovered built-in views and the redesign log's deferred items (Runtime/Trajectories/Database dev views, comms GUI, ElizaOsAppsView).

6. **Generalize hero coverage.** Make `scripts/generate-view-heroes.mjs` derive its view list from the plugin manifest scan (the same `elizaos.app` source `useAvailableViews` reads) instead of the 15-entry hand list, so every cataloged view gets an on-brand hero and the `view-catalog` `hasHero` path is never an icon-only fallback by omission.

7. **Land the harness output and verdicts as a checked-in gate.** Commit screenshots + `manual-review/*.md` together (mirroring the cloud-frontend protocol), and make a `needs-work`/`broken` verdict block "done" for any UI change touching `packages/ui/src/components/pages` or a plugin view.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Every walked view has a checked-in `manual-review/<slug>.md` with a verdict; no view is `needs-work` or `broken` at close.
- [ ] The harness flags (and fails on) any blue color and any orange↔black hover transition, and any non-token border-radius, across all views.

## Open questions

- **Blue, definitively:** confirm "no blue anywhere" overrides the master-plan's "blue as info/secondary." If info states need a second hue, what is it (neutral gray vs. a desaturated orange)? This decides the token repoint in step 1.

2b:T3f97,## Summary

The LifeOps surface (`plugin-personal-assistant`, `plugin-health`, `plugin-scheduling`) already has a *substantial* but *unevenly distributed* test/benchmark substrate: ~155 `.scenario.ts` scenarios, a real-runtime scenario harness, a 1020-scenario Python `lifeops-bench`, and a working GEPA/DSPy optimizer in `plugin-training`. The problem is that these pieces are **disconnected**: the GEPA/`OptimizedPromptService` task taxonomy is generic-runtime-only (no LifeOps tasks exist), no LifeOps prompt actually loads an optimized prompt, the TS benchmark runner is orphaned (no test drives it), `plugin-health`/screentime has almost no scenario coverage, and the trajectory→optimization loop for LifeOps capabilities is unbuilt. This issue is to (a) close the per-capability scenario/benchmark gaps and (b) wire a real GEPA + trajectory-review + context-management optimization loop for each LifeOps capability.

## Current state

### What exists and works

- **Scenario harness** — `packages/scenario-runner` boots a real `AgentRuntime` on PGLite and drives `message` / `action` / `api` / `tick` turns with per-turn assertions, `responseJudge` (LLM-as-judge), final checks (`actionCalled`, `connectorDispatchOccurred`, `memoryWriteOccurred`, `approvalRequestExists`, …), deterministic fixture-LLM proxy (`SCENARIO_USE_LLM_PROXY=1`), and a native-export bridge to the training corpus (`packages/scenario-runner/src/native-export.ts`, `exportScenarioNativeJsonl`, `recordedTrajectoryToNativeRows`). README at `packages/scenario-runner/README.md`.
- **LifeOps scenarios** — `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/test/scenarios/` has **155** `.scenario.ts` files plus 23 JSON scenarios in `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/scenarios/`. Coverage is skewed: `grep` of `domain:` shows ~10 `tasks`, 6 `habits`, 3 `lifeops`, 3 `gmail`, 2 `calendar`, 1 `goals` — heavy on executive-assistant edge cases (estate/probate/payroll), thin on the core daily loop (calendar conflicts, reminder lifecycle, meeting prep, recaps, approvals). 94 test files total under `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/test/`.
- **Python benchmark** — `packages/benchmarks/lifeops-bench` is mature: ~492 static + 528 live scenarios across 10 domains (calendar/mail/messages/contacts/reminders/finance/travel/health/sleep/focus), hashable `LifeWorld` state, `state_hash` + `output_substring` + `pass@k` scoring (`eliza_lifeops_bench/scorer.py`), 4 swappable adapters (eliza/hermes/openclaw/cerebras-direct) + perfect/wrong oracles, 574 tests, and 7 CI workflows (`.github/workflows/lifeops-bench*.yml`, `cerebras-nightly.yml`).
- **GEPA optimizer** — `plugins/plugin-training/src/optimizers/gepa.ts` is a real reflective-Pareto GEPA implementation (feedback rewrite + token-compression + crossover). `OptimizedPromptService` (`packages/core/src/services/optimized-prompt.ts`) versions artifacts under `<stateDir>/optimized-prompts/<task>/` with `current`/`previous` symlinks and auto-loads at boot.
- **Trajectory logging** — `packages/agent/src/runtime/trajectory-storage.ts` + `trajectory-steps-writer.ts`; gated by `ELIZA_DISABLE_TRAJECTORY_LOGGING` (`packages/agent/src/runtime/trajectory-internals.ts`). lifeops-bench can re-ingest real on-disk trajectories via `eliza_lifeops_bench/ingest/trajectories.py` with a mandatory privacy filter.
- **Scheduled-task spine** — the runner is `plugins/plugin-scheduling/src/scheduled-task/runner.ts` (1216 lines), structural routing only (never inspects `promptInstructions`). E2E coverage exists: `scheduled-task-end-to-end.e2e.test.ts`, `reminder-review-job.real.e2e.test.ts`, `scheduler.integration.test.ts`.

### What is weak / missing / broken

1. **GEPA/OptimizedPrompt has zero LifeOps tasks.** `OptimizedPromptTask` (`packages/core/src/services/optimized-prompt.ts`) = `should_respond | action_planner | response | media_description | action_descriptions | autonomy | view_context`. `TrajectoryTrainingTask` (`plugins/plugin-training/src/core/trajectory-task-datasets.ts:18`) = `should_respond | context_routing | action_planner | response | media_description`. `loadBaselineForTask` in `plugins/plugin-training/src/core/training-orchestrator.ts` only switches on those generic tasks. **There is no `calendar_extract`, `schedule_plan`, `reminder_dispatch`, `health_checkin`, `meeting_prep`, `morning_brief`, or `inbox_triage` optimization task anywhere.** The optimizer can never improve a LifeOps prompt because LifeOps prompts are not registered as optimizable units.

2. **No LifeOps prompt consumes an optimized prompt.** `grep` for `OptimizedPrompt` / `getPrompt(` across `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src` and `plugins/plugin-health/src` returns **nothing**. The LifeOps LLM call sites (e.g. `src/lifeops/llm/extract-gmail-plan.ts`, the calendar/scheduling extractors, the 10 providers under `src/providers/`) build prompts inline and never route through `OptimizedPromptService`. Even if a LifeOps artifact existed, it would not be loaded.

3. **The TS LifeOps benchmark runner is orphaned.** `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/test/helpers/lifeops-prompt-benchmark-runner.ts` (exports `runLifeOpsPromptBenchmark`, `buildPromptBenchmarkReport`, `buildAxOptimizationRows`, with full token/cost accounting via `packages/core/src/features/trajectories/pricing.ts`) and its 15 cases in `lifeops-prompt-benchmark-cases.ts` are imported by **no `.test.ts`** — they are dead infrastructure. There is a real benchmarking apparatus that nothing runs.

4. **`plugin-health` / screentime is nearly untested at the behavior level.** `plugins/plugin-health/test/` has **9** files, all connector contract/real tests (oura/withings/fitbit/strava). Behavior coverage is a single `src/__tests__/smoke.test.ts` plus colocated unit tests (`actions/screen-time.test.ts`, `screen-time/*.test.ts`). **Zero `.scenario.ts` scenarios** for health/screentime/sleep/bedtime despite default packs existing (`src/default-packs/{bedtime,wake-up,sleep-recap}.ts`). The Python bench has health/sleep/focus domains but no TS scenario parity.

5. **Trajectory→optimization loop is not wired for LifeOps.** `ingest/trajectories.py` is imported only by `tests/test_privacy.py`. The scenario native-export tags rows with `task_type` (`native-export.ts:290`) but only for the generic task buckets; LifeOps trajectories are not tagged with a LifeOps task/domain, so they cannot be bucketed into a per-capability dataset for GEPA. `plugin-training` `collectTrajectoryExamplesByTask` only recognizes the 5 generic tasks.

6. **Context-management for LifeOps is ad-hoc.** Context compaction lives in core (`packages/core/src/runtime/planner-loop.ts`: `trajectory_token_budget`, compress-mode escape hatch). LifeOps injects context through 10 providers (`src/providers/lifeops.ts`, `pending-prompts.ts`, `recent-task-states.ts`, `cross-channel-context.ts`, `work-threads.ts`, `inbox-triage.ts`, `activity-profile.ts`, `health.ts`, `room-policy.ts`, `first-run.ts`) with no measured token budget, no per-provider relevance scoring, and no benchmark that asserts the provider payload stays within budget or that pruning a provider doesn't regress task accuracy.

7. **Stale docs.** `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/README.md` and `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` repeatedly cite `src/lifeops/scheduled-task/runner.ts` as the runner, but that file does not exist — the runner moved to `plugins/plugin-scheduling/src/scheduled-task/runner.ts`. `src/lifeops/scheduled-task/` now only has `scheduler.ts` (a thin `processDueScheduledTasks` wrapper), `service.ts`, `runtime-wiring.ts`, `index.ts`. Anyone following the docs lands on a missing file.

## Proposed work

Ordered, each step verifiable on its own.

1. **Define a LifeOps optimization task taxonomy.** Extend `OptimizedPromptTask` (`packages/core/src/services/optimized-prompt.ts`) and `TrajectoryTrainingTask` (`plugins/plugin-training/src/core/trajectory-task-datasets.ts`) with concrete LifeOps tasks: `calendar_extract`, `schedule_plan`, `reminder_dispatch`, `inbox_triage`, `meeting_prep`, `morning_brief`, `health_checkin`, `screentime_recap`. Add corresponding `TASK_FILE_NAMES` entries and `loadBaselineForTask` cases (baselines pulled from the live LifeOps prompt templates, not re-hardcoded).

2. **Route LifeOps LLM call sites through `OptimizedPromptService`.** For each task above, locate the inline prompt construction (start with `src/lifeops/llm/extract-gmail-plan.ts`, the calendar/scheduling extractors, and the providers feeding the planner) and make them consult `OptimizedPromptService.getPrompt(task)` with the inline template as the fallback baseline. Keep the fallback so absence of an artifact is a no-op, never a failure.

3. **Tag LifeOps trajectories with task/domain.** Ensure LifeOps planner/extractor LLM calls write `task_type` (+ a `domain` metadata field) into the trajectory so `collectTrajectoryExamplesByTask` can bucket real and scenario-exported rows into the new LifeOps datasets. Extend `recordedTrajectoryToNativeRows` / `exportScenarioNativeJsonl` to emit the LifeOps task buckets.

4. **Build per-capability scorers for GEPA.** `plugins/plugin-training/src/optimizers/scoring.ts` currently only has `scorePlannerAction` / `scoreViewSelection`. Add scorers for the LifeOps tasks — reuse the lifeops-bench semantics where possible: action-set match (via `packages/scenario-runner/src/action-families.ts` `actionsAreScenarioEquivalent`) and structured-field exact-match for extraction tasks (date/time/recurrence/recipient). For chat-shaped tasks, gate on the existing `responseJudge` rubric.

5. **Activate and CI-wire the orphaned TS benchmark runner.** Add a `.test.ts` (gated behind a live-provider key, in the `post-merge` lane) that drives `runLifeOpsPromptBenchmark` over the 15 cases (expand to cover all 8 tasks), asserts a minimum pass rate per slice, and emits the markdown/Ax-optimization report. Wire it into a workflow alongside the existing `lifeops-bench*.yml`.

6. **Author the missing scenarios.** Fill the thin domains in `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/test/scenarios/`: calendar conflict-detect + reschedule, reminder full lifecycle (fire→ack→snooze→complete→escalate), meeting prebrief, recap generation, approval queue happy/deny path. Add a **new** `plugins/plugin-health/test/scenarios/` directory with health/sleep/bedtime/screentime scenarios at parity with the Python bench's health/sleep/focus domains, driven through the same scenario-runner.

7. **Build the GEPA optimization loop per capability.** Add a script/CLI verb (under `plugin-training`) that: exports LifeOps scenario + real trajectories → per-task datasets → runs `gepa.ts` with the per-capability scorer (APOLLO for any training leg, per repo convention) → writes `OptimizedPromptArtifact` via `OptimizedPromptService.setPrompt(task, …)` → re-runs the benchmark to confirm a non-regression before promotion (reuse `plugins/plugin-training/src/core/promotion-gate.ts`).

8. **Context-management benchmark + budget.** Add a benchmark that measures the token payload of the 10 LifeOps providers per scenario and asserts a per-turn budget, and an ablation harness that drops each provider to quantify its accuracy contribution (feeds provider-prioritization / compaction decisions). Tie into the core `trajectory_token_budget` path so over-budget LifeOps context surfaces as a measured regression, not a silent truncation.

9. **Fix stale docs.** Update `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/README.md` + `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` to point at the real runner path `plugins/plugin-scheduling/src/scheduled-task/runner.ts`.
