## Open questions

- **Optimization target metric per task:** action-set match vs structured-field exact-match vs judge rubric — which is authoritative per LifeOps task for GEPA's Pareto scorer? (Extraction tasks want exact-match; chat tasks want judge.)
- **Single source of benchmark truth:** should the TS `lifeops-prompt-benchmark-runner` and the Python `lifeops-bench` converge (one corpus), or stay split (TS = optimization-loop scorer, Python = cross-model leaderboard)? They currently duplicate scenario intent.
- **Where does the GEPA loop run** — nightly CI (like `cerebras-nightly.yml`) writing artifacts to a store, or on-device `OptimizedPromptService` per user? The state-dir layout supports per-user; the benchmark gating suggests CI.
- **Privacy for real-trajectory training:** the Python ingest enforces a privacy filter; is there an equivalent before LifeOps real trajectories enter the TS GEPA dataset, or is GEPA scenario-only until that exists?
- **Default-on for optimized LifeOps prompts:** ship optimized artifacts enabled by default, or behind the Settings → Auto-Training panel that already drives `OptimizedPromptService.refresh()`?
2c:T4455,## Summary

The eliza-1 local-model line is built end-to-end on **Qwen3.5** (0.8B/2B/4B/9B) and **Qwen3.6** (27B) bases — the training registry, GGUF kernels, KV-cache quantization, MTP speculative-decode heads, end-of-turn detection, and abliteration step all assume the Qwen3.5 hybrid Gated-DeltaNet architecture and the 248,320-token tokenizer. The product owner wants us to (a) explore swapping eliza-1 to a **Gemma** base end-to-end and (b) chase **Apple- and Google-specific on-device optimizations**, comparing **tokens/sec, memory, and overhead** on Apple and Android (caring less about quality benchmarks). This issue scopes that exploration honestly: the data-gathering and Apple/Android optimization work is high-value and low-risk, but a literal "Gemma end-to-end" swap is a deep architectural fork, not a config change, and several frozen contracts in `plugins/plugin-local-inference/native/AGENTS.md` and `packages/training/AGENTS.md` would have to be re-opened. eliza-1 is the *recommended, not enforced* local model, so this can proceed as a parallel evaluation track without disrupting the shipped line.

## Current state

**Base model is Qwen, asserted as a frozen product contract.**
- `packages/training/scripts/training/model_registry.py` is the single source of truth. Every active entry maps a Qwen base to an eliza-1 tier: `Qwen/Qwen3.5-0.8B-Base` → `eliza-1-0_8b`, `Qwen/Qwen3.5-2B-Base` → `eliza-1-2b`, `Qwen/Qwen3.5-4B-Base` → `eliza-1-4b`, `Qwen/Qwen3.5-9B` → `eliza-1-9b`, `Qwen/Qwen3.6-27B` → `eliza-1-27b` (lines 251-471). `packages/training/AGENTS.md` §"Locked targets" says explicitly: "Do not edit configs to point at Qwen base names or OpenAI judges — the lock above is the product contract." `plugins/plugin-local-inference/native/AGENTS.md` §1 lists Qwen3.5/3.6 as backbones "do not change without explicit human approval."
- The catalog (`packages/shared/src/local-inference/catalog.ts`) stamps `tokenizerFamily: "qwen35"` on every chat tier (lines 640, 665) and references `qwen35.nextn_predict_layers` for the same-file MTP head (lines 470-472). `TokenizerFamily` is typed in `packages/shared/src/local-inference/types.ts:290` as `"qwen35" | "eliza1" | "sentencepiece" | (string & {})`.

**The architecture is Qwen-shaped at every layer below the catalog — this is the crux:**
1. **Hybrid linear-attention.** `model_registry.py` (lines 235-259) and `abliterate.py` (header note) document that Qwen3.5 is a 4-layer period of 3× Gated DeltaNet (linear/recurrent attention) + 1× Gated Attention (full softmax), so KV-bearing layers = `total_layers // 4`. The inference memory math in `_compute_inference_mem` and `packages/training/scripts/training/memory_calc.py` assumes this 3:1 ratio. The Vulkan kernels for it exist: `plugins/plugin-local-inference/native/omnivoice.cpp/ggml/src/ggml-vulkan/vulkan-shaders/{gated_delta_net.comp, ssm_conv.comp, ssm_scan.comp}`. **Gemma is a dense full-attention transformer with no linear-attention layers** — the entire KV-budget model, layer-count math, and these SSM kernels are inapplicable, and full-attention Gemma's KV cache is larger per token.
2. **Tokenizer/vocab is hard-wired.** `memory_calc.py:87` pins `vocab_size = 248_320` ("Qwen3.5/3.6 vocab"); the fp32 logits transient (`B*S*V*4`) drives training seq-len budgets. The MTP drafters must share the target's 248320 vocab (`MTP_DRAFTER_BASE`, lines 251-259). Gemma's tokenizer is ~256k SentencePiece — different special tokens, different vocab, different logits transient.
3. **End-of-turn detection reads the Qwen ChatML token.** The voice loop's EOT detector reads `P(<|im_end|>)` from next-token logits (`plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/eot-classifier-ggml.ts`, header + `LIVEKIT_IM_END_TOKEN = "<|im_end|>"` at line 116; also `eliza1-eot-scorer.ts`, `fused-eot-scorer.ts`). Gemma's chat template uses `<start_of_turn>`/`<end_of_turn>` — this EOT path would need re-derivation.
4. **Quantization + custom KV kernels assume Qwen geometry.** The shipped llama.cpp fork (`v1.2.0-eliza`, submodule at `plugins/plugin-local-inference/native/llama.cpp`) carries TurboQuant (turbo3/turbo4/turbo3_tcq), QJL K-cache (`block_qjl1_256`), and PolarQuant V-cache (`block_q4_polar`) kernels for Metal/Vulkan/CUDA/CPU. The catalog wires `kvCache: { typeK: "qjl1_256", typeV: "tbq3_0" }` (catalog.ts:498-502). These are validated against the Qwen attention shape; the publish gate (`packages/training/AGENTS.md` §6-8) requires 8/8 kernel verification against the bundle's quantized weights.
5. **MTP same-file head** is embedded as a NextN head in the Qwen3.5 GGUF (`qwen35.nextn_predict_layers`, catalog.ts:470-472, types.ts:259-274). Gemma has no equivalent published NextN head; speculative decoding would need a separately-trained drafter or a different spec-decode strategy.
6. **Abliteration** (`packages/training/scripts/training/abliterate.py`) monkey-patches Heretic to skip the Gated-DeltaNet writers and orthogonalize only the GA `o_proj` + dense `down_proj` — Qwen3.5-specific surgery.

**Apple / Google acceleration today — the relevant ground truth:**
- **Apple:** Metal is the on-device GPU path. `plugins/plugin-local-inference/native/metal/*.metal` ships the fused kernels; `darwin-arm64-metal` and `ios-arm64-metal` are verified-here (M4 Max, iPhone 15 Pro) per `plugins/plugin-local-inference/native/verify/PLATFORM_MATRIX.md` (lines 93-100). **MLX was explicitly removed** (commit `20d50d7553`); the in-process unblock plan is `plugins/plugin-local-inference/MLX_IN_PROCESS_PLAN.md`. The backend selector header (`src/services/backend-selector.ts:30-34`) states MLX, if ever needed, belongs compiled into the fused `libelizainference`, never as an HTTP sidecar. **Apple Foundation Models** is wired as an opportunistic fast-path only (`src/backends/apple-foundation.ts`) — Apple's own model, weights we don't own. **CoreML/ANE** is used for image-gen, not text (`src/services/imagegen/`, `coreml-unavailable.ts`); there is no ANE text path.
- **Google/Android:** GPU via Vulkan (Mali/Adreno) is the on-device accelerator (`android-arm64-vulkan`, `android-x86_64-vulkan`; build via `packages/app-core/scripts/aosp/compile-libllama.mjs`). PLATFORM_MATRIX.md (lines 133-152) records a **documented verdict against TPU/NPU**: NNAPI is deprecated, no public Pixel-Tensor delegate, Edge-TPU doesn't fit; the one open angle is a `ELIZA_VAD_QNN_DELEGATE` battery optimization for wake-word VAD on the Hexagon NPU. **There is no MediaPipe / LiteRT / TFLite path in the repo** (confirmed by grep — the only `gemini` matches are the unrelated cloud `plugin-google-genai` API provider). Gemma's first-party Google deployment story (MediaPipe LLM Inference / LiteRT-LM, `.task` bundles) is therefore entirely greenfield here and orthogonal to the existing llama.cpp-only single-runtime contract (`native/AGENTS.md` §11).

**Measurement harnesses already exist and capture exactly what's asked.**
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/native/verify/e2e_loop_bench.mjs` records `decodeTokPerSec` (line 706), `firstTokenMs`, `peakRssMb` (line 626), `voice_rtf`, and MTP acceptance per turn.
- `thirty_turn_endurance_harness.mjs` and `bargein_endurance_harness.test.mjs` cover RSS-over-time / leak detection.
- `llama-bench` (built per target) gives prompt + decode tokens/sec with cache-type flags. PLATFORM_MATRIX.md already has Qwen-base vs eliza-bundle `llama-bench` numbers on CUDA (lines 77). **These harnesses are model-agnostic enough to bench a Gemma GGUF immediately** for the tokens/sec + memory comparison the product owner wants, before any deeper integration.

**Net assessment:** "Switch eliza-1 to Gemma end-to-end" touches the training base, the tokenizer family, the KV-cache geometry + custom quant kernels, the MTP head, the EOT voice path, and the abliteration step — six frozen contracts. The *comparison* the owner actually prioritizes (tokens/sec, memory, overhead on Apple + Android) is achievable now with the existing benches against stock Gemma GGUFs and a stock-kernel llama.cpp, and that data is what should drive any decision to open the contracts. The Apple/Google optimization work is partly independent of the base-model choice and can land regardless.

## Proposed work

Ordered, exploratory-first. Treat phases 1-2 as the deliverable; phases 3+ are gated on phase-2 data and explicit human approval to touch frozen contracts.

1. **Baseline measurement harness (no integration).** Stand up a reproducible A/B bench: Qwen3.5 eliza-1 bundles (current) vs. stock Gemma GGUFs (e.g. Gemma 3 1B / 4B / 12B / 27B at matched quant levels) using the existing `native/verify/e2e_loop_bench.mjs`, `llama-bench`, and `thirty_turn_endurance_harness.mjs`. Capture **tokens/sec (prefill + decode), peak RSS, first-token latency, model+KV memory footprint, and load/arm overhead** at matched context lengths. Run on at least: Apple Silicon (M-series Metal + an iPhone via the existing iOS smoke path) and Android (Mali/Adreno Vulkan via the AOSP build). Land results as JSON evidence under `native/verify/reports/` alongside the existing reports.
2. **Write the comparison report + decision memo.** Produce a doc (e.g. `plugins/plugin-local-inference/docs/gemma-vs-qwen-eval.md`) that tabulates the phase-1 numbers and explicitly answers: where does Gemma win/lose on tokens/sec and memory per tier and per platform; what is the overhead delta of running stock-kernel Gemma vs. the fused Qwen kernels; and whether the gap (if any) justifies the integration cost in phases 3-5.
3. **Apple-specific optimization track (base-model-independent where possible).** Evaluate and, where they win, implement: (a) the in-process MLX backend per `MLX_IN_PROCESS_PLAN.md` (compiled into `libelizainference`, never a sidecar — honor `backend-selector.ts`), benched against the current Metal path; (b) widening the Apple Foundation Models fast-path (`src/backends/apple-foundation.ts`) usage envelope; (c) ANE feasibility for text (currently CoreML is image-gen-only). Each item ships with a `darwin-arm64-metal` / `ios-arm64-metal` bench delta.
4. **Google/Android-specific optimization track.** Evaluate: (a) Gemma's first-party **MediaPipe LLM Inference / LiteRT-LM** path as a *Gemma-only* alternative runtime, explicitly weighed against the `native/AGENTS.md` §11 single-runtime (llama.cpp-only) contract — this is the one place a Gemma base has a genuine first-party Google deployment advantage worth quantifying; (b) the documented `ELIZA_VAD_QNN_DELEGATE` Hexagon-NPU battery optimization for always-listening VAD (PLATFORM_MATRIX.md lines 147-150); (c) Vulkan kernel tuning for Adreno (currently only Mali fixtures are verified-here). Do **not** add a `plugin-coral`/`plugin-qnn` — the existing verdict stands unless data overturns it.
5. **(Gated on phase-2 approval) Gemma integration spike, one small tier only.** If phases 2-4 justify it, prototype `eliza-1-gemma-<tier>` as a *new, parallel, non-default* catalog entry (eliza-1 is recommended-not-enforced, so this need not replace the Qwen line): add a `gemma3` `TokenizerFamily`, add a Gemma `ModelEntry` to `model_registry.py`, run the APOLLO + Liger SFT pipeline (training must keep APOLLO per the lock), and produce a stock-kernel GGUF (no TurboQuant/QJL/Polar/MTP initially — those are Qwen-validated). Re-derive the EOT path for Gemma's `<end_of_turn>` token. Bench against the Qwen tier with phase-1 harnesses. Keep `defaultEligible: false` throughout.
6. **(Far-future, only if phase-5 wins decisively) Port the optimization kernels to Gemma geometry.** Re-validate TurboQuant/QJL/PolarQuant against Gemma's full-attention KV shape, train a Gemma MTP drafter, and re-baseline the publish gates. This is the actual "end-to-end" swap and must go through the full `native/AGENTS.md` §8 + `training/AGENTS.md` §6-8 publish-gate process with explicit human sign-off.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A reproducible A/B bench script/config exists that runs the existing `e2e_loop_bench.mjs` + `llama-bench` + endurance harness against both a current Qwen eliza-1 bundle and a stock Gemma GGUF, at matched quant + context.
- [ ] Tokens/sec (prefill + decode), peak RSS, model+KV memory, first-token latency, and arm/load overhead are captured as checked-in JSON evidence for at least Apple Silicon (Metal) and Android (Vulkan), with an iPhone and a physical Android device row where feasible.
- [ ] A written `gemma-vs-qwen-eval.md` report tabulates the results per tier/platform and gives an explicit recommendation on whether to pursue deeper Gemma integration.
- [ ] The report enumerates every frozen contract a real swap would re-open (training base lock, `tokenizerFamily`, KV-cache geometry, TurboQuant/QJL/Polar kernels, same-file MTP head, EOT `<|im_end|>` path, abliteration) and the cost of each.
- [ ] Apple track: at least one optimization (MLX in-process eval, Apple Foundation envelope, or ANE-text feasibility verdict) is benched with a Metal/iOS delta, or explicitly rejected with data.
- [ ] Google/Android track: a written verdict on MediaPipe/LiteRT-LM as a Gemma-only runtime vs. the single-runtime contract, plus a result for the QNN-VAD battery optimization or Adreno Vulkan tuning.
- [ ] No change to the shipped Qwen eliza-1 default line and no edit to the `packages/training/AGENTS.md` "Locked targets" without explicit human approval recorded in the issue; any Gemma catalog entry stays `defaultEligible: false` and non-default.
- [ ] eliza-1 naming/branding rules (`native/AGENTS.md` §9 — users never see "Qwen" or "Gemma") are preserved in any new surface.

## Open questions

- **Which Gemma family/sizes** are the target? Gemma 3 (1B/4B/12B/27B, multimodal) maps most naturally onto the eliza-1 size tiers; confirm before benching.
- **Is the goal to replace the Qwen line or add a parallel Gemma line?** eliza-1 is recommended-not-enforced, so a parallel `defaultEligible: false` Gemma tier is the low-risk path. A full replacement re-opens the "Locked targets" contract and the publish baselines for every tier.
- **Is the single-runtime (llama.cpp-only) contract negotiable for Android?** Gemma's strongest Google-specific advantage is MediaPipe LLM Inference / LiteRT-LM, which violates `native/AGENTS.md` §11. Do we evaluate it as a hard exception (Gemma-only second runtime) or stay llama.cpp-only and forgo that advantage?
- **Voice pipeline coupling:** OmniVoice/ASR/Kokoro are frozen and Qwen-ASR-based; the MTP↔TTS streaming handoff and EOT both assume Qwen tokens. Does "end-to-end Gemma" include re-deriving the voice loop, or is the scope text-only for the spike?
- **Quality tolerance:** the owner says "care less about benchmarks" — confirm there's no minimum quality gate for the spike so we can ship a stock-kernel Gemma GGUF and measure speed/memory without first matching Qwen's eval scores.
- **APOLLO + Liger compatibility with Gemma:** the training lock mandates APOLLO; confirm Gemma's architecture trains cleanly under the existing `run_pipeline.py` APOLLO + Liger fused-CE path (vocab/tokenizer differences aside).
2d:T3142,## Summary

The settings surface has two structural problems the product owner flagged. First, individual settings sections can crash the **entire** app shell: `SettingsView` renders each section component bare, and the only error boundary is a single top-level one in `App.tsx`, so one section's mount-time throw blanks the whole view ("many of the settings menus don't even load"). Second, a canonical, mobile-friendly control vocabulary already exists (`SettingsSelectRow`, `SettingsSegmentedGroup`, `SettingsRow` with 44px touch targets + agent-surface wiring), but at least six settings files bypass it and hand-roll native `<select>` elements — the "menu selectors whose options are a whole page away on mobile." A third, smaller problem lives in `cloud-frontend`, whose settings nav collapses to a native-feeling dropdown on mobile and hardcodes hex colors that violate the palette gate.

This is a consolidation + resilience task, not a rewrite: the good primitives are already in the tree and well-used by `RuntimeSettingsSection` and `ProviderSwitcher`.

## Current state

### In-app settings (packages/ui, consumed by packages/app)

- **No per-section error isolation.** `SettingsView` renders the active section as a bare `<Component />` inside `PagePanel` (`packages/ui/src/components/pages/SettingsView.tsx:206`, `:229-233`). There is no `ErrorBoundary` around it. The only boundary is the global one wrapping the entire view tree in `App.tsx:1067` (`<ErrorBoundary><LazyViewBoundary>{view}</LazyViewBoundary></ErrorBoundary>`). Net effect: if any section throws during render or a mount-time fetch rejects synchronously, the whole shell shows the global fallback instead of the offending section degrading gracefully while the nav rail stays usable. Many sections fetch on mount and are therefore exposed: `AppsManagementSection` (8 client calls), `CloudAgentsSection` (10), `CapabilitiesSection` (4), `VoiceSectionMount` (3), `PolicyControlsView` (3), etc. A dedicated regression spec already exists for exactly this failure mode — `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/settings-mobile-load.spec.ts` ("some settings pages just don't load because they have errors") — which asserts every section root `#<id>` becomes visible with no uncaught exception or error boundary at 390x844.

- **Native `<select>` proliferation (mobile-hostile).** A canonical Radix-based, agent-addressable, 44px-touch select already exists as `SettingsSelectRow` (`packages/ui/src/components/settings/settings-agent-rows.tsx:116`), backed by `SettingsSelectTrigger` / the `touch` variant in `packages/ui/src/components/ui/settings-controls.tsx:21-39`. A `SettingsSegmentedGroup` primitive also exists for small option sets (`settings-controls.tsx:113`). Despite this, settings sections hand-roll native `<select>`:
  - `VoiceSection.tsx:303` — "Local vs Cloud" with exactly 3 options (`auto` / `force-local` / `force-cloud`): a textbook segmented-control case rendered as a dropdown.
  - `RoutingMatrix.tsx:224` and `:253` (rendered inside the AI Model section) — policy + preferred-provider dropdowns, and they use the non-token class `text-muted-foreground` and `bg-bg/50` rather than the settings token vocabulary.
  - `AppsManagementSection.tsx:578` — "Based on existing app" picker over an unbounded `installed` list (long native picker on mobile).
  - `VoiceProfileSection.tsx:242` — per-profile relationship picker.
  - `vault-tabs/RoutingTab.tsx` — five native selects (`:320`, `:428`, `:452`, `:472`, `:495`).
  - `PolicyControlsView.tsx` — three native selects (`:984`, `:1010`, `:1049`).
  - `VaultInventoryPanel.tsx:1194` — one native select.
  
  Native `<select>` on iOS/Android opens the OS wheel/sheet, ignores the app theme, and gives no search for long lists — which is precisely the "options are a whole page away" complaint.

- **Inconsistency, not absence, is the disease.** `RuntimeSettingsSection` (card rows via `SettingsRow`), `ProviderSwitcher` (chip-cloud `ProviderCard`s), and `CapabilitiesSection` (uses `SettingsSelectRow`) demonstrate the intended pattern. The native-`<select>` files predate or ignore it. Roughly 6 settings files use native `<select>` vs ~4 using the canonical `SettingsSelectRow`.

### Cloud dashboard settings (packages/cloud-frontend)

- `packages/cloud-frontend/src/dashboard/settings/_components/settings-tabs.tsx` renders the **primary section nav** as a `Select` dropdown on mobile (`block md:hidden`, line 65-100) while desktop gets real tabs. The mobile dropdown also hardcodes hex/rgba colors: `bg-[rgba(0,0,0,0.4)]`, `bg-[#1A1A1A]`, `text-[#A2A2A2]`, `hover:bg-[rgba(255,255,255,0.07)]` (and the desktop tabs repeat `#A2A2A2` / `rgba(255,255,255,0.07)`). Per `packages/cloud-frontend/AGENTS.md` and `docs/HOVER_SYSTEM.md`, brand orange is accent-only, no blue, and palette/border-radius are audit-enforced — hardcoded hex bypasses the token system and the audit. The eight tabs (`general/account/connections/usage/billing/apis/analytics/organization`) reduced to a phone dropdown is the same far-away-options pattern as the in-app side.

### Shared error-boundary primitive

- `packages/ui/src/components/ui/error-boundary.tsx` is a clean, reusable class boundary with a `fallback` render-prop and reset, but it is currently only used at the App root and a few feature hosts (`WidgetHost`, `ConditionalWalletProviders`). Settings does not use it per-section. Its default fallback uses `text-destructive` / `bg-destructive/5` / `border-destructive`, which diverges from the warn-token vocabulary the rest of settings uses (`text-warn`, `bg-warn/12`); confirm `destructive` resolves to a theme token and is not introducing an off-palette red.

## Proposed work

Ordered, targeted scope. Reuse existing primitives; do not introduce a new control framework.

1. **Isolate every settings section behind its own error boundary.** In `SettingsView.tsx`, wrap the rendered `<Component />` (around line 229-233 in `SettingsSectionContent`, and the mobile/desktop content paths) in the existing `ErrorBoundary` with a per-section `fallback` that keeps the nav rail intact and offers retry. A crashing section must show an inline "this section failed to load — retry" card, never blank the shell. This is the single highest-impact fix for "menus don't load."

2. **Replace native `<select>` with the canonical control per case:**
   - Small, fixed option sets (≤ ~4) → `SettingsSegmentedGroup` (segmented control). Apply to `VoiceSection` "Local vs Cloud" (3 options) and `RoutingMatrix` policy where it has 5 fixed policies (segmented or `SettingsSelectRow` if 5 is too wide on mobile).
   - Larger / dynamic lists (installed apps, providers, profiles, vault routing targets) → `SettingsSelectRow` (Radix sheet-style select, themed, agent-addressable). Apply to `AppsManagementSection`, `VoiceProfileSection`, `RoutingMatrix` preferred-provider, `RoutingTab` (×5), `PolicyControlsView` (×3), `VaultInventoryPanel`.
   - Preserve the existing `useAgentElement` registration on each control (these are agent-addressable today via native `<select>` + `role: "select"`); `SettingsSelectRow` already wires `useAgentElement` internally, so migrate the agent id/label into its props and drop the duplicate hook.
   - Replace off-token classes (`text-muted-foreground`, raw `bg-bg/50`) introduced by `RoutingMatrix` with settings tokens.

3. **Cloud-frontend mobile settings nav.** Replace the mobile `Select` dropdown in `settings-tabs.tsx` with a horizontally scrollable / wrapping segmented or pill tab strip (consistent with desktop tabs), and remove all hardcoded hex/rgba in favor of theme tokens. Then run the required `audit:cloud` loop until every touched settings route is `good`.

4. **Audit the remaining sections for load failures.** Run `settings-mobile-load.spec.ts` against the current tree to enumerate which of the 16 built-in + `cloud-agents` + 11 cloud sections actually crash/blank today, and fix the root cause in each (most likely unguarded mount-time fetches or `?? `-free destructuring of an absent config). The per-section boundary from step 1 turns hard crashes into recoverable fallbacks; this step removes the underlying throws so the fallback is rarely hit.

5. **Lint guard (optional, recommended).** Add a Biome/grep guard (or extend an existing settings test) that fails CI if a new raw `<select>` appears under `packages/ui/src/components/settings/`, steering authors to `SettingsSelectRow`.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/settings-mobile-load.spec.ts` passes for all 16 built-in sections, `cloud-agents`, and all 11 cloud sections at 390x844 (no uncaught exceptions, no error-boundary fallback, section root visible).
- [ ] A deliberately-throwing section renders an inline per-section fallback with a working retry, and the settings nav rail / other sections remain interactive (no full-shell blank).
- [ ] No raw `<select>` remains in `packages/ui/src/components/settings/**` or `packages/ui/src/components/local-inference/RoutingMatrix.tsx`; each is replaced by `SettingsSelectRow` or `SettingsSegmentedGroup` with the original agent-surface id/label preserved.
- [ ] `VoiceSection` "Local vs Cloud" renders as a segmented control with all three options visible at phone width without opening an OS picker.
- [ ] `RoutingMatrix` uses settings theme tokens (no `text-muted-foreground` / raw `bg-bg/50`).
- [ ] `cloud-frontend` settings nav no longer collapses to a dropdown on mobile and contains no hardcoded hex/rgba colors; `audit:cloud` reports `good` for every touched settings route (no blue, orange accent-only, themed hover).
- [ ] `bun run --cwd packages/ui test` and `bun run --cwd packages/app test:e2e` (settings specs) pass; `bun run verify` is clean.

## Open questions

2e:T3bdb,## Summary

When the user fires a shortcut, runs a slash command, or switches views, the agent should be able to proactively comment — anticipating what the user wants and offering it — without the user typing anything. The runtime already has the *injection* half of this (`routeAutonomyTextToUser` → `proactive-message` WS event → chat append) and a proactive *cadence* reference (the LifeOps proactive worker), but there is **no event for these UI interactions, no pipeline that turns one into a scoped/rate-limited/dismissible comment, and — critically — client-initiated navigation never even reaches the agent**. This issue specifies the event → proactive-comment pipeline plus the UX rules (when to speak, frequency caps, dismissibility, text-vs-voice) and tests.

## Current state

### What exists

**1. A working proactive-message injection pipeline (server → chat).**
`routeAutonomyTextToUser` in `packages/agent/src/api/server-helpers-swarm.ts` (L121-189) persists an assistant memory and broadcasts `state.broadcastWs({ type: "proactive-message", conversationId, message })`. The client consumes it in `packages/ui/src/state/startup-phase-hydrate.ts` (L528-569): if the message belongs to the active conversation it is appended to `conversationMessages`; otherwise the conversation is flagged unread. The same event is surfaced on the activity rail (`packages/ui/src/hooks/useActivityEvents.ts` L146-163) and HomeScreen (`packages/ui/src/components/shell/HomeScreen.tsx` L146). The parser is `parseProactiveMessageEvent` (`packages/ui/src/state/parsers.ts` L263-271). **Today this path is only driven by the swarm coordinator / autonomy / coding-agent** — there is no caller for UI interactions.

**2. A proactive-cadence reference with real rate-limiting.**
`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/activity-profile/proactive-worker.ts` runs GM/GN/nudges on a 60s task. It demonstrates the gating we need: per-day `firedLog` dedup (`recordFiredAction`, L801-843), a `STALE_ACTION_THRESHOLD_MS` guard (L65, L503-509), an `isAppFirstRunComplete` gate, model-readiness checks, and the `ELIZA_DISABLE_PROACTIVE_AGENT` env kill-switch. Its proactive sources (`proactive-gm`, `proactive-gn`, `proactive-nudge`) are explicitly **chat-suppressed** in `CHAT_SUPPRESSED_AUTONOMY_SOURCES` (`server-helpers-swarm.ts` L80-86) and instead emitted as `agent_event` assistant streams. This is the canonical pattern for "speak, but quietly and capped."

**3. A quieter notification rail (alternative to chat).**
The `NOTIFY` action (`packages/agent/src/actions/notify.ts`) + `NotificationService` push structured, categorized, priority-tagged, group-collapsible notifications to the notification center + OS/in-app toasts — distinct from a chat reply. This is the right surface for low-confidence/low-urgency proactive offers that shouldn't interrupt the conversation.

**4. View-switch awareness — but only inside a message turn.**
`plugins/plugin-app-control` already reasons about views: `currentViewProvider` (`src/providers/current-view.ts`) injects the active view into every reply prompt by reading server-side `currentViewState` over loopback; `viewContextEvaluator` (`src/evaluators/view-context.ts`) is a *post-response* evaluator that proactively opens a view inferred from the user's situation; `viewCommandShortcutEvaluator` forces the VIEWS action on explicit commands. **All of these are triggered by a user message** — none fire on a standalone view-switch with no user turn.

**5. The server-side navigation seam.**
`POST /api/views/:id/navigate` (`packages/agent/src/api/views-routes.ts` L756-840) is the one place that records `currentViewState`, calls `setActiveViewContext(...)` to bias the planner, and broadcasts `shell:navigate:view`. This is the natural hook point for an agent-initiated switch to trigger a comment.

### What is missing / broken

- **No event type for these interactions.** `packages/core/src/types/events.ts` (`EventType` enum, L13-115) has no `VIEW_SWITCHED`, `SLASH_COMMAND_INVOKED`, or `SHORTCUT_FIRED`. The closest is `CONTROL_MESSAGE`. There is no `EventPayload`/`EventPayloadMap` entry for any of them, so a plugin cannot subscribe to "user just switched to the wallet view" the way it subscribes to `MESSAGE_RECEIVED`.

- **Client-initiated navigation never reaches the agent (the core gap).** Slash commands with a `navigate` target (`/settings`, `/views orchestrator`, `/wallet`) resolve to `navigate-view` / `navigate-tab` / `navigate-settings` and are executed **purely client-side** via `runSlashExecution` (`packages/ui/src/chat/slash-menu.ts` L305-343 + `useSlashCommandController.ts` `navigateView`/`navigateTab`/`navigateSettings`). They dispatch a `eliza:navigate:view` / `eliza:navigate:settings` window `CustomEvent`, handled locally by `createNavigateViewHandler` in `App.tsx` (L1488-1509). They do **not** `POST /api/views/:id/navigate`. Tab clicks (`setTab`) and HomeScreen tile taps (`App.tsx` L1269-1280) are likewise local-only. As a result `currentViewState` is stale and `currentViewProvider` reports the wrong view after any client-side switch — the agent literally doesn't know it happened. Only `agent`-target slash commands send text to the agent.

- **No interaction → proactive-comment pipeline.** Nothing maps a (view-switch | slash | shortcut) event to a scoped, model-judged "what does the user want here" comment routed through `routeAutonomyTextToUser`.

- **No UX governance for interaction-triggered comments.** The existing `proactive-message` path has no frequency cap, no per-surface cooldown, no "stay silent on low confidence" gate, no dedup against an identical recent comment, and no dismissibility for interaction-driven comments specifically. Without these, commenting on every view switch / slash command would be exactly the "annoying" outcome the product owner warned against.

- **No visual/voice distinction.** A `proactive-message` renders as an ordinary assistant bubble (`startup-phase-hydrate.ts` L536-539 just pushes it into `conversationMessages`); it is not labeled "proactive," is not one-tap dismissible, and there is no gating to keep it text-only (proactive comments should not auto-speak via TTS unless the user is in hands-free voice mode).

## Proposed work

**A. Add first-class interaction events (core).**
In `packages/core/src/types/events.ts` add `EventType.VIEW_SWITCHED`, `EventType.SLASH_COMMAND_INVOKED`, `EventType.SHORTCUT_FIRED` with payload interfaces (extend `EventPayload`): view id/label/path/viewType + `initiatedBy: "agent" | "user"` + previous view; command native name + args + resolved target kind; shortcut id + context. Wire them into `EventPayloadMap`. These are the connect-once contract every surface emits.

**B. Close the client→agent reporting gap (UI + agent API).**
Make client-initiated navigation observable to the agent. Either (preferred) have `createNavigateViewHandler` (`App.tsx`) and the slash `navigate-*` executions call a lightweight `POST /api/views/:id/navigate` (or a new `POST /api/interactions/observe`) so `currentViewState`/`setActiveViewContext` stay correct and the server emits the new event; or add a dedicated thin endpoint that only records state + emits the event without re-broadcasting `shell:navigate:view` (to avoid a navigation echo loop). This is a prerequisite — the agent cannot comment on an interaction it never sees. Respect AGENTS.md rule 10 (every endpoint has a real caller) and keep the BFF as auth+proxy only.

**C. Build the proactive-comment decider (new service/evaluator in plugin-app-control or a new plugin-proactive).**
A handler subscribes to the three events and decides whether to comment. Reuse the `viewContextEvaluator` model-judgment shape (small model, JSON output `{ comment | none, reason }`, GEPA-optimizable instruction via `resolveOptimizedPromptForRuntime`). On a positive decision, route the text through `routeAutonomyTextToUser(state, text, "proactive-interaction")` so it reuses the proven `proactive-message` WS pipeline. Context for the prompt: the new active view, recent messages, and any pending tasks for that surface.

**D. UX governance layer (the "not annoying" rules).**
Implement, modeled on `proactive-worker.ts`'s `firedLog`/staleness pattern but per-interaction:
- **When to speak:** only when the model is confident a *specific* offer helps (e.g. switched to wallet → "want me to pull your latest balances?"; opened task-coordinator → "want me to summarize open tasks?"). Default to silence on ambiguous/low-value switches, repeat switches, and rapid back-and-forth.
- **Frequency caps:** a global cooldown (e.g. ≥1 proactive interaction-comment per N minutes), a per-surface cooldown (don't re-comment on the same view within a window), and a hard daily cap.
- **Dedup:** suppress a comment textually equivalent to a recent one (same surface, same offer).
- **Debounce:** coalesce a burst of switches; comment only on the settled view.
- **Quiet by default for power use:** suppress entirely while a turn is streaming, while the user is typing, or during obviously deliberate navigation (e.g. slash command that itself already expressed intent).
- **Kill-switch + setting:** an env flag mirroring `ELIZA_DISABLE_PROACTIVE_AGENT` and a user-facing toggle ("Proactive suggestions: off / subtle / chatty").
- **Surface routing:** high-confidence, conversation-relevant → chat (`proactive-message`); low-urgency/low-confidence → `NOTIFY` rail (`notifyAction`) so it doesn't interrupt.

**E. Chat UX for the comment itself (UI).**
Tag interaction-driven proactive messages (`source: "proactive-interaction"`) and render them visually distinct (subtle "suggestion" affordance, not a normal reply) with a one-tap **dismiss** and optional **"yes, do it"** accept that sends the implied action. Make them **text-only by default** — do not feed them to TTS unless the user is already in hands-free voice mode (check the voice/dictation state in `packages/ui/src/voice` / shell overlay). Ensure dismiss removes the bubble locally and records suppression so it isn't re-offered immediately.

**F. Tests.**
- Core: event emission + payload typing for the three new events.
- Agent: client-navigation reporting hits the endpoint and emits the event; decider routes through `routeAutonomyTextToUser` and is suppressed for `proactive-interaction` only when caps are hit.
- Decider unit tests: cooldown, daily cap, dedup, debounce, low-confidence → silence, low-urgency → NOTIFY.
- UI: `proactive-message` of source `proactive-interaction` renders the suggestion affordance, is dismissible, does NOT auto-speak outside voice mode, and accept sends the implied action. Reuse the existing `startup-phase-hydrate` and `useActivityEvents` test harnesses.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] `EventType.VIEW_SWITCHED`, `SLASH_COMMAND_INVOKED`, `SHORTCUT_FIRED` exist with typed payloads in `packages/core/src/types/events.ts` and `EventPayloadMap`.
- [ ] Client-initiated view/tab/settings navigation (slash `navigate` targets, tab clicks, HomeScreen tiles) is reported to the agent so `currentViewState`/`setActiveViewContext` are accurate and the new event fires; `currentViewProvider` reflects client-side switches.
- [ ] A decider consumes the three events and, on a confident decision, emits a scoped proactive comment through the existing `routeAutonomyTextToUser` → `proactive-message` pipeline with `source: "proactive-interaction"`.
- [ ] Frequency caps enforced: global cooldown, per-surface cooldown, daily cap, textual dedup, and burst debounce — all unit-tested.
- [ ] Low-confidence/ambiguous interactions produce **no** comment; low-urgency offers route to `NOTIFY` instead of chat.
- [ ] A kill-switch env flag and a user-facing "Proactive suggestions" setting (off/subtle/chatty) both gate the behavior.
- [ ] In chat, an interaction-driven proactive message is visually distinct, one-tap dismissible, optionally acceptable, and **not** spoken via TTS unless the user is in hands-free voice mode.
- [ ] No regression: the existing swarm/coordinator/autonomy `proactive-message` path is unchanged; commenting does not fire during active streaming/typing.
- [ ] `bun run verify` and the new tests pass; cloud-frontend visual review is N/A (changes are in the agent app, not `cloud-frontend`) or completed if any shared UI bleeds in.

## Files & areas likely involved

- `packages/core/src/types/events.ts` — new `EventType`s + payloads + `EventPayloadMap`.
- `packages/agent/src/api/views-routes.ts` — `POST /api/views/:id/navigate` seam (L756-840); emit interaction event; possibly a new thin observe endpoint.
- `packages/agent/src/api/server-helpers-swarm.ts` — `routeAutonomyTextToUser` (L121-189), `CHAT_SUPPRESSED_AUTONOMY_SOURCES` (L80-86); add `proactive-interaction` source handling.
- `packages/agent/src/actions/notify.ts` + core `NotificationService` — low-urgency routing surface.
- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/evaluators/view-context.ts` — model-judgment shape to reuse; `src/providers/current-view.ts`; new decider service/evaluator + `src/index.ts` registration.
- `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/activity-profile/proactive-worker.ts` — rate-limit/dedup/staleness/kill-switch reference (`firedLog`, `STALE_ACTION_THRESHOLD_MS`, `ELIZA_DISABLE_PROACTIVE_AGENT`).
- `packages/ui/src/chat/slash-menu.ts` (L228-343) + `packages/ui/src/chat/useSlashCommandController.ts` — navigate-target execution that must report to the agent.
- `packages/ui/src/App.tsx` (L1269-1280, L1488-1509) — client navigation handlers (tabs, tiles, `createNavigateViewHandler`).
- `packages/ui/src/state/startup-phase-hydrate.ts` (L528-569) + `packages/ui/src/state/parsers.ts` (L263-271) — `proactive-message` ingestion; add `source` tagging / suggestion affordance.
- `packages/ui/src/hooks/useActivityEvents.ts` (L146-163), `packages/ui/src/components/shell/HomeScreen.tsx` — proactive activity surfacing.
- `packages/ui/src/voice/*` and the shell overlay — voice-mode gate so proactive comments stay text-only by default.
- New tests alongside the above (core event tests, agent decider tests, UI render/dismiss tests).

## Open questions

- **Default chattiness:** should proactive interaction-comments default to off, subtle, or chatty for new users? (Recommend "subtle" with an easy in-chat dismiss that teaches the cap.)
- **Which interactions are in-scope for *chat* comments vs *notification-rail* offers?** e.g. switching to wallet (clear intent) warrants a chat offer, but a `/clear-chat` client command almost certainly should stay silent. Need a per-target policy.
- **Should slash commands the user explicitly typed (already an expressed intent) ever trigger an additional comment, or only view switches / shortcuts?** Risk of double-talk.
- **Cap tuning:** concrete numbers for global cooldown / per-surface cooldown / daily cap (and whether they scale with the chatty/subtle setting).
- **Mobile/desktop parity:** the navigation seams differ (HomeScreen tiles, desktop tabs, Electrobun windows). Do we report all of them, or start with the in-app chat surface only?
- **Voice mode:** when the user *is* hands-free, should proactive comments be spoken, or should voice raise the bar (only speak the highest-confidence offers)?
2f:T3a76,## Summary

A large fraction of agent turns are deterministic intents — "open settings", "stop", "new chat", "show status", "read this aloud" — that the runtime currently resolves with a full Stage-1 LLM inference pass (`ModelType.RESPONSE_HANDLER`) plus, often, a second planner pass. We should add a **shortcut registry**: a plugin-registrable list of pattern/grammar-matched intents that resolve to a concrete action (or a client navigation) **before** the first model call, short-circuiting inference when a match is confident. The matcher is a pre-send-to-LLM evaluator that runs identically on typed text and on voice/ASR transcripts (a transcript is just a `Memory.content.text`), and it should reuse the "old-school" parsing infrastructure that already exists in `@elizaos/plugin-commands` (alias/regex/grammar matching) rather than inventing a new one. This cuts latency and token cost for the most common turns and makes routing for those intents deterministic and testable instead of probabilistic.

## Current state

**There is no pre-LLM deterministic action short-circuit today.** Every non-fast-path turn enters Stage 1 via `runV5MessageRuntimeStage1` (`packages/core/src/services/message.ts:5422`), which builds a structured `HANDLE_RESPONSE` envelope + GBNF grammar and calls `runtime.useModel(ModelType.RESPONSE_HANDLER, …)` at `packages/core/src/services/message.ts:5713`. Only the model decides `shouldRespond`, `contexts`, and `candidateActionNames`. The output is parsed/routed by `parseMessageHandlerOutput` / `routeMessageHandlerOutput` (`packages/core/src/runtime/message-handler.ts:49,197`).

**The only existing "shortcut" is itself LLM-classified.** `message-handler.ts` defines `SIMPLE_CONTEXT_ID = "simple"` (line 38) — when the *model* routes a turn to `simple`, the runtime emits `replyText` without the planner. That is a planner-skip, not an inference-skip. Likewise `shouldUseDirectReplyFastPath` (`packages/core/src/services/message.ts:4080`) only skips the HANDLE_RESPONSE scaffold; it still issues a model call via `generateDirectReplyModel`. Neither bypasses the LLM.

**Action selection is LLM-driven, with keyword retrieval only as a ranking aid.** `packages/core/src/runtime/action-retrieval.ts` runs a multi-stage funnel (`exact | regex | keyword | bm25 | embedding | contextMatch`, RRF-fused) — but its header docstring is explicit that it is *retrieval/ranking only*, producing candidate actions surfaced to the planner, never a hard availability/execution decision (`packages/core/src/i18n/action-search-keywords.ts:1-7`).

**Deterministic intent detection already exists — but it is scattered, monolithic, and only emits hints.** `inferDirectCurrentRequestCandidateActions` (`packages/core/src/services/message.ts:4043`) maps text to a candidate action name via a wall of hand-written regexes: `looksLikeLocalShellRequest` (line 7451), `looksLikeWebSearchRequest` (line 7536), `looksLikeCodingWorkRequest` (line 7565), `findViewShellActionName` (line 4329), `findViewCapabilityActionName` (line 4362), plus the giant negative regex inside `shouldUseDirectReplyFastPath` (lines 4095-4100). These are real "shortcut" logic in spirit, but: (a) they are hardcoded in a single 11.5k-line file, not a registry; (b) plugins cannot contribute patterns for their own actions; (c) the result is a *candidate hint passed to the LLM*, not a short-circuit that fires the action. This is exactly the duplication/sprawl the matcher should replace.

**A clean grammar/registry primitive already exists for the slash-command surface.** `@elizaos/plugin-commands` provides `CommandDefinition` (`plugins/plugin-commands/src/types.ts:32`) with `textAliases`, args, `argsParsing`, auth flags; a per-runtime registry (`registry.ts`: `registerCommand`, `findCommandByAlias`, `startsWithCommand`); and an "old-school" parser (`parser.ts`: `detectCommand`, `parseCommand`, tokenizer respecting quotes, colon-arg syntax). But it **only fires on `/` or `!` prefixes** (`parser.ts:21,36`) and registers no actions itself — it supplies definitions + a `COMMAND_REGISTRY` provider that injects command docs into the LLM prompt (`plugins/plugin-commands/src/index.ts:61`). Natural-language phrasing ("open settings", no slash) never hits it.

**The client already short-circuits slash commands, proving the target UX exists for navigation.** `ConnectorCommandTarget` (`plugins/plugin-commands/src/connector-catalog.ts:40`) discriminates `agent | navigate | client`; the GUI controller `useSlashCommandController.ts` runs `navigate`/`client` targets locally with no agent round-trip (e.g. `/settings` → `NAVIGATE_SETTINGS_EVENT`, `/clear` → `clear-chat`). Agent-target commands reconstruct text and route through the full pipeline. So "open settings" via `/settings` is already instant — but the same intent typed or *spoken* in natural language is not.

**No grammar/intent DSL exists.** A repo-wide search for `peg`, `nearley`, `chevrotain`, `ohm-js`, `AIML`, `GLR` returns nothing. The only "grammar" in the codebase is **GBNF used to constrain LLM output** (`packages/core/src/runtime/response-grammar.ts`), which is the opposite direction (forcing model tokens, not matching user input). So "explore old-school languages" is greenfield: there is no pattern-grammar matcher to build on except the alias/regex matching in plugin-commands.

**Voice already lands as plain text before routing.** ASR transcripts flow through `plugins/plugin-local-inference` (`routes/local-inference-asr-route.ts`) and the UI voice capture (`packages/ui/src/voice/voice-capture-factory.ts`) and become a normal `Memory.content.text`. So a matcher placed in the message pipeline covers voice automatically — *provided* it does not assume a `/` prefix and tolerates ASR noise (no punctuation, "open settings please", homophones).

**Mechanism to fire an action directly already exists.** `runtime.runActionsByMode` (`packages/core/src/runtime.ts:2994`) validates and executes actions with `EventType.ACTION_STARTED` lifecycle events and a callback — i.e. the runtime can already invoke an action's `handler` without going through the planner, given a chosen action name.

## Proposed work

1. **Define the shortcut contract (core).** Add a `ShortcutDefinition` type and a per-runtime `ShortcutRegistry` (mirror `plugin-commands`' per-`agentId` store pattern). A shortcut declares: an `id`; a `pattern` (start with the "old-school" tier: exact aliases + word-boundary regex + a small PEG/EBNF-style template grammar for slots, e.g. `open {section}`); the resolved `target` (`{ kind: "action"; name; parameters? }` or reuse `ConnectorCommandTarget` `navigate`/`client` for client-resolved intents); an optional `requiresContext`/`view` gate; a `confidence`/priority; and auth flags reusing `requiresAuth`/`requiresElevated` semantics. Co-locate it with or adjacent to `CommandDefinition` so a single registration can power both `/settings` and natural-language "open settings".

2. **Build the matcher (core), grammar-first.** Implement `matchShortcut(text, { actions, view, role })` returning `{ shortcut, parameters, confidence } | null`. Tier the matching old-school style: (a) normalized exact-alias lookup (reuse `startsWithCommand`/`findCommandByAlias` machinery, but prefix-optional); (b) anchored regex with named capture groups for slots; (c) an optional lightweight PEG/EBNF grammar layer for slot extraction (`open <section>`, `set thinking to <level>`). Require a confidence floor and refuse on ambiguity (multiple high-confidence matches → fall through to the LLM, never guess). Add ASR-tolerant normalization (strip filler/punctuation, lowercase, collapse whitespace).

3. **Place the pre-LLM gate.** Insert the matcher at the top of `runV5MessageRuntimeStage1` (`packages/core/src/services/message.ts:5478`, before the `directMessageChannel`/`useModel` block) and/or as a `MESSAGE_RECEIVED` pre-handler. On confident match for an `action` target, build the action invocation and execute via `runtime.runActionsByMode` (or a focused direct-invoke path), then return a `V5MessageRuntimeStage1Result` shaped like the existing `direct_reply` kind — **emitting zero `RESPONSE_HANDLER` tokens**. On a `navigate`/`client` target reachable from the connector, emit the client/navigation event the slash path already uses. On no match, proceed unchanged. Gate the whole thing behind a flag (default conservative) and a per-action opt-in so noisy actions never shortcut.

4. **Migrate the scattered heuristics into the registry.** Move `looksLikeLocalShellRequest` / `looksLikeWebSearchRequest` / `findViewShellActionName` / `findViewCapabilityActionName` and the `shouldUseDirectReplyFastPath` negative-regex into shortcut definitions (or shortcut-adjacent providers), collapsing the duplicated regex sprawl in `message.ts` into declarative registrations. Where today they only *hint*, decide per-pattern whether the match is confident enough to short-circuit vs. continue to hint the planner.

5. **Wire the plugin registration API.** Expose `registerShortcut`/`registerShortcuts` (parallel to `registerCommand`) and let a `Plugin` declare `shortcuts: ShortcutDefinition[]` so e.g. the settings/views plugins own "open settings" and the personal-assistant plugin owns "remind me…". Document that shortcut targets must name a real registered action and respect the same context/view gating actions already use (`Action.contexts`, `contextGate`).

6. **Voice path validation.** Ensure ASR transcripts hit the same gate (they already become `content.text`); add normalization for missing punctuation and an optional wake-word/leading-filler strip. No separate voice code path — the matcher must be source-agnostic.

7. **Optimization hook (optional, follow-on).** Because matches/misses are observable, log shortcut hit/miss/ambiguity into the existing trajectory recorder so the pattern set can later be tuned by the DSPy/GEPA artifacts under `~/.local/state/milady/optimized-prompts/<task>/` — the matcher stays deterministic at runtime; optimization only curates the pattern catalog offline.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A `ShortcutDefinition` type + per-`agentId` `ShortcutRegistry` exist in core (or alongside `plugin-commands`), with `registerShortcut(s)` and a `Plugin.shortcuts` declaration path.
- [ ] `matchShortcut()` resolves "open settings" (and slash-free variants) to a concrete action/navigation target with a confidence score, supports slot extraction via the grammar tier, and returns `null` on ambiguity or low confidence.
- [ ] On a confident `action`-target match, the turn executes the action and replies with **zero `RESPONSE_HANDLER`/planner model calls** (verified via trajectory: no `ModelType.RESPONSE_HANDLER` span for that turn).
- [ ] `navigate`/`client` shortcuts resolve through the same client side-effect path the slash menu already uses (no agent round-trip).
- [ ] The matcher runs identically on a typed message and on an ASR transcript; an ASR-style input ("open settings please", no punctuation) matches.
- [ ] At least the shell/web-search/view heuristics currently inlined in `message.ts` are migrated to declarative shortcut definitions, and the corresponding hand-written regex blocks are removed/reduced.
- [ ] A per-action opt-in and a global feature flag gate shortcutting; with the flag off, behavior is byte-identical to today.
- [ ] Ambiguous, low-confidence, or auth-failing inputs fall through to the existing LLM pipeline (no regressions, no silent misfires).
- [ ] Unit tests cover exact/regex/grammar tiers, ambiguity refusal, slot extraction, voice-normalized input, and auth gating; an integration test asserts the no-inference path end-to-end.

## Files & areas likely involved

- `packages/core/src/services/message.ts` — `runV5MessageRuntimeStage1` (L5422), the `RESPONSE_HANDLER` call (L5713), `shouldUseDirectReplyFastPath` (L4080), `inferDirectCurrentRequestCandidateActions` (L4043), `looksLikeLocalShellRequest` (L7451), `looksLikeWebSearchRequest` (L7536), `findViewShellActionName`/`findViewCapabilityActionName` (L4329/L4362)
- `packages/core/src/runtime/message-handler.ts` — `SIMPLE_CONTEXT_ID`, `routeMessageHandlerOutput`; add a `shortcut`/`direct_action` route shape
- `packages/core/src/runtime.ts` — `runActionsByMode` (L2994) as the direct-fire mechanism
- `packages/core/src/runtime/action-retrieval.ts`, `packages/core/src/i18n/action-search-keywords.ts` — existing keyword/regex retrieval to reuse for the matcher's tiers
- `packages/core/src/types/components.ts` — `Action` type (L268); add per-action shortcut opt-in / pattern field if shortcuts live on the action
- `plugins/plugin-commands/src/{types,registry,parser,connector-catalog,index}.ts` — `CommandDefinition`, the per-runtime store, the alias/quote/colon parser, and `ConnectorCommandTarget` to extend/share with shortcuts
- `packages/ui/src/chat/useSlashCommandController.ts`, `packages/ui/src/chat/slash-menu.ts` — client `navigate`/`client` execution to reuse for natural-language navigation shortcuts
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/routes/local-inference-asr-route.ts`, `packages/ui/src/voice/voice-capture-factory.ts` — voice/ASR entry, to confirm transcripts reach the same gate
- `packages/core/src/runtime/trajectory-recorder.ts` — to log shortcut hit/miss for offline optimization

## Open questions

- **Default-on or default-off?** Shortcutting changes routing determinism for real users; should it ship behind a flag (default off) until the pattern catalog is curated, or default-on for a fixed safe seed set (settings/stop/new/status only)?
- **Where do shortcuts live — on the `Action`, in a separate `ShortcutRegistry`, or merged into `CommandDefinition`?** Merging with `plugin-commands` gives one declaration for `/settings` + "open settings" but couples two surfaces; a separate registry is cleaner but duplicates registration.
- **Grammar engine choice.** Hand-rolled alias+regex+template is zero-dep and matches the existing `parser.ts` style; a real PEG (peggy/nearley) buys slot grammars but adds a dependency and a build step. How far do we go on "old-school languages" — is a tiny EBNF/template DSL enough, or do we want a full PEG?
- **Confidence threshold + ambiguity policy.** What confidence floor short-circuits vs. only hints the planner, and is "two near-ties → always defer to LLM" acceptable, or do we want a deterministic tiebreak by action priority?
- **Voice false-positive risk.** ASR mishears ("open settings" vs "open sentence") could misfire destructive actions. Do mutating/destructive shortcuts require a confirmation step, or are shortcuts read/navigation-only by policy?
- **Localization.** The matcher must work in the locales the keyword data already covers (`packages/shared/src/i18n/keywords/*.json`); do shortcut patterns get localized term sources like the action-search keywords, or English-first to start?

30:T40e2,## Summary

A universal slash-command system exists and is partially wired: `@elizaos/plugin-commands` is the catalog source of truth, `GET /api/commands` serves it, the web composer (`SlashCommandMenu` / `useSlashCommandController`), the TUI autocomplete, Discord native application commands, and Telegram `setMyCommands` + `bot.command` handlers all consume it. **But the system is incomplete in three load-bearing ways:** (1) **no action handlers exist** — `agent`-target commands like `/think`, `/model`, `/reset`, `/status`, `/compact` are routed to the agent as raw text and answered conversationally by the LLM, not by deterministic handlers; (2) **no plugin except the agent's skills registration calls `registerCommand`** — plugins do not contribute their own commands/actions, so the catalog is only built-ins + navigation + skills; (3) **the catalog projection drops `surfaces`, auth, `category`, and dynamic-choice metadata at the HTTP boundary**, so per-surface filtering and auth gating are dead, and the package's own docs (`UNIVERSAL_SLASH_COMMANDS.md`) describe a richer `CommandDefinition`/`serializeCommand` design that was never landed on `develop`.

## Current state

**The catalog source — `@elizaos/plugin-commands` (landed on `develop`).**
- `plugins/plugin-commands/src/registry.ts` defines `DEFAULT_COMMANDS` (25 built-ins: help/commands/status/context/whoami, stop/restart/reset/new/compact, think/verbose/reasoning/elevated/model/models/usage/queue, allowlist/approve/subagents/config/debug, tts, bash) with a per-runtime store (`initForRuntime`, `useRuntime`).
- `plugins/plugin-commands/src/connector-catalog.ts` re-projects the enabled, connector-scoped registry + a hardcoded `navigationCommands()` list into `ConnectorCommand[]` via `getConnectorCommands(surface)`. Navigation/client commands are defined here, NOT in the registry.
- The plugin registers **only a provider** (`COMMAND_REGISTRY`). Its own `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` state plainly: *"No actions, services, evaluators, routes, or events are registered directly by this plugin… the actual handlers are expected to be implemented by the consuming agent or other plugins"* (`plugins/plugin-commands/AGENTS.md:19,118`).

**No command action handlers exist anywhere.** A tree-wide search for command-handling actions (`hasCommand`/`detectCommand`/`startsWithCommand`/`formatCommandResult`/`CommandContext` in `packages/agent/src/actions` and `plugins/plugin-bootstrap/src`) returns nothing. The only `registerCommand` caller outside the package is `packages/agent/src/runtime/eliza-plugin.ts:150-191`, which registers loaded skills as `skill-<slug>` commands. So:
- TUI `resolveSlashDispatch` comments (`packages/agent/src/tui/slash-commands.ts:206-208`): *"Agent target (the default) — send the literal slash text; an action handler produces the reply."* — there is no such handler.
- Discord `routeCommandToAgent` (`plugins/plugin-discord/catalog-commands.ts:79-150`) and Telegram `buildCommandHandler` (`plugins/plugin-telegram/src/command-registration.ts:160-173`) both route the reconstructed `/command args` text through the agent message pipeline, relying on the LLM to "do the right thing."
- The GUI send path (`packages/ui/src/state/useChatSend.ts:457-546`) only resolves **saved custom commands** and **custom actions** client-side; built-in `agent` commands fall through to "normal slash routing" (raw text to the agent). So `/model gpt-5`, `/think high`, `/reset`, `/usage` have no deterministic effect on any surface.

**Per-surface routing wiring (what is wired):**
- **Route:** `packages/agent/src/api/commands-routes.ts` handles `GET /api/commands`, registered in `packages/agent/src/api/server.ts:344,3115`. It maps `ConnectorCommand` → `SlashCommandCatalogItem`.
- **Web/GUI:** `packages/ui/src/chat/slash-menu.ts` (pure logic) + `useSlashCommandController.ts` (fetch + dynamic choices for `settings-sections`/`views`) + `components/shell/SlashCommandMenu.tsx`. `navigate`/`client` commands run client-side; `agent` commands send raw text.
- **Discord:** `catalog-commands.ts` `registerCatalogSlashCommands` is called from `onReady` (`plugins/plugin-discord/discord-interactions.ts:743`); built-ins win name collisions; folded into `DISCORD_REGISTER_COMMANDS` → `client.application.commands.set`.
- **Telegram:** `command-registration.ts` `registerTelegramCommandHandlers` + `applyTelegramSetMyCommands` invoked from `service.ts:730,762`.
- **TUI:** `packages/agent/src/tui/slash-commands.ts` maps `GET /api/commands?surface=tui` into the `@elizaos/tui` autocomplete.

**Contract drift at the HTTP boundary.** The connector catalog `ConnectorCommand` (`connector-catalog.ts:61-66`) carries only `name`/`description`/`target`/`options`. The client transport type `SlashCommandCatalogItem` (`packages/ui/src/api/client-types-commands.ts`) and the TUI's `SerializedCommand` carry far more: `key`, `category`, `requiresAuth`, `requiresElevated`, `surfaces`, `target.action`, `args[].dynamicChoices`, `args[].captureRemaining`, `icon`, `source`. The route **fabricates/flattens** the missing fields: `commands-routes.ts:121-135` hardcodes `requiresAuth: false`, `requiresElevated: false`, `scope: "both"`, `source: "builtin"`, `key: command.name`, and never emits `surfaces`. Consequences:
- **Per-surface filtering is dead on the wire** — `surfaces` is always `undefined`, so every client renders every command regardless of surface intent. The `?surface=` param is acknowledged not to filter (`commands-routes.ts:6-8`).
- **Auth gating is lost** — `restart`/`reset`/`elevated`/`bash` carry `requiresAuth`/`requiresElevated` in the registry, but the route reports `false` for all. The `bash` (text-scoped, elevated) command is filtered out of connectors by scope, but the GUI catalog gets no auth signal.
- **`dynamicChoices` is inferred, not declared** — `mapOption` (`commands-routes.ts:88-98`) tags `dynamicChoices: "settings-sections"` purely by matching `option.name === "section"`; `models`/`views`/`skills`/`providers` dynamic sources are never emitted by the registry, so model/view arg completion only works because the GUI controller special-cases it client-side.

**Documentation is ahead of the code.** `plugins/plugin-commands/UNIVERSAL_SLASH_COMMANDS.md` documents `CommandSurface`, `CommandTarget`, `surfaces?`, `target?`, `dynamicChoices`, `serializeCommand()`, and a `src/navigation-commands.ts` module — **none of which exist on `develop`** (confirmed: no `surfaces`/`target`/`serializeCommand` in `types.ts`/`registry.ts`; no `navigation-commands.ts` file). It also claims "42 unit tests + 6 route tests"; the package actually ships **23 tests** (`__tests__/connector-catalog.test.ts` 8, `__tests__/parser.test.ts` 15), and there are **no tests for `commands-routes.ts`**, the Discord `catalog-commands` dispatch beyond mapping, or any agent-target handler (because none exists). This is the `shaw/universal-slash-commands` branch design partially merged with a thinner `ConnectorCommand` projection landing instead.

**Coverage gap across plugins.** Only built-in + navigation + skill commands populate the catalog. Plugins that own views/services — `plugin-app-control`, `plugin-facewear`, `plugin-phone`, `plugin-task-coordinator`, `plugin-wallet-ui`, plus connectors and providers — register **zero** commands and **zero** command actions. There is no standard "a plugin contributes commands + handlers" path being used by anything except the skills auto-registration.

## Proposed work

1. **Define the canonical command contract once and stop fabricating it at the route.** Promote the richer fields the clients already expect into `CommandDefinition` (`plugins/plugin-commands/src/types.ts`): `surfaces?: CommandSurface[]`, `target: CommandTarget` (`agent`/`navigate`/`client`), `args[].dynamicChoices`, `args[].captureRemaining`, `category`, `icon`. Add a `serializeCommand()` that drops function-valued choices and produces exactly the `SlashCommandCatalogItem` wire shape. Move `navigationCommands()` and client commands into the registry/a `navigation-commands.ts` module so they carry `surfaces` and auth like everything else. Reconcile `UNIVERSAL_SLASH_COMMANDS.md` with what ships (or land the design it documents).

2. **Make `GET /api/commands` a pure projection.** Rewrite `packages/agent/src/api/commands-routes.ts` to emit `serializeCommand(cmd, surface)` directly — preserving `surfaces`, `requiresAuth`, `requiresElevated`, `category`, real `dynamicChoices`, and actually filtering by `?surface=`. Remove the hardcoded `false`/`"both"`/`"builtin"` literals.

3. **Add the missing command action layer (the core of the ask).** Introduce deterministic handlers for `agent`-target commands so `/status`, `/help`, `/commands`, `/model`, `/think`, `/reasoning`, `/verbose`, `/reset`, `/new`, `/compact`, `/usage`, `/queue`, `/whoami`, `/context`, `/tts` produce real, tested effects instead of LLM passthrough. Implement these as proper elizaOS Actions (slash-only `validate()` via `hasCommand`/`detectCommand`, no natural-language similes — per `plugin-commands/src/index.ts:13-21`) registered by the agent plugin (or a dedicated `plugin-commands` action set), reusing the existing `CommandContext`/`CommandResult`/`isAuthorized`/`isElevated` helpers. This is the single piece that makes commands "work in the chat" the same on every surface.

4. **Establish and document the standard connector interface.** Codify the pattern the three surfaces already approximate (`getConnectorCommands(surface)` → native registration → per-target dispatch: `agent` → message pipeline + reply, `navigate` → deep-link reply, `client` → filtered/local) into a single documented `ConnectorCommandBridge` contract so every communication connector registers + invokes + replies identically. Verify the dispatch + setMyCommands/application.commands paths against live tokens (currently unit-only per the doc's own "Verification status").

5. **Universalize plugin participation.** Give plugins a first-class way to contribute commands and handlers: each plugin that owns a view or capability registers its navigation/agent commands (e.g. `plugin-wallet-ui` → `/wallet`, `plugin-phone` → its surface, `plugin-task-coordinator` → `/tasks` with a real handler). Wire skills (`eliza-plugin.ts`) and custom actions through the same `serializeCommand` path so the GUI's client-side special-casing in `useChatSend.ts` can shrink.

6. **Honor auth/surface on the client.** Once `surfaces`/`requiresAuth` survive serialization, have `slash-menu.ts`/`useSlashCommandController.ts` filter by surface and reflect auth (hide or mark `requiresAuth` commands for unauthorized senders), and have connectors gate `requiresAuth`/`requiresElevated` commands.

7. **Backfill tests.** Add route tests for `commands-routes.ts` (surface filtering, auth pass-through, dynamic-choice emission), action-handler tests for each agent-target command, and Discord/Telegram dispatch tests covering the `agent`/`navigate`/`client` branches end-to-end against mocked clients. Bring the count in line with what the docs claim, or correct the docs.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A user typing `/status`, `/model <m>`, `/think <level>`, `/reset`, `/compact`, `/usage`, `/help`, `/commands` in the floating chat gets a **deterministic** response from a registered action — identical behavior on web, TUI, Discord, and Telegram — not an LLM-improvised reply.
- [ ] `GET /api/commands?surface=<s>` returns commands filtered to that surface, with `surfaces`, `requiresAuth`, `requiresElevated`, `category`, and `dynamicChoices` populated from the registry (no hardcoded route-level literals).
- [ ] At least the built-in, navigation, client, and skill commands all flow through one `serializeCommand()` projection; the client `SlashCommandCatalogItem`, TUI `SerializedCommand`, and connector mappings consume that single shape with no fabricated fields.
- [ ] Every communication connector (`plugin-discord`, `plugin-telegram`, and any future one) registers and dispatches commands through one documented `ConnectorCommandBridge` interface; `agent`/`navigate`/`client` targets behave consistently.
- [ ] Discord registers catalog commands via `application.commands.set` and Telegram via `setMyCommands` + `bot.command`, both gating `requiresAuth`/`requiresElevated` commands.
- [ ] `requiresAuth`/`requiresElevated` commands are hidden or marked for unauthorized senders in the GUI menu and refused server-side on every surface.
- [ ] At least one non-core plugin contributes its own command + handler through the standard registration path, demonstrating the "all plugins register their commands" requirement.
- [ ] `UNIVERSAL_SLASH_COMMANDS.md` matches shipped code (types, modules, and the stated test counts), or the documented design is fully landed.
- [ ] New tests cover the route, each agent-target action, and Discord/Telegram dispatch branches; `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-commands test`, `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-discord test`, `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-telegram test`, and the agent TUI/command tests pass.

## Files & areas likely involved

- `plugins/plugin-commands/src/types.ts` — promote `surfaces`/`target`/`dynamicChoices`/`category`/auth into `CommandDefinition`; add `serializeCommand()`.
- `plugins/plugin-commands/src/registry.ts` — `DEFAULT_COMMANDS`; carry the new fields; possible `navigation-commands.ts` extraction.
- `plugins/plugin-commands/src/connector-catalog.ts` — `getConnectorCommands`, navigation/client command definitions, surface filtering.

- `packages/agent/src/api/commands-routes.ts` — pure projection; surface/auth pass-through. Registered in `packages/agent/src/api/server.ts:344,3115`.
- **NEW** agent-target command action handlers — likely `packages/agent/src/actions/` (registered via `createElizaPlugin` in `packages/agent/src/runtime/eliza-plugin.ts`) or a new action set in `plugins/plugin-commands`.
