- Plugin command participation: `plugins/plugin-wallet-ui`, `plugins/plugin-phone`, `plugins/plugin-task-coordinator`, `plugins/plugin-app-control`, `plugins/plugin-facewear`.

## Open questions

- **Should agent-target command behavior live in `@elizaos/plugin-commands` as actions, or in `@elizaos/agent`?** The package deliberately ships no actions today; centralizing handlers there makes commands universal by default, but couples the catalog package to runtime behaviors. Which package owns `/status`, `/model`, `/reset` handlers?
- **Is the richer `CommandDefinition` (the `UNIVERSAL_SLASH_COMMANDS.md` design with `surfaces`/`target`/`serializeCommand`) the intended target,** i.e. should the thinner `ConnectorCommand` projection be replaced rather than extended?
- **How should `/reset`, `/new`, `/compact` behave on connectors** (Discord/Telegram), where there is no client session to clear? Reply with guidance, perform a server-side conversation reset, or no-op?
- **Auth model on connectors:** which sender identity gates `requiresAuth`/`requiresElevated` over Discord/Telegram (owner pairing, allowlist, role)? Today the route reports `requiresAuth: false` for everything.
- **Do we want a single `ConnectorCommandBridge` abstraction** in `@elizaos/core` or `@elizaos/plugin-commands` that connectors implement, vs. keeping the current per-plugin `register*/dispatch` copies?
- **Should every plugin be required to register commands, or only those with a user-facing capability/view?** "All plugins should have this" needs a concrete bar (e.g. every plugin that registers a view or a primary action exposes at least one command).
31:T3e72,## Summary

There is **no transcription mode anywhere in the codebase today** — only ASR model configuration (`TRANSCRIPTION_MODEL`/`OPENROUTER_TRANSCRIPTION_MODEL`, which is unrelated). The product wants a long-form "transcription mode" where the user records continuously, every utterance lands in the conversation as text, and the agent stays silent until the user says an exit phrase ("exit transcription mode", or any utterance containing transcribe/transcription/stop/exit) — at which point normal response evaluation resumes. The good news: the existing voice stack already has every primitive needed to build this cleanly — a transcript-emitting capture loop (`useShellController.startCapture`), a server-side deterministic reply-suppression seam (`core.voice_turn_signal` evaluator + `voiceTurnSignalSuppressesAgent`), a `metadata` channel from client → server message content, and a chat-header control row. This issue specs a `transcription` capture intent + a `transcriptionMode` UI/state flag + a server-side suppression evaluator + an exit-phrase detector, plus a top-bar toggle button and a `/transcribe` slash command.

## Current state

**There is no transcription-mode concept.** A repo-wide search for `transcriptionMode|transcription_mode|recordOnly|noteToSelf|suppressResponse|noReply|silentTurn` returns only the unrelated ASR model env vars in `plugins/plugin-openrouter/` (`OPENROUTER_TRANSCRIPTION_MODEL`, `DEFAULT_TRANSCRIPTION_MODEL = "openai/whisper-large-v3"`). The product feature does not exist.

**The voice capture state machine lives in `packages/ui/src/components/shell/useShellController.ts`.** It is the central controller (`ShellController`) bridging the chat shell to the agent message flow. Key facts:
- It defines exactly two capture intents today: `export type CaptureIntent = "converse" | "dictate";` (line 26). `converse` sends a `VOICE_DM` and the reply is spoken; `dictate` (push-to-talk hold) routes the final transcript to a "dictation sink" (the composer draft) and does **not** send.
- `startCapture(intent)` (lines 320–455) is where finals are consumed. For `converse`, finals flow through a `TurnAggregator` (`onCommit`) which calls `send(turn, { channelType: "VOICE_DM", metadata: { voiceSource, voiceTurnSignal } })` (lines 365–368). For `dictate`, finals go to `onDictatedTextRef.current?.(text)` (line 407) and are never sent.
- The hands-free loop (lines 583–608) re-opens the mic after each spoken reply — this is the long-running capture mechanism transcription mode can reuse, but it is built around *sending and getting replies*, not around *accumulating silent turns*.
- There is no third intent and no "record but suppress reply" path.

**The transcript pipeline (`packages/ui/src/hooks/useVoiceChat.ts`, 2277 lines) and the capture factory (`packages/ui/src/voice/voice-capture-factory.ts`)** already produce interim + final transcripts via `onTranscript`/`onTranscriptPreview` across browser SpeechRecognition, native talkmode, and local-inference backends. `applyTranscriptUpdate` (lines 569–630) emits finals; for `passive` mode it emits each final immediately (lines 616–627). The session-mode union (`VoiceSessionMode = "idle" | "compose" | "push-to-talk" | "hands-free" | "passive"`, `voice-chat-types.ts:74-80`) has no "transcription" member.

**The exit-phrase / "should the agent reply" gate already has a server-side seam.** In `packages/core/src/services/message.ts`:
- `core.voice_turn_signal` is a `BUILTIN_RESPONSE_HANDLER_EVALUATOR` (lines 2765–2786) that deterministically returns `{ processMessage: "IGNORE", clearReply: true }` when `voiceTurnSignalSuppressesAgent(...)` is true for a voice-channel message.
- `voiceTurnSignalSuppressesAgent` (lines 1647–1657) and `getVoiceTurnSignalMetadata` (lines 1605–1645) read `content.voiceTurnSignal` **or** `content.metadata.voiceTurnSignal`. This is the exact pattern a `transcriptionMode` flag should follow — ride in `metadata`, gate deterministically in a response-handler evaluator. This means **the suppression mechanism already exists** and a transcription turn can be persisted as a normal message while the agent is told not to reply.
- The client `metadata` object reaches `content.metadata` via `buildUserMessages` in `packages/agent/src/api/server-helpers.ts` (metadata spread at lines 664/678), so a `transcriptionMode: true` flag added to the `send(..., { metadata })` call would arrive at exactly where the gate reads it.

**A weaker client-side reply gate also exists:** `packages/ui/src/voice/should-respond.ts` (`shouldRespondToVoiceTurn`) drops disfluencies and TTS echo before a server round-trip. It is conservative and is **not** a mode — it cannot hold transcription turns.

**UI surfaces that a top-bar toggle should live next to:**
- `packages/ui/src/components/composites/chat/ContinuousChatToggle.tsx` — a three-segment Off/VAD/Live switch already designed to sit in the chat header. Mode metadata at lines ~40–66.
- `packages/ui/src/components/composites/chat/ChatVoiceStatusBar.tsx` — the live status strip above the composer (status dot, partial transcript, speaker pill). It renders `VoiceContinuousStatus` (`idle|listening|thinking|speaking|interrupting`) but has no "transcribing" status.
- `packages/ui/src/components/shell/ContinuousChatOverlay.tsx` — the main chat overlay. It has a glass header-button row (`GlassHeaderButton`, ~line 282) for maximize/clear/home/settings and consumes the controller (`handsFree`, `toggleHandsFree`, `startRecording`, `stopRecording`, `setDictationSink` — destructured ~line 756). The push-to-talk state machine (lines 1061–1137) and `handleMicClick → toggleHandsFree` (line 1136) are here.

**Persistence pattern to mirror:** `packages/ui/src/state/persistence.ts` has `loadContinuousChatMode`/`saveContinuousChatMode` (lines 733–747, key `eliza:voice:continuous-chat-mode`). A transcription-mode flag does not need to persist across reloads (it is a transient session state), but the dual-store pattern is available if product wants it sticky.

**The slash-command catalog (`plugins/plugin-commands/src/registry.ts` + `types.ts`)** has no `/transcribe` command. `CommandDefinition` (`types.ts:31-44`) supports `key`, `textAliases`, `scope`, `category` (`media` is an existing category), so a `/transcribe` (aliases: transcription, dictate-long) command is straightforward to register.

## Proposed work

Ordered, layered inward-out so each step is independently verifiable:

1. **Add a `transcriptionMode` runtime/transport flag (core).** In `packages/core/src/services/message.ts`, add a `getTranscriptionModeFlag(message)` reader mirroring `getVoiceTurnSignalMetadata` (read `content.metadata.transcriptionMode` and `content.transcriptionMode`). Add a new builtin response-handler evaluator `core.transcription_mode` to `BUILTIN_RESPONSE_HANDLER_EVALUATORS` with `shouldRun: ({message}) => transcriptionModeActive(message)` returning `{ processMessage: "IGNORE", requiresTool: false, clearReply: true, debug: [...] }`. This persists the user turn as a normal message but suppresses any reply — the canonical "record into the conversation, agent stays silent" behavior, reusing the proven `voice_turn_signal` suppression path. Do **not** invent a new ChannelType; transcription turns are normal `DM`/`VOICE_DM` turns carrying the flag.

2. **Add a `"transcription"` capture intent to the shell controller (ui).** In `packages/ui/src/components/shell/useShellController.ts`: extend `CaptureIntent` to `"converse" | "dictate" | "transcription"`; add `transcriptionMode: boolean` + `toggleTranscriptionMode()` to `ShellController`. In `startCapture`, the `transcription` intent uses a long-running capture (reuse the hands-free re-listen loop semantics) and routes **every** final through `send(text, { channelType: "DM", metadata: { transcriptionMode: true } })` so each utterance lands in the transcript silently. Do **not** route through the `TurnAggregator` echo/disfluency gate (we want a verbatim long-form transcript) — but **do** run exit-phrase detection (step 3) on every final before sending.

3. **Exit-phrase detection (ui).** Add `packages/ui/src/voice/transcription-exit.ts` exporting `isTranscriptionExitPhrase(text): boolean` and `stripExitPhrase(text)`. Trigger on an utterance that contains the explicit phrase "exit transcription mode" / "stop transcription" / "end transcription", and also (per product) on any standalone short utterance whose tokens include transcribe/transcription/stop/exit. On detection: (a) commit any preceding non-exit text as a final transcription turn, (b) call `toggleTranscriptionMode()` off, (c) re-enable normal response evaluation — the *next* utterance (or the residual non-exit text, product decision in Open Questions) is sent as a normal turn so the agent responds. This is the "run the response evaluation" trigger.

4. **Top-bar toggle button + status (ui).** Add a transcription-mode toggle to the chat header. Cleanest placement: a new segment/icon in `ContinuousChatToggle.tsx` *or* a dedicated `GlassHeaderButton` in `ContinuousChatOverlay.tsx`'s header row (FileText/Captions lucide icon). When `transcriptionMode` is true, show a persistent badge/pill at the top ("Transcribing — say 'exit transcription mode' to stop") and add a `"transcribing"` status to `VoiceContinuousStatus` so `ChatVoiceStatusBar.tsx` surfaces the live partial transcript and a distinct dot color. Follow brand rules (orange accent only, neutral→neutral-opacity hover).

5. **Agent-enable path: "transcribe"/"transcription" intent (ui + core).** The user asking "transcribe this" / "start transcription" should enable the mode. Two complementary triggers: (a) Register a `/transcribe` slash command in `plugins/plugin-commands/src/registry.ts` (category `media`, aliases `transcription`, `dictate`) whose handler flips the client into transcription mode; (b) optionally, a lightweight intent recognizer on the *first* voice/text turn ("can you transcribe", "turn on transcription") that enables the mode — but prefer the explicit command + button to avoid false positives (see Open Questions). When enabled by the agent/command, the same top-bar button lights up.

6. **Tests.** Unit-test `isTranscriptionExitPhrase`/`stripExitPhrase`; unit-test the new `core.transcription_mode` evaluator (`shouldRun`/`evaluate`) with a transcription-flagged message → reply suppressed, turn persisted; controller test that `startCapture("transcription")` sends finals with `metadata.transcriptionMode` and never speaks; an overlay/e2e screenshot test showing the top-bar transcribing badge.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A `transcriptionMode` flag travels client → server via `content.metadata` and a `core.transcription_mode` response-handler evaluator deterministically suppresses the agent's reply while the user turn is still persisted to the conversation (verified by a core unit test).
- [ ] `useShellController` exposes `transcriptionMode: boolean` and `toggleTranscriptionMode()`, and a `"transcription"` `CaptureIntent` that records long-form, routes every final into the conversation silently, and never triggers spoken replies.
- [ ] Saying "exit transcription mode" (and the other documented phrases, plus a standalone stop/exit/transcribe utterance) turns transcription mode **off** and the very next turn is response-evaluated normally (the agent replies again).
- [ ] A top-bar control toggles transcription mode; while active, a persistent badge is visible with the exit hint, and `ChatVoiceStatusBar` shows a distinct "transcribing" status + live partial transcript.
- [ ] Asking the agent to transcribe (via `/transcribe` and/or a recognized request) enables transcription mode and lights the top-bar button.
- [ ] No new `ChannelType` is introduced; transcription turns reuse existing channels with the metadata flag.
- [ ] Brand/color rules honored on the new control (orange accent only; correct hover); `bun run --cwd packages/ui test` and `bun run --cwd packages/core test` pass; no `console.*` added (structured logger only on server side).

- `packages/ui/src/voice/transcription-exit.ts` — **new** exit-phrase detector (`isTranscriptionExitPhrase`, `stripExitPhrase`).
- `packages/ui/src/hooks/useVoiceChat.ts` / `packages/ui/src/voice/voice-capture-factory.ts` — long-running capture reuse; possibly a `"transcription"` member added to `VoiceSessionMode` (`voice-chat-types.ts:74-80`).

- `packages/ui/src/components/composites/chat/ChatVoiceStatusBar.tsx` — transcribing status dot + live transcript.
- `packages/core/src/services/message.ts` — `getTranscriptionModeFlag`, `core.transcription_mode` evaluator in `BUILTIN_RESPONSE_HANDLER_EVALUATORS` (alongside `core.voice_turn_signal`, lines 2765–2786; gate helpers near lines 1605–1657).

## Open questions

- **Residual non-exit text on the exit utterance:** if the user says "...and that's the end. Exit transcription mode." in one breath, should the leading clause be committed as the final transcription turn and the exit consumed (recommended), or should the whole utterance be discarded? Spec assumes commit-then-exit.
- **After exit, what gets response-evaluated?** The product says "run the response evaluation" on exit. Does exit itself trigger a reply (e.g. "Transcription saved — N turns"), or does the agent simply resume normal replies on the *next* turn? Spec assumes the latter (silent exit, normal replies resume) unless a confirmation reply is desired.
- **Implicit enable via natural language** ("transcribe this for me"): how aggressive should intent recognition be? False enables are annoying. Recommend shipping the explicit `/transcribe` command + button first, and gating any NL auto-enable behind a confirmation.
- **Transcript persistence/export:** is a long transcription session just a sequence of normal conversation messages (recommended — reuses history/search/export for free), or does it need a distinct "transcript document" artifact (e.g. via plugin-documents)? Spec assumes normal messages tagged with `metadata.transcriptionMode`.
- **Scope of utterance-level stop triggers:** product says "any time transcription/transcribe/stop/exit etc are uttered, run the response evaluation." Taken literally this would exit on a transcription *containing* the word "stop" mid-sentence ("don't stop believing"). Spec narrows this to *short standalone* utterances or the explicit phrase to avoid accidental exits — needs product confirmation on exact matching strictness.
- **Cross-surface scope:** is transcription mode app-only (dashboard/desktop chat shell), or should Discord/Telegram voice and the TUI also honor it? The server gate (`core.transcription_mode`) makes it surface-agnostic, but the UI work in this issue is the app shell only.
32:T3dc0,## Summary

When the agent switches the user's view — via the deterministic early shortcut, the `VIEWS` action, or the contextual post-turn evaluator — the reply the user reads was already generated **before** the switch was decided or executed, so the agent rarely acknowledges that it just moved them ("...opening your calendar now"). The `current_view` provider that is supposed to carry this awareness exists but is wired so it (a) is not reliably composed into the Stage-1 response state where the reply text is produced, and (b) reads server state that reflects the view at turn *start*, not the just-happened switch. We should make the view switch a first-class, turn-scoped context signal that lands in the response prompt, and reconcile the evaluator path with the action path so whichever one fires produces the same acknowledgement.

## Current state

The view-switch machinery is already substantial and lives in `@elizaos/plugin-app-control`, fed by server-side state in `@elizaos/agent`. There is no missing subsystem — the problem is **timing and provider wiring**, not absence.

**Server-side current-view state** is held in `packages/agent/src/api/views-routes.ts`:
- `POST /api/views/:id/navigate` writes `currentViewState` and calls `setActiveViewContext(...)` (`views-routes.ts:808-826`), and `GET /api/views/current` reads it back (`views-routes.ts:388-392`).
- The same state is mirrored into the planner-affinity module `packages/agent/src/runtime/view-action-affinity.ts` (`setActiveViewContext` / `getActiveViewContext`, L49-61), which renders an "# Active View" awareness block into the *planner* prompt (`renderActiveViewContextBlock`, L210-253) and applies action affinity. That block is about *where the user is*, injected for tool selection — it is not a reply-time "you just switched" acknowledgement.

**The three-stage view-switch cascade** (documented in `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/index.ts:111-125`):
1. EARLY — `viewCommandShortcutEvaluator` (`evaluators/view-command-shortcut.ts`), a `responseHandlerEvaluator` that on an explicit multilingual command forces the `VIEWS` action onto the plan (`requiresTool: true`, `addCandidateActions: ["VIEWS"]`).
2. ACTION — `viewsAction` (`actions/views.ts`) → `runViewsShow` (`actions/views-show.ts`), which POSTs `/api/views/:id/navigate` and returns a callback string like `Navigated to Calendar (gui).`
3. POST — `viewContextEvaluator` (`evaluators/view-context.ts`), a post-turn `Evaluator` (small model) that catches contextual intent ("fix the login bug" → `task-coordinator`) and navigates in a processor (`navigateToContextualView`, L78-114).

**The provider that should carry acknowledgement already exists but is mis-wired.** `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/providers/current-view.ts` defines `current_view` (`position: -7`), whose `get()` reads `GET /api/views/current` and returns text "The user is currently viewing the X view...". Two concrete problems:

- **It is not in the Stage-1 response state.** Reply text is generated from `composeResponseState` (`packages/core/src/services/message.ts:1097-1126`), whose provider set is `CORE_RESPONSE_STATE_PROVIDERS` (`message.ts:843-868`) plus `alwaysOnResponseStateProviderNames` (providers that opt in via the `alwaysInResponseState` flag, `message.ts:876-886`; flag defined at `packages/core/src/types/components.ts:576-583`). `current_view` is in neither list, sets no `contexts`/`contextGate`, and is not `alwaysInResponseState`, so under context-routed selection it has no declared context to be picked by and effectively never reaches the reply-generation prompt. It only surfaces in the Stage-2 planner state — too late to shape the user-visible reply.
- **Even if composed, it is one turn behind.** `composeResponseState` runs at the *start* of the turn, before `runResponseHandlerEvaluators` (`message.ts:5965`) forces `VIEWS`, before the action executes, and before the post-turn `viewContextEvaluator` navigates. So `GET /api/views/current` returns the *previous* view, and the just-happened switch never appears in the reply that announces it. The acknowledgement only shows up on the *next* turn.

**User-initiated navigation may not update the server state at all.** When the user clicks a tab in the shell, the UI dispatches the `eliza:navigate:view` DOM event (`packages/ui/src/App.tsx:1274,1500`; `packages/ui/src/app-navigate-view.ts`), but there is no corresponding client → `POST /api/views/:id/navigate` reporting the new active view back to the agent (grep of `packages/app/src` and `packages/ui/src` finds no such call — only `shell:navigate:view` *outbound* from server to client). So `currentViewState` reflects only *agent-initiated* navigation; a user who manually switches tabs leaves the server's notion of "current view" stale, which `current_view`, the planner "# Active View" block, and any acknowledgement signal all read from.

**Relevant new infrastructure on this branch.** The branch `shaw/composestate-provider-hook` adds a `compose_state_providers` pipeline hook (`packages/core/src/types/pipeline-hooks.ts:75-93,176-200`; tested in `packages/core/src/__tests__/compose-state-provider-hook.test.ts`). It fires inside `composeState` after provider *names* are selected but before any `get()` runs, and lets a host reassign `providers.current` to add/drop providers per message intent. This is the clean seam to force the view-switch provider into the response state for exactly the turns where a switch is happening, without giving `current_view` a global `alwaysInResponseState` cost on every turn.

## Proposed work

Ordered, targeted scope. Reuse the existing modules; do not introduce a second view-state store.

1. **Define a turn-scoped "view switch just happened" signal** (not "where the user is" — that already exists as the planner block). Add a small typed record alongside `currentViewState` in `packages/agent/src/api/views-routes.ts`: when `/api/views/:id/navigate` runs, stamp `{ viewId, viewLabel, viewPath, viewType, at: <timestamp>, source: "agent" }`. Expose it on `GET /api/views/current` (e.g. add a `switchedAt`/`justSwitched` field) so a single loopback read tells the provider both *current view* and *whether it changed very recently*.

2. **Make the switch land in the same turn's reply.** Two coordinated changes:
   - Have the `VIEWS` action and the contextual evaluator's `navigateToContextualView` processor record the switch synchronously when they navigate (they already call navigate; just make sure the server stamp is fresh before the acknowledgement is composed).
   - Recompose or augment the response-state context so the post-switch provider runs for the reply that announces it. Concretely: in `composeResponseState` (`packages/core/src/services/message.ts`), either add `current_view` to the response provider set for view-capable surfaces, or register a `compose_state_providers` pipeline hook (the seam this branch adds) that injects the view-switch provider when a recent switch is detected for the room. Prefer the hook so the cost is paid only on switch turns.

3. **Reconcile evaluator path vs action path into one acknowledgement source.** The product requirement is "if it didn't hit in the evaluator, it can hit in the action." Today the early shortcut forces the action (acknowledged via the action callback string), while the contextual evaluator navigates silently in a processor (no user-facing reply about the move). Unify so both produce the *same* surfaced acknowledgement:
   - When the contextual `viewContextEvaluator` navigates post-turn (`evaluators/view-context.ts:104-112`), it should surface a short acknowledgement to the user (it currently only logs + returns `values.contextualView`), or hand the switch to the provider/next-turn pathway so the agent acknowledges it rather than moving the user with no comment.
   - Keep the existing disjointness guarantee (`shouldRun` bails when `resolveIntentView` matched — `view-context.ts:161`) so the two paths never double-navigate.

4. **Strengthen the `current_view` provider into the acknowledgement provider.** Update `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/providers/current-view.ts` so that, when the new `justSwitched` signal is set for this turn, its text is phrased as an acknowledgement ("You just switched the user to the Calendar view") rather than only ambient state ("The user is currently viewing..."). Give it the right wiring to be selected in response state on switch turns (via the hook from step 2, or `alwaysInResponseState` scoped behind a cheap guard, or a declared context).

5. **Close the user-initiated-navigation gap (decision-gated).** Decide whether manual tab switches should report back to the agent. If yes, add a client → `POST /api/views/:id/navigate` (with `source: "user"`) on the `eliza:navigate:view` handler path in `packages/ui/src/App.tsx` / `packages/app`, so `currentViewState` and the acknowledgement provider reflect reality regardless of who switched. If no, document that "current view" tracks agent-initiated navigation only. (See Open questions.)

6. **Tests.** Extend the existing suites rather than adding parallel ones:
   - `packages/agent/src/api/views-routes.navigate-broadcast.test.ts` already asserts `getCurrentViewState()` after navigate (L307-317) — add coverage for the `justSwitched`/`switchedAt` stamp and its expiry semantics.
   - Add a `compose_state_providers` hook test (mirroring `packages/core/src/__tests__/compose-state-provider-hook.test.ts`) proving the view-switch provider is injected into response state only on switch turns.
   - Add a scenario in `packages/scenario-runner/test/scenarios/` (next to the existing `deterministic-view-switching.scenario.ts` referenced in `views-routes.navigate-broadcast.test.ts:24-26`) asserting the reply *acknowledges* the switch in the same turn, for both the action path and the contextual-evaluator path.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] After an agent-initiated view switch, the **same-turn** reply can reference the switch (the acknowledgement signal is present in the Stage-1 response prompt for that turn), not just the next turn.
- [ ] Both the `VIEWS` action path and the contextual `viewContextEvaluator` path produce a user-facing acknowledgement of the switch; neither moves the user silently.
- [ ] The action path and evaluator path remain mutually exclusive (no double-navigation) — the existing `resolveIntentView` gate in `view-context.ts` still holds.
- [ ] `GET /api/views/current` exposes a recent-switch signal (e.g. `switchedAt`/`justSwitched`) distinct from the always-present current-view, and it expires so a stale switch is not re-acknowledged on every subsequent turn.
- [ ] The acknowledgement provider only adds prompt/token cost on turns where a switch actually happened (no unconditional always-on cost on every message), achieved via the `compose_state_providers` hook or an equivalently scoped gate.
- [ ] A documented decision on user-initiated navigation: either manual tab switches report back to the server (and the provider reflects them) or it is explicitly scoped to agent-initiated navigation.
- [ ] Tests cover: the server-side switch stamp + expiry, provider injection on switch turns only, and an end-to-end scenario where the reply acknowledges the switch for both navigation paths.
- [ ] `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-app-control test` and `bun run --cwd packages/agent test` pass; `bun run verify` is clean.

## Files & areas likely involved

- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/providers/current-view.ts` — the acknowledgement provider; phrasing + selection wiring.
- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/evaluators/view-context.ts` — contextual post-turn evaluator + `navigateToContextualView` processor (surface acknowledgement / hand off the switch).
- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/evaluators/view-command-shortcut.ts` — early deterministic forcing of `VIEWS` (acknowledgement consistency).
- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/views-show.ts` / `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/views.ts` — action navigate path; ensure switch is stamped before acknowledgement composes.
- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/views-client.ts` — `getCurrentView` / `navigate` loopback client; carry the new `justSwitched` field.
- `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/index.ts` — provider/evaluator registration; confirm acknowledgement provider is in the response path.
- `packages/agent/src/api/views-routes.ts` — `currentViewState`, `/navigate`, `/current`; add the switch stamp + expiry.
- `packages/agent/src/runtime/view-action-affinity.ts` — existing planner "# Active View" block / active-view context (do not duplicate; reuse).
- `packages/core/src/services/message.ts` — `composeResponseState`, `CORE_RESPONSE_STATE_PROVIDERS`, `alwaysOnResponseStateProviderNames`; selection of the provider for the response turn.
- `packages/core/src/types/pipeline-hooks.ts` + `packages/core/src/__tests__/compose-state-provider-hook.test.ts` — the `compose_state_providers` hook seam (this branch) to inject the provider on switch turns.
- `packages/core/src/types/components.ts:576-583` — `alwaysInResponseState` flag semantics.
- `packages/ui/src/App.tsx` (`eliza:navigate:view` handler, ~L1261-1502) / `packages/ui/src/app-navigate-view.ts` / `packages/app/src` — user-initiated navigation report-back (decision-gated).
- `packages/agent/src/api/views-routes.navigate-broadcast.test.ts`, `packages/scenario-runner/test/scenarios/deterministic-view-switching.scenario.ts` — test surfaces to extend.

## Open questions

- **User-initiated navigation:** should manual tab switches report back to the agent (so "current view" and acknowledgement reflect user clicks), or is the signal intentionally scoped to agent-initiated navigation only? This drives whether step 5 is in scope and whether the provider phrasing must distinguish "I switched you" vs "you're now on X".
- **Acknowledgement strength:** should the agent *always* verbally acknowledge a switch, or only when the switch was contextual/inferred (not when the user explicitly asked "open calendar", where the move is self-evident)? Over-acknowledging explicit commands may feel verbose.
- **Same-turn cost vs latency:** surfacing the switch in the *same* turn implies either composing the acknowledgement provider before knowing a switch will occur, or a recompose after the plan forces `VIEWS`. The early `viewCommandShortcutEvaluator` decides the switch before the action runs but after the reply text is generated — confirm the cleanest insertion point (recompose response state after the response-handler evaluators patch the plan, vs. injecting via the `compose_state_providers` hook keyed on the forced plan) without adding a second model call.
- **Switch-signal expiry window:** how recent counts as "just switched" (a fixed TTL like a few seconds, or strictly the same `runId`/turn)? Tie this to `runId` correlation (already threaded through pipeline hooks, `pipeline-hooks.ts:136-140`) rather than wall-clock if possible.
- **Multi-surface / split-view:** `/navigate` already accepts `split-view`/`tile-views` with multiple `views[]` (`views-routes.ts:773-791`). Does acknowledgement need to describe multi-view layouts, or only single-view switches initially?

## Notes for maintainer

This issue is wiring/timing, not green-field: `current_view`, the three-stage cascade, the server `currentViewState`, and the planner "# Active View" block all already exist. The single highest-leverage fix is getting a *turn-scoped, freshly-stamped* switch signal into the **Stage-1 response state** (where reply text is generated), which the `compose_state_providers` hook added on this branch (`shaw/composestate-provider-hook`) is purpose-built to enable.
33:T446c,## Summary

The product goal is "Kokoro and OmniVoice working everywhere; on mobile, Kokoro is the default local voice (OmniVoice desktop-only for now); streaming end-to-end on all models; max optimization." The selection *policy* for this is already correct and well-tested in code — `selectVoiceBackend()` forces Kokoro unconditionally on mobile and the catalog (`ELIZA_1_VOICE_BACKENDS`) maps the mobile-class tiers (0.8b/2b/4b) to `["kokoro"]` and the large tiers to `["omnivoice"]`. The problem is **below** the policy: (1) the in-process Kokoro FFI path that mobile *requires* (`KokoroFfiRuntime` over `eliza_inference_kokoro_*`) depends on FFI export symbols that do not exist in the vendored native source and are only present in an **orphaned, never-applied patch**; (2) the iOS XCFramework slice is pinned to an older ABI v4 fork that does not carry the Kokoro symbols and is not even verified to; and (3) the entire backend-selection module is duplicated as committed dead code in `@elizaos/shared`. Net effect: the mobile-Kokoro path will throw at synth time on iOS (and is unverified on Android beyond a one-off Pixel note), so "Kokoro working everywhere / mobile default" is not actually shippable as-is.

## Current state

### The selection policy is correct and live (good)
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/kokoro/runtime-selection.ts` — `selectVoiceBackend()` short-circuits to Kokoro when `inputs.mobile === true`, ignoring `mode`/RTF/TTFA, and **throws** if Kokoro artifacts are missing rather than falling back to OmniVoice (lines 115–126). This matches the product intent ("OmniVoice desktop-only for now").
- `packages/shared/src/local-inference/catalog.ts:168–178` — `ELIZA_1_VOICE_BACKENDS` maps `eliza-1-0_8b/2b/4b → ["kokoro"]`, `9b → ["omnivoice","kokoro"]`, `27b/27b-256k → ["omnivoice"]`. First entry is the tier default consumed by `selectVoiceBackend`'s `tierVoiceBackends`.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/engine.ts:955–977` is the live caller. It passes `mobile: isMobilePlatform()`, `kokoroAvailable`, `omnivoiceAvailable`, `tierVoiceBackends: bundle.voiceBackends`. When the decision is `kokoro` it calls `startVoice({ kokoroOnly: kokoro })`.
- `createKokoroTtsBackend()` in `engine-bridge.ts:699–733` calls `pickKokoroRuntimeBackend({ defaultBackend: "ffi", ... })`, so the in-process fused FFI path (`KokoroFfiRuntime`) is the default on every platform. `KOKORO_BACKEND=fork` (llama-server `/v1/audio/speech`) is an explicit dev/desktop opt-in only, never resolved on mobile (it requires opening a local TCP socket, which iOS/Play forbid).

### The native Kokoro FFI symbols the mobile path needs do not exist in source (broken)
This is the central blocker. `KokoroFfiRuntime` (`kokoro-ffi-runtime.ts`) probes `ffi.kokoroSupported()` and calls `ffi.kokoroLoad` / `ffi.kokoroSynthesize`, which bind to native exports `eliza_inference_kokoro_supported/load/synthesize/sample_rate` (declared optional in `ffi-bindings.ts:644–671, 977–985`, ABI v10).

But those native exports are **not defined anywhere in the vendored source**:
- `grep` for `eliza_inference_kokoro_synthesize` across all `.cpp/.c/.h` files returns **zero definitions**.
- The fused FFI surface (`plugins/plugin-local-inference/native/llama.cpp/tools/omnivoice/src/eliza-inference-ffi.cpp`) has **0** kokoro references (only two stray comments).
- `tools/omnivoice/CMakeLists.txt` has **no** `if(TARGET kokoro_lib)` fold; `weight-ctx.h` has **no** `kokoro_model_path` field; there is **no** `ELIZA_ENABLE_KOKORO` / `load_kokoro_tts` anywhere in source.
- The standalone Kokoro engine *does* exist (`plugins/plugin-local-inference/native/llama.cpp/tools/kokoro/src/kokoro.cpp` exports `kokoro_load_model`/`kokoro_load_voice_preset`/`kokoro_synthesize`), but nothing wraps it into the fused `libelizainference` ABI.

All of the wiring that would produce the `eliza_inference_kokoro_*` exports lives **only** in `packages/app-core/scripts/patches/llama-mobile-kokoro-tts.patch` — and an exhaustive search shows **nothing references or applies that patch** (no build script, no AOSP `apply-patches.mjs`, no `omnivoice-fuse/prepare.mjs` step). The patch is orphaned. So unless someone applies it by hand, the fused library never exports the Kokoro symbols → `kokoroSupported()` returns false → on mobile (where there is no fallback) `KokoroFfiRuntime`'s constructor throws `VoiceLifecycleError("kernel-missing", …)`.

The most recent commit `4ebbd2dc50 "route Kokoro TTS onto in-process fused FFI (ABI v10)"` added the JS runtime + the patch but did not land the native fold into the build path.

### iOS XCFramework is on a stale ABI and doesn't verify Kokoro (broken for mobile-default)
- `packages/app-core/scripts/build-llama-cpp-mtp.mjs` builds fused iOS slices with `-DLLAMA_BUILD_KOKORO=ON` and collects `libkokoro_lib*.a` (lines 222, 327, 343), but its `REQUIRED_FUSED_SYMBOLS` gate (line 113) only asserts `eliza_inference_tts_synthesize` (OmniVoice) and `eliza_inference_asr_transcribe` — it **never verifies the `eliza_inference_kokoro_*` symbols landed**.
- Worse, the emitted `CAPABILITIES.json` hardcodes `omnivoice.abiVersion: 4` (TTS/ASR/VAD only) for the fused iOS slice (lines ~415–425). That fork revision predates Kokoro (v10), text streaming, and EOT (v11). So the iOS build advertises and is built against an ABI with no Kokoro at all, while the desktop/AOSP fused path is at ABI v11.
- The standalone symbol verifier `packages/app-core/scripts/build-helpers/verify-fused-symbols.mjs:306–309` *does* require the four `eliza_inference_kokoro_*` symbols and is invoked by the AOSP (`aosp/compile-libllama.mjs`) and merged-omnivoice paths. Its comment cites a one-off "Pixel: kokoro_supported()==1 + 2.36s of synthesized PCM," which implies Android *can* produce a Kokoro-capable fused lib **when the patch is applied** — but the AOSP `apply-patches.mjs` series directory only contains `polarquant`/`qjl`, not the Kokoro FFI patch, so even the Android path's Kokoro fold is not reproducibly wired in source.

### Streaming / TTFA is "streaming-shaped," not incremental decode
`KokoroTtsBackend` (`kokoro-backend.ts:44–158`) is `StreamingTtsBackend`, but the runtime "produces the full waveform in one forward" and the backend re-chunks it into `sampleRate/4`-sample slices to feed the scheduler ring buffer (line 60–61, 136–141). Real time-to-first-audio is therefore the **whole-phrase** synth latency, mitigated only by upstream phrase chunking (`phrase-chunker.ts`, `first-line-cache.ts`). OmniVoice has the same one-forward shape on the FFI path. ASR has a genuine streaming surface (`ffi-bindings.ts:458–486` `asrStreamOpen/Feed/Partial/Finish`, plus `streaming-asr/streaming-pipeline-adapter.ts`), so the asymmetry is on the TTS side: there is no per-token/per-frame incremental TTS decode, which caps how low TTFA can go on long first phrases.

### Backend-selection module is duplicated as committed dead code
`packages/shared/src/local-inference/kokoro/` is a **stale, committed (git-tracked) fork** of the live `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/kokoro/` directory. `runtime-selection.ts` is byte-identical except tabs-vs-spaces (237 lines each). The shared copy is **not** re-exported from `packages/shared/src/local-inference/index.ts` and **no package imports it** (verified by grep). It is also already drifting: the plugin copy has `kokoro-ffi-runtime.ts` and `pick-runtime.test.ts`; the shared copy does not. This is exactly the duplication the cleanup mission targets — two sources of truth for backend selection, one of them dead.

### Mobile platform flag dependency
`selectVoiceBackend({ mobile: isMobilePlatform() })` is only correct if `ELIZA_PLATFORM` is set to `ios`/`android` at runtime (`runtime-env.ts:375`). The mobile transports set it (`packages/ui/src/api/ios-local-agent-transport.ts`), but this should be asserted in the device-bridge boot path so a misconfigured device doesn't silently fall through to the desktop heuristic (and then try OmniVoice, which isn't shipped on mobile).

## Proposed work

Ordered, highest-leverage first.

1. **Land the Kokoro FFI fold into the vendored native source (unblock mobile Kokoro).** Apply `llama-mobile-kokoro-tts.patch` into the actual checked-in fork tree (`plugins/plugin-local-inference/native/llama.cpp/`): the `if(TARGET kokoro_lib)` fold in `tools/omnivoice/CMakeLists.txt`, the `kokoro_model_path` field in `weight-ctx.h`, and the `eliza_inference_kokoro_*` exports + `ELIZA_ENABLE_KOKORO` blocks in `tools/omnivoice/src/eliza-inference-ffi.cpp`. Then **delete the orphaned patch file** (or, if patches are the chosen mechanism, register it in the AOSP `apply-patches.mjs` series and the iOS build so it is reproducibly applied). The end state must be: a from-clean fused build exports `eliza_inference_kokoro_supported/load/synthesize/sample_rate`.

2. **Add Kokoro to the iOS XCFramework symbol gate and bump its advertised ABI.** In `build-llama-cpp-mtp.mjs`, add the four `eliza_inference_kokoro_*` symbols to `REQUIRED_FUSED_SYMBOLS` for fused slices, and stop hardcoding `omnivoice.abiVersion: 4` — derive it from the actual built ABI (v11) or at minimum advertise Kokoro capability. The iOS fused slice must fail the build if Kokoro symbols are missing, identical to how AOSP/`verify-fused-symbols.mjs` already enforces it.

3. **Delete the dead `@elizaos/shared/local-inference/kokoro/` directory.** It is committed, unreferenced, and already drifting from the live plugin copy. Remove the whole directory (runtime-selection, kokoro-backend, kokoro-runtime, pick-runtime, phonemizer, phoneme-stream, voices, voice-presets, types, index, and their tests). Confirm nothing imports it (already verified). The single source of truth is the plugin copy.

4. **Enforce the mobile platform invariant at the device-bridge boot.** In the capacitor/AOSP/electrobun-mobile boot path, assert `ELIZA_PLATFORM ∈ {ios, android}` (and set it if a device-bridge context is detected) so `isMobilePlatform()` is reliable and OmniVoice can never be selected on a phone. Add a boot-time log line of the resolved backend decision (`decision.reason`) for field debugging.

5. **Add a real end-to-end Kokoro-on-fused-FFI verification test per platform.** A `*.real.test.ts` (gated like the existing `voice-roundtrip.real.test.ts` / `whisper-cpp-asr.real.test.ts`) that loads the fused lib, asserts `kokoroSupported() === 1`, synthesizes a phrase, and asserts non-empty 24 kHz PCM. Run it in the desktop fused lane and wire the AOSP/iOS device-smoke equivalents so the "device-proven on a Pixel" note becomes CI, not folklore.

6. **Close the streaming/TTFA gap on TTS (max-speed objective).** Two concrete, separable improvements: (a) verify the first-phrase budget path (`phrase-chunker.ts` + `first-line-cache.ts` + `voice-budget.ts`) actually shrinks the first audible chunk on mobile-class tiers and add a TTFA assertion to the roundtrip test; (b) evaluate exposing an incremental/streamed Kokoro decode at the FFI boundary (emit PCM as the iSTFT decoder produces it rather than one final forward) so TTFA on long first phrases is bounded by a sub-phrase, matching the ASR streaming surface that already exists.

7. **Reconcile the ABI-version story in docs and bindings.** `kokoro-ffi-runtime.ts` and `pick-runtime.ts` comments say "ABI v10"; `ffi-bindings.ts` is now at `ELIZA_INFERENCE_ABI_VERSION = 11` (accepting "10" only when EOT symbols are absent). Update the Kokoro runtime/doc comments to the v11 reality so the next reader doesn't trust a stale ABI claim.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A from-clean fused build (desktop, AOSP, iOS) exports all four `eliza_inference_kokoro_*` symbols; `verify-fused-symbols.mjs` and the iOS `REQUIRED_FUSED_SYMBOLS` gate both enforce them.
- [ ] `packages/app-core/scripts/patches/llama-mobile-kokoro-tts.patch` is either applied-into-source-and-deleted, or registered in a build step that runs in CI (no orphaned patch).
- [ ] iOS `CAPABILITIES.json` advertises the correct fused ABI (not a hardcoded v4) and a non-null Kokoro capability.
- [ ] On a mobile build (`ELIZA_PLATFORM=ios|android`), `selectVoiceBackend` returns `kokoro` and `KokoroFfiRuntime` synthesizes real PCM — verified by an automated device/real test, not a manual note.
- [ ] `packages/shared/src/local-inference/kokoro/` is deleted; `bun run build` + `bun run verify` + `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-local-inference test` stay green.
- [ ] On desktop, the tier policy resolves OmniVoice for 27b/9b and Kokoro for 0.8b/2b/4b; OmniVoice is never selected on a mobile build.
- [ ] A TTFA assertion exists for the Kokoro path's first audible chunk on a mobile-class tier.
- [ ] Kokoro runtime/doc comments reference ABI v11 (no stale "v10" claims that contradict `ffi-bindings.ts`).

## Open questions

- **Mechanism decision for the native Kokoro fold:** merge the patch directly into the vendored `native/llama.cpp/` fork tree (simplest, most reproducible), or keep it as a patch applied by a registered build step? The repo already has two patch systems (AOSP `apply-patches.mjs` series + `omnivoice-fuse/prepare.mjs`); whichever is chosen, the current orphaned-patch state must not persist.
- **iOS fork revision:** is the iOS XCFramework intentionally pinned to the older ABI v4 fork, or is that stale? Advancing it to the v10/v11 fork (with Kokoro + EOT) is a prerequisite for "Kokoro working on iOS" and may be a non-trivial fork bump with its own validation.
- **Android default-voice scope:** memory/notes indicate the kokoro-native runtime was validated once on a Pixel. Is Android currently shipping a Kokoro-capable fused lib in the release AOSP build, or only in ad-hoc dev builds? Determines how much of item 1/5 is "wire it" vs "verify it."
- **Incremental TTS decode (item 6b):** is streamed sub-phrase Kokoro decode in scope for this issue, or should it be split into a follow-up? It is the only path to materially lower TTFA on long first phrases but is a deeper native change than the wiring fixes.
- **OmniVoice-on-mobile timeline:** the policy bakes in "OmniVoice desktop-only for now." Should the mobile-exclusive throw-on-missing-Kokoro behavior stay hard (current), or is a documented degraded path desired if a device ever lacks Kokoro artifacts? 34:T3fd2,## Summary

Voice in elizaOS today is **two disconnected pipelines bolted onto the message loop**, not one first-class voice path. The web/desktop "ambient" voice path (`useShellController`) does send a real `VOICE_DM` message through the full server pipeline (extraction, providers, planner, actions, evaluators all run, and the `core.voice_turn_signal` gate honors a client-built signal) — so for that one path the product owner's "extract everything, run actions, reply" requirement is largely met. But the **shouldReply/shouldRespond decision is split across three uncoordinated layers** (a client heuristic, a client `voiceTurnSignal`, and a server gate that can only *suppress*, never decide), the **diarization/speaker-attribution pipeline runs entirely separately from ASR and never carries a transcript** (so voice recognition can identify *who* spoke but the name/entity extraction it feeds gets `text: ""`), the **native in-process voice-session engine (`engine-bridge.runVoiceTurn`) is not wired to any production runtime**, the **desktop Electrobun adapter sends voice as a plain DM** (drops `VOICE_DM` + the turn signal entirely), and there is **no multi-agent / multi-speaker voice-room story** beyond the generic `addressedTo` edge writer.

## Current state

### The message pipeline and where shouldRespond lives (server)
- The canonical Stage‑1 decision is `shouldRespond` → normalized to `processMessage: "RESPOND" | "IGNORE" | "STOP"` in `packages/core/src/runtime/message-handler.ts` (`parseMessageHandlerOutput`, `normalizeMessageHandlerAction`). Routing to simple-reply vs planner is `routeMessageHandlerOutput` in the same file. This is the real `shouldReply` authority.
- Extraction is part of Stage 1: `parseExtract` pulls `facts`, `relationships`, and `addressedTo` out of the same `HANDLE_RESPONSE` envelope (`message-handler.ts:149`). These are persisted **channel-independently** in `packages/core/src/services/message.ts:5916-5957` via `runFactsAndRelationshipsStage` (`packages/core/src/runtime/facts-and-relationships.ts`) and `applyAddressedTo` (`packages/core/src/runtime/addressed-to.ts`). So a message that reaches `handleMessage` gets full extraction regardless of being voice or text — this is the good news.
- Voice is detected purely by channel: `isVoiceChannelMessage` checks `content.channelType === ChannelType.VOICE_DM || VOICE_GROUP` (`message.ts:1590`).
- The only voice-specific server logic is the `core.voice_turn_signal` response-handler evaluator (`message.ts:2768-2786`). It is **suppression-only**: it reads a pre-computed `voiceTurnSignal` blob (`getVoiceTurnSignalMetadata`, `message.ts:1605`) and forces `IGNORE` when `agentShouldSpeak === false || nextSpeaker === "user" || endOfTurnProbability < 0.4` (`voiceTurnSignalSuppressesAgent`, `message.ts:1647`). It **cannot positively decide to respond**, has **no awareness of the speaker's identity, wake-word, or end-of-turn on its own**, and is a no-op unless the client already populated the signal.

### shouldRespond for voice is actually decided on the client, in three places
1. `shouldRespondToVoiceTurn` (`packages/ui/src/voice/should-respond.ts`) — a conservative client heuristic that only drops pure disfluencies (`"um"`, `"uh"`) and near-verbatim TTS echo. Its own doc admits "The full semantic shouldRespond (wake-word / direct-address / the server `core.voice_turn_signal` evaluator) is a separate, heavier layer."
2. `buildVoiceTurnSignal` (`packages/ui/src/voice/voice-turn-signal.ts`) — composes the heuristic with semantic end-of-turn + optional diarization/wake-word, producing the `voiceTurnSignal` the server gate consumes.
3. `scoreEndOfTurn` / `TurnAggregator` (`packages/ui/src/voice/end-of-turn.ts`) — a pure-string heuristic EOT scorer (trailing conjunction/preposition/article → hold; sentence-final punctuation → commit).

These run in `useShellController` (`packages/ui/src/components/shell/useShellController.ts:339-368`): on turn commit it cheaply pre-filters with `shouldRespondToVoiceTurn`, builds the signal, then `send(turn, { channelType: "VOICE_DM", metadata: { voiceSource, voiceTurnSignal } })`. That goes through the normal `sendChatText` → `/api/conversations/:id/messages`, so this path **does** get the full pipeline + extraction + actions + reply. The server gate is positioned as "the single authority on whether to reply," but in practice it can only *veto*, and the heuristics that matter run client-side and duplicate the EOT logic (the `end-of-turn.ts` doc explicitly says it "mirrors the `HeuristicEotClassifier`" in plugin-local-inference — two copies of the same scorer).

### Diarization / speaker attribution runs as a SEPARATE pipeline with no transcript
- Live diarization is `LiveDiarizationSession` (`plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/live-diarization-session.ts`), fed by `POST /api/voice/audio-frames` (`plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/routes/live-diarization-route.ts`). It runs real ggml VAD + speaker encoder + diarizer (`build()`, lines 156-221) and emits `VOICE_TURN_OBSERVED`.
- The audio-frame consumer **explicitly does not transcribe**: "it does not transcribe (ASR text is the separate streaming-ASR path)" (`plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/audio-frame-consumer.ts:27`).
- The automatic attribution seam `handleLiveVoiceAttribution` (`plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/runtime/voice-entity-binding.ts:231`) emits `VOICE_TURN_OBSERVED` with **`text: ""`** (line 244). The merge-engine consumer in plugin-personal-assistant (`VoiceObserver.ingestTurn`, `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/entities/voice-observer.ts:83`) does real name/partner extraction — `extractSelfNameClaim` ("I'm Jill"), `extractPartnerClaim` ("Jill is my wife"), `EntityStore.observeIdentity`, `RelationshipStore.observe` — but it is fed empty text from the live path. **So voice recognition matching an existing entity works (imprint cluster → entityId via `VOICE_ENTITY_BOUND`, issue #8234), but "infer the name from what they said" cannot fire from the live audio-frame path because the transcript never reaches the observer.** It only works when something calls `emitVoiceTurnObserved` with real `text`, which the live diarization route does not do.

### The in-process native voice-session engine is not production-wired
- `VoiceEngineBridge.runVoiceTurn` (`plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/engine-bridge.ts`) is the full native loop (VAD → ASR → EOT → attribution → TTS) and supports a `liveAttribution` config that auto-emits `VOICE_TURN_OBSERVED` and folds diarization into `voiceTurnSignal`. The fused libelizainference composite EOT (ABI v11) is resolved here (`engine.ts:1248`). But its only non-test caller is `LocalInferenceEngine.runVoiceTurn` (`engine.ts:1559`), which itself has no production call site — the live surfaces use the HTTP routes (`/api/asr/local-inference`, `/api/voice/audio-frames`) instead. The EOT comment in `packages/ui/src/voice/end-of-turn.ts` confirms the heuristic "drives the **unwired** native voice-session engine."

### Desktop Electrobun adapter degrades voice to a plain DM
- `RuntimeHttpVoiceAdapter.sendRuntimeMessage` (`packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/src/voice/voice-runtime-adapter.ts:339`) creates a conversation and posts `{ text }` — **no `channelType: "VOICE_DM"`, no `voiceTurnSignal`**. So when the desktop voice service routes a transcript through this adapter, the server treats it as text: `core.voice_turn_signal` never fires, the reply is not flagged voice-originated, and TTS-back semantics are lost. The whole adapter is also feature-flag-gated behind `ELIZA_VOICE_LIVE_*` env vars and `playAudio` throws "not wired yet" (line 332).

### Multi-agent / multi-speaker room
- `VOICE_GROUP` is referenced exactly once (`message.ts:1593`) — only to classify the channel. There is no group-voice shouldRespond policy (turn-taking among multiple agents, "is this addressed to me", barge-in arbitration).
- The only multi-party primitive is `applyAddressedTo` (`addressed-to.ts`), which writes "addressed" relationship edges from speaker → resolved participant. It does not influence the agent's own decision to speak, and in a multi-*agent* room there is no mechanism for one agent to defer to another.

## Proposed work

1. **Make the server the single shouldRespond authority for voice, and let it positively decide — not just veto.** Promote the turn-taking decision (`endOfTurnProbability`, `nextSpeaker`, `agentShouldSpeak`, wake-word, speaker standing) into a first-class server-side input to `parseMessageHandlerOutput` / the response-handler evaluators in `packages/core/src/services/message.ts`, so `core.voice_turn_signal` can both suppress AND confirm. The client should send raw signals (EOT score, speaker attribution, wake-word) as structured metadata; the *decision* belongs server-side next to `shouldRespond`.

2. **Join the ASR transcript and the diarization attribution into one turn before it hits the pipeline.** Today `/api/asr/local-inference` (transcript) and `/api/voice/audio-frames` (speaker) are independent. Define a single voice-turn ingestion that carries `{ transcript, speakerEntityId, confidence, isOwner, voiceTurnSignal, channelType: VOICE_DM }` and writes one `VOICE_DM` message. Pass the real transcript into `emitVoiceTurnObserved` (fix `text: ""` at `voice-entity-binding.ts:244`) so name/partner extraction (`VoiceObserver.ingestTurn`) actually runs from live audio.

3. **Stamp speaker identity onto the voice message** so providers/extraction know who spoke. The resolved `entityId` from attribution should become the message's `entityId` (or a `content.metadata.speakerEntityId`) so `applyAddressedTo`, facts/relationships, and the planner attribute the turn to the right person, and so `RelationshipStore` edges form correctly in multi-speaker rooms.

4. **De-duplicate the EOT/shouldRespond logic.** There are two heuristic EOT scorers (`packages/ui/src/voice/end-of-turn.ts` and `plugins/plugin-local-inference/.../eot-classifier.ts`) and the client `should-respond.ts`. Pick one canonical implementation (prefer the fused composite EOT ABI v11 when present, falling back to the heuristic) and have both surfaces consume it.

5. **Wire (or explicitly retire) the native voice-session engine.** Either connect `engine-bridge.runVoiceTurn` (`engine.ts:1559`) to a production runtime path so the in-process VAD→ASR→EOT→attribution→TTS loop with `liveAttribution` is the real on-device voice path, or delete the unwired engine and consolidate on the HTTP-route path. Pick one; do not keep a dead second path.

6. **Fix the desktop Electrobun adapter to preserve voice semantics.** `RuntimeHttpVoiceAdapter.sendRuntimeMessage` (`voice-runtime-adapter.ts:339`) must send `channelType: "VOICE_DM"` and the `voiceTurnSignal`/speaker metadata, matching `useShellController`. Otherwise desktop voice silently bypasses the entire voice gate.

7. **Add a multi-agent / multi-speaker voice-room policy.** Define group-voice turn-taking: who responds when multiple agents share a `VOICE_GROUP` room, how `addressedTo` (and the new speaker identity) gates an individual agent's `shouldRespond`, and barge-in/defer arbitration. This is greenfield — `VOICE_GROUP` is classified but otherwise unhandled.

8. **End-to-end tests for the joined path.** A test that drives audio → transcript+speaker → `VOICE_DM` → full pipeline → extraction persisted (facts/relationship/name inferred) → action run → spoken reply, plus a multi-speaker test asserting only the addressed agent replies.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A voice turn (web/desktop/native) reaches the agent as a single `VOICE_DM` message carrying transcript, resolved `speakerEntityId`, and a structured turn signal — produced by one ingestion path, not two parallel ones.
- [ ] The server (`core.voice_turn_signal` + Stage‑1 `shouldRespond`) is the authority that decides respond/ignore for voice; the client sends raw signals, not the final decision. The gate can confirm a reply, not only suppress.
- [ ] Name inference from speech works from the **live** path: speaking "I'm Jill" / "Jill is my wife" creates/updates the entity + relationship (verified via `VoiceObserver`), because `VOICE_TURN_OBSERVED` now carries real `text` (no more `text: ""`).
- [ ] Voice recognition matching an existing enrolled entity continues to work (imprint → `entityId`), and that `entityId` is attached to the voice message.
- [ ] Voice messages run the identical extraction + providers + actions + evaluators as text (regression-proven, not assumed).
- [ ] The two heuristic EOT scorers are collapsed to one canonical implementation; the fused composite EOT (ABI v11) is preferred when available.
- [ ] The native voice-session engine (`engine-bridge.runVoiceTurn`) is either wired to a production runtime or removed — no dead second voice path remains.
- [ ] The Electrobun desktop adapter sends `VOICE_DM` + turn signal (no plain-DM degradation).
- [ ] A `VOICE_GROUP` policy decides which agent responds in a multi-agent/multi-speaker room; an e2e test proves only the addressed agent replies.

## Files & areas likely involved

- `packages/core/src/runtime/message-handler.ts` — `shouldRespond` parse/route + `parseExtract`
- `packages/core/src/services/message.ts` — `isVoiceChannelMessage`, `getVoiceTurnSignalMetadata`, `voiceTurnSignalSuppressesAgent`, the `core.voice_turn_signal` evaluator (`:2768`), extraction application (`:5916-5957`)
- `packages/core/src/runtime/addressed-to.ts`, `packages/core/src/runtime/facts-and-relationships.ts` — extraction persistence (multi-party edges)
- `packages/ui/src/voice/should-respond.ts`, `voice-turn-signal.ts`, `end-of-turn.ts` — client shouldRespond/EOT (dedup target)
- `packages/ui/src/components/shell/useShellController.ts` — the working ambient `VOICE_DM` send path (`:339-368`)
- `packages/ui/src/hooks/useVoiceChat.ts`, `packages/ui/src/voice/local-asr-transcribe.ts` — transcript client
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/routes/local-inference-asr-route.ts` — ASR transcript route
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/routes/live-diarization-route.ts`, `services/voice/live-diarization-session.ts`, `services/voice/audio-frame-consumer.ts` — diarization (no-transcript) path
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/runtime/voice-entity-binding.ts` — `emitVoiceTurnObserved` (the `text: ""` bug at `:244`), `handleLiveVoiceAttribution`
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/engine-bridge.ts`, `services/engine.ts` — unwired native voice-session engine + fused EOT
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/eot-classifier.ts` — second EOT scorer (dedup target)
- `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/entities/voice-observer.ts`, `voice-observer-bridge.ts`, `voice-attribution.ts` — merge engine, name/partner extraction
- `packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/src/voice/voice-runtime-adapter.ts` — desktop adapter dropping `VOICE_DM` (`:339`)
- `packages/core/src/types/events.ts` — `VOICE_TURN_OBSERVED` / `VOICE_ENTITY_BOUND` payload contracts

## Open questions

- **Where should the speaker→transcript join happen** — in a new app-core ingestion route, in the native `engine-bridge` (if we wire it), or by having the diarization route also call ASR? This determines whether we keep the HTTP-route architecture or revive the in-process engine.
- **Is the native voice-session engine (`runVoiceTurn`) meant to be the on-device production path, or legacy?** If it stays unwired we should delete it; the answer drives proposal item 5.
- **Multi-agent room semantics:** when multiple Eliza agents share a `VOICE_GROUP`, is turn-taking deterministic (e.g. owner-addressed agent only), round-robin, or model-decided per agent? Needs a product decision before building item 7.
- **Wake-word vs always-on default per surface:** desktop ambient mode opens the mic continuously; should group/multi-speaker rooms require a wake word by default to avoid cross-talk storms?
- **Privacy/consent for bystander voices:** confident-bystander suppression exists (`BYSTANDER_SUPPRESS_CONFIDENCE = 0.7`), but should unenrolled voices be transcribed/extracted at all, or dropped pre-pipeline?
35:T4623,## Summary

elizaOS already ships a deep, production-grade voice pipeline (VAD, streaming ASR, EOT classifier, barge-in, diarization, speaker imprint/profiles, Kokoro/OmniVoice TTS) plus a sprawling but **fragmented** set of voice test harnesses — a pure scoring library, a two-agent `voice:duet` harness, native benchmark `.mjs` scripts, Python benchmark suites, and a single-turn headful self-test screen. None of these are unified, and the headful surface only covers a **single-speaker, single-turn STT→agent→TTS** round-trip. The product-owner scenarios (multi-voice, pauses, respond/don't-respond, capture-and-simulate, multi-speaker, entity extraction, voice→entity recognition, diarization accuracy, EOT latency, transcription-mode entry/exit, multi-agent rooms) have **no headful coverage and no scenario-runner home**. This issue proposes a single **Voice Workbench** that unifies headless API tests + headful real-frontend tests + a benchmark layer (EOT latency, false-trigger rate, diarization accuracy, WER) over a shared corpus of TTS-generated scenario audio.

## Current state

### What exists and is genuinely strong

**The voice pipeline itself is mature** (`plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/`, ~90 source files):
- EOT detection: `eot-classifier.ts` (`CompositeEotClassifier`), `eliza1-eot-scorer.ts`, `fused-eot-scorer.ts`, `eot-classifier-ggml.ts`; `composite-eot-classifier.test.ts` exercises the heuristic/model blend. Backend selected via `ELIZA_VOICE_EOT_BACKEND`.
- VAD: `vad.ts`, `vad-ggml.ts`, with `vad.test.ts`, `vad.v1-v4.test.ts`.
- Diarization + speaker ID: `live-diarization-session.ts`, `speaker-imprint.ts`, `profile-store.ts`, `voice-emotion-classifier.ts`, plus `speaker/` and `streaming-asr/` subdirs.
- Barge-in / pause handling: `barge-in.ts`, `voice-state-machine.ts` (explicit IDLE→LISTENING→PAUSE_TENTATIVE→SPEAKING state machine with C1 checkpoint rollback), `optimistic-policy.ts`, `optimistic-rollback.ts`, `partial-stabilizer.ts`.
- Entity binding: `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/runtime/voice-entity-binding.ts` emits `VOICE_TURN_OBSERVED` / consumes `VOICE_ENTITY_BOUND`; `IDENTIFY_SPEAKER` action (`src/actions/identify-speaker.ts`) names a recent unidentified voice. Routes in `src/routes/voice-speaker-profile-routes.ts` bind/unbind a voice centroid to an elizaOS entity.

**Headless scoring library** — `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/e2e-harness.ts` is the one genuinely shared piece: pure functions `wordErrorRate`, `scoreTtsAsrRoundTrip`, `scoreBargeInInterruption`, `scorePauseContinuation`, `scoreOptimisticRollbackRestart`, `scoreFirstResponseLatency`, `summarizeVoiceE2e` + `assertRequiredVoiceArtifacts`. It is **pure** (no model loading) — hardware scripts feed it measurements (`packages/app-core/scripts/voice-e2e-hardware.ts`). Tested by `e2e-harness.test.ts`.

**Two-agent duet harness** — `packages/app-core/scripts/voice-duet.mjs` (`bun run voice:duet`) wires two `LocalInferenceEngine` instances A↔B over an in-memory `DuetAudioBridge` (`packages/app-core/scripts/lib/duet-bridge.mjs`), records `ttftFromUtteranceEndMs` etc. Shape-tested headlessly in `voice-duet.test.ts` with fake backends; real-model run gated in `voice-duet.e2e.test.ts`.

**Synthetic speech generation already exists** — `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/__test-helpers__/synthetic-speech.ts` produces formant-resonator speech-like PCM that Silero VAD reads as speech (no recorded WAV needed). `packages/benchmarks/voice/three-voice-scenario.mjs` already builds a 7-turn, 3-voice (alice/bob/eliza) diarization scenario with should-respond logic ("turns containing 'Eliza' should respond; turn 5 Bob→Alice should NOT").

**Native + Python benchmark suites** — `packages/benchmarks/voice/` (diarizer, speaker-encoder, enrollment, owner-voice, real-diarization, three-voice e2e), `voicebench/` (TS latency runner: transcription, response-decision, TTFT, TTS, e2e p95/p99), `voiceagentbench/` (Python: voice-in→tool-call, 5,757 queries, multi-turn coherence, safety/refusal, multilingual), `voicebench-quality/`, `voice-emotion/`, `voice-speaker-validation/`.

**Headful (Playwright)** — `packages/ui/src/voice/voice-selftest/voice-selftest-harness.ts` (`runVoiceSelfTest`) drives the REAL production client functions (`transcribeLocalInferenceWav`, `ElizaClient.sendConversationMessageStream`, real TTS fetch + `decodeAudioData` + playback graph) and emits a machine-readable per-stage report with a DOM mirror (`data-testid="voice-selftest-overall"`). Reached via `?shellMode=voice-selftest` (`packages/ui/src/App.tsx:86,358`). Driven by:
  - `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/voice-selftest-e2e.spec.ts` (mocked backends, `wav-direct`)
  - `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/voice-realaudio.spec.ts` (real `getUserMedia` capture via Chromium `--use-file-for-fake-audio-capture`, mocked backends)
  - `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/voice-desktop-selftest.spec.ts`
  - `packages/app/test/android/voice-selftest.android.spec.ts` (REAL on-device round-trip on a Pixel 9a)

### What is weak, missing, or broken

1. **No unified workbench. Five disjoint harness families** (pure scoring lib, `voice:duet` mjs, native benchmark `.mjs`, Python benches, headful self-test) with no common scenario format, no shared corpus, no single entrypoint, and divergent metric definitions (e.g. WER is implemented **twice** — `e2e-harness.ts:wordErrorRate` and `voice-selftest-harness.ts:wordErrorRate`; latency thresholds are scattered as inline `?? 1500` / `?? 250` defaults).

2. **Headful coverage is single-speaker, single-turn only.** `runVoiceSelfTest` has exactly three stages (ASR→SEND→TTS) for one phrase. There is **no** headful test for: multiple voices, pauses mid-utterance, respond-vs-not-respond, multi-speaker turn-taking, diarization in the UI, entity extraction/inference from conversation, voice-recognition matching an existing entity, EOT "jump in / don't jump in", transcription-mode entry/exit, or multi-agent room participation. The rich `voice-state-machine.ts` / `live-diarization-session.ts` logic is **never exercised through the real frontend**.

3. **scenario-runner cannot host voice scenarios.** `packages/scenario-runner` boots a real `AgentRuntime` over PGLite and runs scripted turns, but the turn model (`ScenarioTurnExecution`, `TurnReport.kind`) is text/http/connector only — there is **no audio/voice turn kind**, no PCM/WAV input, no diarization/EOT/speaker assertions. Grep of `packages/scenario-runner/test/scenarios/` for `voice|audio|asr|tts|diariz` returns zero voice scenarios. This is the natural home for "real voice-generated scenarios" and it is empty.

4. **"Capture user voice to simulate" ('talk about X for a few minutes') has no harness.** There is no tool to TTS-synthesize a long, multi-paragraph monologue (with pauses) from a prompt and feed it as a single user turn to test long-form ASR stability, EOT suppression across pauses, and partial-stabilizer behavior.

5. **Metrics are computed but never aggregated into benchmark verdicts that gate.** EOT latency, false-trigger rate (respond-when-shouldn't), diarization accuracy (DER), and WER each have *some* code path, but there is no single report that runs the matrix and produces pass/fail with regression baselines. `voicebench` reports latency; `e2e-harness.ts` scores latency; nothing rolls EOT false-trigger-rate or diarization DER into a gating summary.

6. **Backends are mocked in every CI-runnable headful test.** `voice-selftest-e2e.spec.ts` / `voice-realaudio.spec.ts` mock ASR/agent/TTS routes; real-backend coverage exists only on physical Android (`voice-selftest.android.spec.ts`) and gated hardware scripts. There is no documented "headful + real local Eliza-1 backend on a dev host" lane that the workbench could standardize.

7. **No shared, versioned audio corpus.** Fixtures are ad hoc: a hand-rolled `tinyWav()` 220 Hz sine inlined in each Playwright spec, `packages/app/test-results/.voice/known-phrase.wav`, `packages/benchmarks/voicebench/shared/generated/*.wav`, and on-the-fly synthesis. No labeled multi-speaker corpus with ground-truth diarization/EOT/transcript labels for accuracy benchmarking.

## Proposed work

Build a **Voice Workbench** package (proposed `packages/voice-workbench/`, or a `voice/` subtree inside `packages/scenario-runner`) that unifies the three execution modes over one scenario format and one corpus. Ordered scope:

1. **Define a shared voice-scenario schema and corpus.**
   - A `VoiceScenario` type: ordered `VoiceTurn[]` where each turn carries `{ speaker, text|audioRef, ttsVoiceId, pausesMs[], expectRespond: boolean, expectedTranscript?, expectedSpeakerLabel?, expectedEntity? }`, plus scenario-level `participants` (named voices/entities) and `assertions` (diarization labels, EOT decisions, WER ceilings, latency budgets).
   - A **corpus generator** that TTS-synthesizes each turn from text using the existing TTS routes + `samantha-preset` / Kokoro voices, splices in pauses, and mixes multi-speaker streams — extend `packages/benchmarks/voice/three-voice-scenario.mjs` and reuse `__test-helpers__/synthetic-speech.ts`. Persist labeled WAV + ground-truth JSON under a versioned `corpus/` dir so accuracy metrics are reproducible.
   - Add the long-form "talk about X for a few minutes" generator (synthesize a multi-paragraph monologue with natural pauses) as a corpus recipe.

2. **Consolidate the metric layer.** Promote `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice/e2e-harness.ts` into the single source of truth for voice scoring (it is already pure). Delete the duplicate `wordErrorRate` in `voice-selftest-harness.ts` and import the shared one. Add the missing scorers: `scoreEotDecision` (EOT latency + false-trigger/false-suppression rate over a labeled stream), `scoreDiarization` (DER / speaker-confusion against ground-truth), `scoreRespondDecision` (respond-when-should vs respond-when-shouldn't), `scoreEntityExtraction` (inferred name/entity match), `scoreVoiceEntityMatch` (recognized voice → existing entity binding).

3. **Headless mode — API/runtime tests.** A workbench runner that, given a `VoiceScenario`, feeds the corpus audio through the real services *without* a browser:
   - ASR via `/api/asr/local-inference` (`local-inference-asr-route.ts`).
   - Diarization via `LiveDiarizationSession` / `/api/voice/audio-frames` (`live-diarization-route.ts`).
   - EOT via the selected `ELIZA_VOICE_EOT_BACKEND` classifier.
   - respond/don't-respond + multi-agent room via a real `AgentRuntime` (reuse scenario-runner's PGLite boot).
   - entity extraction/voice→entity via `VOICE_TURN_OBSERVED`/`VOICE_ENTITY_BOUND` + `IDENTIFY_SPEAKER`.
   - TTS round-trip via `/api/tts/local-inference`.
   This is where the **scenario-runner** gets an `audio`/`voice` turn kind so voice scenarios become first-class `.scenario.ts` files.

4. **Headful mode — real frontend over the APIs.** Extend `VoiceSelfTestShell` (`packages/ui/src/voice/voice-selftest/`) from a single-turn self-test into a **scenario player**: accept a `VoiceScenario` over `window.__voiceWorkbench(scenario)`, drive the real client pipeline (capture → ASR → SSE → TTS → playback) turn-by-turn including injected pauses (Chromium `--use-file-for-fake-audio-capture` per turn), transcription-mode entry/exit, and multi-speaker streams, and emit a per-turn machine-readable + DOM-mirrored verdict. Add Playwright specs under `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/` (`voice-workbench-*.spec.ts`) that run each scenario class headful: multi-voice, pauses, respond/no-respond, multi-speaker, diarization, entity-extraction, voice-recognition, EOT, transcription-mode, multi-agent-room.

5. **Benchmark/report layer.** A single `voice:workbench` entrypoint that runs the scenario matrix in both modes and rolls up: EOT latency (p50/p95) + false-trigger rate, diarization DER, transcription WER, first-audio/TTFT latency, respond-decision accuracy, entity-match accuracy — into one JSON + Markdown report with baselines, mirroring `voicebench`'s p95/p99 output and the existing `summarizeVoiceE2e` shape. Wire a CI lane (real local backend where provisioned; `skipped` — never `pass` — where artifacts absent, matching the existing self-test honesty contract).

6. **Document and dedupe.** One README that maps the consolidated workbench to the retired/absorbed scripts (`voice:duet`, `voice-e2e-hardware.ts`, `voice-vad-smoke.ts`, `three-voice-scenario.mjs`, the per-spec `tinyWav()`), so the five harness families converge on one.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A `VoiceScenario` schema + versioned labeled corpus exist, generated from text via real TTS, covering: multi-voice, pauses, respond/no-respond, multi-speaker, entity extraction, voice→entity match, diarization, EOT, transcription-mode, multi-agent room, and the long-form "talk about X" monologue.
- [ ] All voice scoring (WER, EOT latency/false-trigger, diarization DER, respond-decision, entity match, latency budgets) lives in one shared module (`e2e-harness.ts` promoted); the duplicate `wordErrorRate` in `voice-selftest-harness.ts` is removed.
- [ ] Headless mode runs every scenario class against the real services (ASR/diarization/EOT/respond/entity/TTS routes + `AgentRuntime`) and produces a pass/fail report; voice scenarios are loadable as scenario-runner `.scenario.ts` files via a new audio turn kind.
- [ ] Headful mode (`VoiceSelfTestShell` extended to a scenario player) runs every scenario class through the real frontend client pipeline with a per-turn machine-readable + DOM-mirrored verdict; new `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/voice-workbench-*.spec.ts` specs exist and pass.
- [ ] A single `voice:workbench` entrypoint emits one JSON + Markdown benchmark report (EOT latency + false-trigger rate, diarization DER, WER, TTFT/first-audio latency, respond accuracy, entity-match accuracy) with regression baselines.
- [ ] CI runs the workbench against mocked backends always and real local backend where provisioned, reporting `skipped` (never `pass`) when artifacts are absent.
- [ ] Multi-agent room voice scenario verifies who-jumps-in / who-stays-silent across ≥3 participants.
- [ ] README documents the consolidation and which legacy scripts the workbench absorbs.
